The contract was MEASURED, not inherited from 209 on the strength of the names matching (203 inherited 202's exactly; 205's differed from ntdll's on the one detail that mattered): probes/lcpa.c re-confirmed terminated-not-padded, n==0 writes nothing at all, n used UNSIGNED, NULL returns NULL, a swallowed fault — and the decisive one, that it READS THE SOURCE BEFORE TESTING THE BOUND (an 8-character unterminated source at a guard page returns the destination at n=8 and NULL at n=9). It also settled a question the wide form does not have: GetACP() is 1252 with ZERO DBCS lead bytes, so no DBCS-aware truncation rule is observable and the copy is byte-wise. THREE THINGS THE MEASUREMENTS FORCED: (1) the bound limits what may be WRITTEN, not what may be READ — reading all 32 in-page bytes cannot fault where Windows would not, which took 8 characters from 1.10x to 1.75x, 16 from 1.07x to 3.12x and the truncating class from 2.61x to 9.90x; (2) the clamped write is a pair of OVERLAPPING power-of-two stores, never a byte past k, because the destination is terminated and NOT padded; (3) the page arithmetic does not belong in the copy loop — hoisting it took 4000 characters from 85.59 ns to 47.35, and pairing two chunks per iteration behind a single vpminub NUL test took it to 34.60 (115.65 GB/s, the L1 store limit). The two short classes sit at the harness's own call-overhead floor: the n=0 no-op measures 2.72 ns, so 8 characters at 3.29 ns is ~0.6 ns of actual work |
| [212](changes/212-pathfindfilenamea/) | `PathFindFileNameA` (narrow last-component) | `shlwapi.dll!PathFindFileNameA` | **LANDED** — **26.82x** (up to **92.31x**). Twenty-two shlwapi exports here were converted and every one was a W; discovery/shlwapi_narrow.c timed the A siblings and they are not "the same cost on half the bytes" like 211 — they are 4.9x to 16.4x the WIDE cost, i.e. up to 32x per byte, the signature of an MBCS-aware per-character walk. FIRST QUESTION WAS NOT "IS IT SLOW" but whether the behaviour is byte-wise here: every byte 0x01..0xFF was tried in the position a lead byte would hide a separator, and ZERO of 255 act as one (GetACP()=1252). THE RULE WAS RE-DERIVED, AND THE OBVIOUS GUESS WAS WRONG: all 349525 strings over {a,backslash,slash,colon} of length 0..9 were enumerated against the live NARROW export — 0 mismatches against the rule 161 derived for the wide form, **76672** against the simpler rule that drops the colon's run condition (a colon separates only when it is the SOLE colon in its run, so ":a" gives 1 but ":a:" gives 0). All sixteen spot checks in probes/pffa.c agree with BOTH rules; only the enumeration separated them, so correctness.c and the live run are both EXHAUSTIVE rather than sampled. Method: one forward pass, per-32-byte "interesting positions" mask (backslash, slash, colon, NUL), uninteresting blocks skipped whole, first load aligned DOWN for page safety. A narrow block carries 32 positions to the wide form's 16 and each match sets ONE mask bit rather than a pair, so ours is also 1.8x faster than 161 in absolute terms |
| [213](changes/213-strrchra/) | `StrRChrA` (narrow reverse character search) | `shlwapi.dll!StrRChrA` | **LANDED** — **149.12x** (up to **210x**). The biggest ratio the narrow survey found: 14351.08 ns to search 4000 characters against 874.77 for StrRChrW over the same character count — 16x the WIDE cost for HALF the bytes, i.e. 32x per byte. ONE MEASUREMENT EXPLAINED THE WHOLE FUNCTION: an pszEnd placed past the terminator makes the live export NEVER RETURN (two inputs did it; the first cost a 300-second timeout to locate, because the probe printed its result AFTER each call and left no trace of which one hung — it prints the label first now). That pins the shipped loop as a FORWARD walk with CharNextA, which does not advance past a terminator and so spins when `end` is unreachable — and every other measurement falls out of it: pszEnd is EXCLUSIVE because the test is `p != end` before the body, searching for the terminator always returns NULL because a valid range never contains one, and the cost is a function call per character. CONTRACT DOMAIN: pszEnd NULL, or pszStart <= pszEnd <= pszStart+strlen; outside it we return an answer instead of hanging, documented as a deliberate divergence. THE DOMAIN IS NARROWER THAN THE WIDE FORM'S — change 134 verified StrRChrW searches the RAW range past embedded NULs, which the A form cannot because CharNextA is in its loop. Method: 134's two AVX2 paths ported to bytes (backward when bounded so it exits at the first match, forward when not), all loads 32-byte ALIGNED so page safety is structural. The end-masks do NOT belong in the loop — recomputing them per block made a 254-char bounded miss 10.02 ns, SLOWER than the unbounded path's 5.11 over the same string; hoisting them to the only two blocks that can be partial gave 4.50 ns |
| [214](changes/214-strcspna/) | `StrCSpnA` (narrow complement-span) | `shlwapi.dll!StrCSpnA` | **LANDED** — **137.08x** (up to **424x**). The narrow SPAN family all carry big ratios against the wide siblings this project already converted (StrCSpnA 13.54x the wide cost, StrPBrkA 10.03x, StrSpnA 7.52x); StrCSpnA has the most available and went first. Probed: all three are BYTE-WISE (0 of 254 byte values act as a lead byte) and so is the SET string (0 of 252 cannot be a member), so a 256-bit membership test reproduces them exactly — and A NULL SET IS NOT THE EMPTY SET (StrCSpnA(s,NULL)=0 but StrCSpnA(s,"")=strlen), the one distinction a reimplementation is most likely to get wrong. THE OBSERVATION THAT MAKES IT CHEAP: the set string is NUL-terminated, so the set can never contain a NUL, so the subject's terminator is never a member — StrCSpnA is exactly "the index of the first position that is either a set member OR the terminator", one scan with no second stopping rule. Method: the set becomes a 256-bit bitmap built IN THE CALLER'S SHADOW SPACE (32 bytes, exactly the size of the bitmap, so nothing is pushed), tested 32 characters at a time by two-table vpshufb selected by the character's BIT 7 — the bitmap is indexed bit-per-byte-value rather than by nibble precisely so the BUILD is cheap and the test costs the same. Two experiments: reading the bitmap once and splitting halves with vperm2i128 to pay the store-forwarding stall only once measured WORSE (125.54 -> 120.19, lane-crossing costs more than the stall) and was reverted; dropping `bts` (microcoded with a register bit offset) for an explicit byte index plus a POW2 table took the set-13 class 17.82 -> 13.04 ns and the geomean 125.54 -> 137.08 |
| [215](changes/215-strpbrka/) | `StrPBrkA` (narrow set search) | `shlwapi.dll!StrPBrkA` | **LANDED** — **167.54x** (up to **633x**). Change 214's core with one different ending — same 256-bit set bitmap, same two-table vpshufb membership test, same page-safe aligned scan; 214 returns the INDEX of the first stop, this returns a POINTER to it or NULL when the stop was the terminator. Because a NUL can never be a set member (the set string is NUL-terminated), ONE TEST OF THE BYTE AT THE STOP separates the two outcomes. THE CONTRACT WAS STILL MEASURED SEPARATELY, and it is not uniform across the family: for this export a NULL set and an EMPTY set BOTH give NULL, where 214 gives 0 and strlen respectively — three functions sharing one core is exactly how a wrong rule carries across unnoticed |
| [216](changes/216-strspna/) | `StrSpnA` (narrow set span) | `shlwapi.dll!StrSpnA` | **LANDED** — **227.43x** (up to **994x**). THE SLOWEST SINGLE ROUTINE THE NARROW SURVEY MEASURED ANYWHERE: 166503 ns — 166 MICROSECONDS — to span 4000 characters, against 22141 for StrSpnW; an MBCS walk with a per-character search of the set is quadratic in the set size on top of everything else. It is 214's core with the membership test INVERTED, and the inversion pays twice: the set can never contain a NUL, so the terminator is a NON-member, so the inverted mask STOPS THERE ON ITS OWN — no terminator test anywhere in the loop, making the inner loop two instructions SHORTER than 214's despite computing the same thing. The `not` is also what keeps the aligned first load safe in this direction: bits shifted in at the top are 0, which inverts to "member" = "no stop", so the scan moves on and re-examines those bytes properly. Bench classes had to be rebuilt too — a set the subject is NOT inside stops at index 0 and measures nothing |
| [217](changes/217-pathfindextensiona/) | `PathFindExtensionA` (narrow extension finder) | `shlwapi.dll!PathFindExtensionA` | **LANDED** — **45.48x** (up to **100x**). THIS TARGET FOUND A BUG IN LANDED CODE. Probing it caught a missing rule in change 132, which had shipped and been passing its own "600k path fuzz" for weeks while disagreeing with the live PathFindExtensionW on **295513 of 2015539** enumerated strings: A SPACE stops the backward scan exactly as a backslash does, so "a.b " returns the terminator, not the dot. 132's fuzz alphabet contained no space, so its oracle, its implementation and its corpus were all wrong TOGETHER — a test that shares its blind spot with the thing it tests proves nothing. The sequence: probes/pfea.c enumerated {a,'.',backslash,'/',':'} exhaustively and got 0 mismatches (a clean-looking inheritance), then widened the alphabet by two characters and got 118587; probes/space.c showed A and W agree with EACH OTHER on all 87381 strings over {a,'.',backslash,space} but both disagree with 132 on 14311, so it was never an A/W asymmetry; probes/rule2.c verified the one-character amendment over 2015539 strings on each of two alphabets — 0 mismatches for both exports, 295513 for the old rule. Change 132 is CORRECTED in the same commit, its correctness test now ENUMERATES that alphabet rather than sampling it, and both halves are driven live together. It is 0x20 specifically and not whitespace: "a.b<TAB>" still yields the dot |
| [218](changes/218-strtrima/) | `StrTrimA` (narrow in-place trim) | `shlwapi.dll!StrTrimA` | **LANDED** — **127.76x** (up to **239x**). Reuses the 256-bit membership bitmap from 214-216; one forward pass finds BOTH ends, because the set can never contain a NUL so a single non-member mask per block gives the first and last kept characters at once. THE CONTRACT'S SUBTLE PART IS WHAT IT WRITES: only what it must, and the ORDER is observable — trimming both ends of "xxabcxx" leaves TWO terminators behind (a b c \0 c \0 x \0, not a b c \0 c x x \0) because it cuts the TRAILING end in place FIRST and only then moves the leading end down. An implementation that moved first and terminated once returns the same BOOL and leaves the same STRING on every input, so correctness.c and the live run compare the WHOLE BUFFER against a poison fill. THAT DISCIPLINE CAUGHT TWO REAL BUGS, both producing a plausible buffer and a correct return: (1) at source offset 27 ONLY, where the string spans a block boundary and its terminator lands as byte 0 of the next block, an empty block-local mask was read as "the whole string is trim characters" and emptied the buffer — "no non-member in this block" and "no non-member anywhere" are different questions; (2) the overlapping short-copy idiom borrowed from change 211 is WRONG here, because 211's source and destination are different buffers while these OVERLAP, so the second load re-read bytes the first store had already moved, turning "xxabc" into c\0c\0c\0. The tail is now read into a register before anything is written |
| [219](changes/219-pathstrippatha/) | `PathStripPathA` (narrow in-place strip) | `shlwapi.dll!PathStripPathA` | **LANDED** — **14.85x** (up to **39x**). Change 212's scan plus a move, and the equivalence was verified on BOTH halves rather than inherited from change 162: over 87381 strings in {a,backslash,slash,colon} and again over 488281 in the same alphabet PLUS A SPACE — 400900 of them containing one — the buffer left by the live PathStripPathA is byte for byte what you get by copying the live PathFindFileNameA answer to the front, 0 mismatches, and the same for PathStripPathW. THE SPACE WAS CHECKED DELIBERATELY: change 132 shipped a rule missing exactly that character and 140/143/144 inherited it, all corrected this session, and 162 is landed code whose own corpus had no space in it either. The move is forward with each block LOADED BEFORE IT IS STORED; the overlapping head/tail pair would be WRONG here, as change 218 proved the hard way, so the remainder walks down 16/8/4/2/1 — and unlike the wide form it must handle a final ODD byte. correctness.c and the live run compare the WHOLE BUFFER, since the export leaves the bytes past the new terminator untouched |
| [220](changes/220-strchra/) | `StrChrA` (narrow character search) | `shlwapi.dll!StrChrA` | **LANDED** — **11.42x** (up to **20.4x**). A DIFFERENT SHAPE FROM THE REST OF THE NARROW FAMILY, and the numbers said so before any code was written: StrRChrA cost 16.41x its wide sibling, StrCSpnA 13.54x, StrPBrkA 10.03x — all MBCS-walk signatures — while StrChrA costs 1.01x the wide cost for HALF the bytes, i.e. only twice the cost per byte. That is a plain byte loop, so the available ratio comes from vectorisation alone and this is not a 200x target. One forward pass, two masks per 32-byte block (target and terminator), first set bit wins; the first block's leading bits are CLEARED rather than shifted out so the block base can be the signed -(psz & 31) and every later block is just +32. ONE WRONG REGISTER COST A WHOLE RUN: StrChrA takes TWO arguments so wMatch is in dx, and the first cut borrowed change 213's layout where the needle is in r8w — the failure signature was clean, EVERY "present" case failing and EVERY "absent" case passing |
| [221](changes/221-pathremoveblanksa/) | `PathRemoveBlanksA` (narrow in-place blank strip) | `shlwapi.dll!PathRemoveBlanksA` | **LANDED** — **20.59x** (up to **68.9x**). The biggest ratio of the twelve narrow siblings still unconverted (discovery/shlwapi_narrow2.c): 158.64 ns against 19.86 for the wide form on the same character count, 7.99x for HALF the bytes. A BLANK IS 0x20 AND NOTHING ELSE — every byte value was tried at both ends and exactly one qualifies; a TAB IS NOT A BLANK. THE WRITE ORDER IS THE OPPOSITE OF CHANGE 218's: StrTrimA cuts the trailing end first and then moves, this one MOVES FIRST and cuts second, so "  abc  " leaves a,b,c,NUL,space,NUL,space,NUL where cutting first would leave a stale 'c' at index 4. Two siblings doing the same job in opposite orders is exactly what gets assumed instead of measured, and only a whole-buffer comparison can see it — this function returns NOTHING. THE BYTE-WISE SCREEN WAS STRENGTHENED FIRST: StrStrA had just been abandoned after passing the usual "byte in front" test while conflating two values INSIDE a candidate, so this probe varies every byte value where the function actually looks (0 of 254 misbehave). One forward pass gives first non-blank, last non-blank and the length at once; no membership bitmap is needed for a one-byte set |
| [222](changes/222-pathremoveextensiona/) | `PathRemoveExtensionA` (narrow extension trim) | `shlwapi.dll!PathRemoveExtensionA` | **LANDED** — **16.94x** (up to **48.1x**). Change 217's scan plus a length check and a single-byte store. TWO INHERITED FACTS, BOTH RE-MEASURED: (1) the SPACE rule — this function's rule was wrong in three landed siblings until earlier in the same session, and the narrow REMOVE agrees with the CORRECTED rule on all 335923 enumerated strings while disagreeing with the one change 140 shipped with on **46158** of them; (2) the MAX_PATH guard — probing every length from 250 to 268 shows 259 truncated and 260 LEFT COMPLETELY UNTOUCHED, the one rule this function has that its find-only sibling does not, so the guard is checked BEFORE any write. The export writes exactly ONE byte and clears nothing past it ("file.txt" becomes "file" with "txt" still in the buffer), so correctness and the live run compare the WHOLE BUFFER |
| [223](changes/223-pathundecoratea/) | `PathUndecorateA` (narrow `[n]` decoration removal) | `shlwapi.dll!PathUndecorateA` | **LANDED** — **27.05x** (up to **81.6x**). THIS CHANGE FOUND A BUG IN ITS OWN WIDE SIBLING. probes/undec.c enumerated the narrow export against change 174's four-conjunct rule over {a,'[',']','.',backslash,'0'} to length 7 and got 0 of 335923 mismatches — the point at which this would normally have ended. probes/space2.c ran it again with a SPACE in the alphabet, because a missing space rule had already made 132, 140, 143 and 144 wrong this session, and got **2724** mismatches for the narrow export **and the same 2724 for the WIDE one**. Change 174 had been wrong since it landed; putting the question structurally (discovery/extension_space_audit2.c) found 158, 159 and 160 too. Eight landed changes, one missing stopper. Smallest case: ". []" → ". ", left alone. THE ASYMMETRY IS THE HARD PART: the backslash was doing two jobs — bounding the extension search for conjunct (b), where a SPACE bounds it too, and delimiting the component for conjunct (d), where a space does NOT ("a b[1].txt" → "a b.txt") — so the scan tracks TWO positions and a corpus carrying only one delimiter cannot tell the jobs apart, which is why correctness and the live run each enumerate TWO alphabets. The byte-wise screen is the STRONGER form adopted after StrStrA: all 255 non-NUL byte values at SIX structural positions, 0 disagreements, and the stopper set re-derived from scratch rather than inherited (GetCPInfo reports 0 DBCS lead bytes for ACP 1252 — measured, not assumed). ONE forward pass gives length, last backslash, last backslash-or-space and last dot from four vpcmpeqb per block, with the common block — none of those four characters — answered by a single vpor + vpmovmskb. The tail move is a descending 16/8/4/2/1 ladder, not a byte loop: decorations sit near the end of a name, so the real workload moves five or six bytes and NOTHING goes through the 32-byte loop — the byte loop put a ~2.5 ns floor under every class and made 16 characters measure SLOWER than 64 |
| [224](changes/224-pathrenameextensiona/) | `PathRenameExtensionA` (narrow extension replace) | `shlwapi.dll!PathRenameExtensionA` | **LANDED** — **23.55x** (up to **46.9x**). The largest absolute gap left among the narrow siblings: 188.14 ns vs 45.20 for the wide form on the same character count, and 764 ns on the bench's 254-char case. NOTHING WAS INHERITED FROM THE WIDE SIBLING, because that sibling (change 158) SHIPPED WRONG — its extension position was change 132's rule without the SPACE stopper, wrong on 46158 of 335923 strings until corrected this session. probes/ren.c re-derived every rule against the narrow export and it matches the CORRECTED rule on all 335923: '/' and ':' do NOT stop the backward scan, a TAB does NOT (the stopper is 0x20 specifically), a SPACE DOES. THE MAX_PATH LIMIT BOUNDS THE RESULT, NOT THE INPUT — established by a TWO-DIMENSIONAL sweep (input length 240..275 x extension length 1..6): the first FALSE moves with the extension length while the last successful RESULT length is 259 in all six sweeps, so an input-length sweep alone would have validated an implementation bounding the wrong quantity. On refusal the buffer is COMPLETELY untouched, so the BOOL and the whole buffer are both checked. AND IT VALIDATES NOTHING, unlike its own PathCch siblings 159/160, which reject a space, a backslash and a non-leading dot — all 255 byte values inside the extension are copied verbatim, including "obj" with no leading dot at all. One forward pass gives length, last backslash-or-space and last dot from three vpcmpeqb per block; NO component position is tracked (unlike 223), because this function never asks which component anything is in, so the two stoppers fold into one mask and stay folded — which is why the space case costs nothing measurable (16.29 vs 16.49 ns). The extension is MEASURED BEFORE ANYTHING IS WRITTEN, since a refusal must not touch the buffer, then copied by a 16/8/4/2/1 ladder |
| [225](changes/225-lstrlena/) | `lstrlenA` — **21.7 → 158.7 GB/s** | `kernelbase.dll!lstrlenA` | **LANDED** — **3.01x** geomean (up to **7.18x**). The NARROW half of lstrlen is FOUR TIMES SLOWER PER BYTE than the wide one on the same subject (183.89 ns for 4000 bytes vs 94.39 for 4000 wchars) — not an MBCS tax, but the signature of a 16-byte SSE2 loop against the wide form's 32-byte AVX2 one. Ours overtakes the WIDE export's 84.8 GB/s, so the narrow half stops being the slow one. PAGE SAFETY IS THE ENTIRE DESIGN, because a length function is the easiest thing here to validate wrongly: every heap string has slack behind it, so an implementation that reads one block too far returns the right answer on every ordinary corpus — and when it finally does over-read it does NOT crash, because the export SWALLOWS the fault and returns 0 (measured: 80 of 80 unterminated tails returned, 0 faulted). The bug would present as a correct length of 3 silently becoming 0. So the safety is structural: the first block is aligned DOWN to 32 (a 32-aligned load never crosses a page, and the bits before the string are shifted out of the mask); every later block is free (no terminator in a block means the next byte is mapped); and the paired loop is 64-BYTE ALIGNED, which is precisely its licence to read 64 bytes at once since 4096 is a multiple of 64. Correctness sweeps NOACCESS guard pages THREE ways — terminated (answer must be the length), unterminated (answer must be 0 without crashing), and the string starting exactly at a page boundary with the PRECEDING page unreadable, which is what align-down has to survive. The __except also issues vzeroupper: unwinding out of assembly skips the core's own, and leaving dirty upper YMM state plants an AVX-SSE transition penalty in the CALLER's code. REJECTED: aligning the first window to 64 (removing the realignment peel) is slower at EVERY size — and the first A/B said the opposite, because classes below 64 bytes sit at 2.5-3.5 ns where a single run swings a full nanosecond; on one unchanged binary the geomean ranges 2.72-3.10 run to run, so everything here is min-of-five |
| [226](changes/226-pathremoveargsa/) | `PathRemoveArgsA` (cut arguments off a path) | `shlwapi.dll!PathRemoveArgsA` | **LANDED** — **34.73x** (up to **71.9x**). THE QUOTE PARITY IS A CARRY-LESS MULTIPLY, NOT A STATE MACHINE. The rule is not "cut at the first space": it is the first 0x20 OUTSIDE double quotes, each '"' toggling the state — which looks inherently sequential, because whether a space splits depends on the parity of every quote before it. It is not. The parity-of-all-preceding-bits of a bitmask is clmul by all-ones (bit k of clmul(q,~0) is the XOR of q over [k-63,k], i.e. the inclusive prefix XOR); shift left by one for the EXCLUSIVE prefix, since a quote toggles what FOLLOWS it, XOR in the carry from earlier blocks, and a whole 32-byte block resolves at once with one popcnt for the next carry. Same trick JSON parsers use for string boundaries. TWO OF THE THREE BEHAVIOURS ARE INVISIBLE TO A STRING COMPARISON, so every test compares the WHOLE BUFFER against a poison fill: "ab   c" writes TWO terminators, at 2 AND at 4 (not 2 and 3) — the second lands PAST the first; and when there is nothing to do the function writes NOTHING AT ALL, not even a redundant terminator over the existing one. The third behaviour IGNORES QUOTING entirely: with no unquoted space it trims trailing spaces, so '"'+' ' IS cut even though that space sits inside an unclosed quote, while '"'+' '+'a' is not. All re-derived against the NARROW export: exhaustive {a,SPACE,QUOTE,TAB} to len 9 (349523 strings, 0 mismatches), every byte value at seven positions (0 of 255 disagree at each), and sweeping all 255 values shows EXACTLY ONE byte splits and EXACTLY ONE is trimmed — 0x20 both times, a TAB neither. No MAX_PATH guard. Correctness drives the quote and the space into DIFFERENT 32-byte blocks at every offset, because the parity carry is where an off-by-one would hide, plus dense quote masks the carry-less prefix would never otherwise see |
| [227](changes/227-lstrcpya/) | `lstrcpyA` — **2.5 → 90.2 GB/s** | `kernelbase.dll!lstrcpyA` | **LANDED** — **11.48x** geomean (up to **35.3x**). discovery/kernelbase_str.c surveyed the rest of the lstr* family the way 225 found lstrlenA: lstrcpyA moves **2.50 bytes/ns** — a BYTE LOOP — against lstrcpyW's 10.01 and memcpy's 318.47. probes/cpya.c proves the byte granularity twice over: a destination overrunning a guard page is filled to an ARBITRARY boundary (80 of 80 rooms, exactly to the last writable byte), and overlapping arguments smear with a PERIOD OF TWO (a 16-byte chunked copy would smear with a period of sixteen). SO THE COPY MUST PAGE-CLAMP BOTH POINTERS, not just the source. lstrcpyA has NO bound, so it always runs off the end of a destination too small for the source, and the export returns NULL rather than faulting while filling that destination to its LAST WRITABLE BYTE. Clamping only the source passes every ordinary corpus AND returns the right NULL, and still leaves a different number of bytes in the caller's buffer — nothing crashes, only the bytes differ. Each chunk is clamped to min(bytes left in the source's page, bytes left in the destination's page), so a chunk can never fault halfway and the fault lands on the first byte of the next page with everything before it written, exactly where the shipped byte loop stops. THE CLAMP IS HOISTED out of the 64-byte loop — it only changes at a page boundary, once per 4096 bytes, so recomputing it per chunk charged six instructions per 64 bytes to re-answer an unchanged question: 4000 bytes 54.33 → 45.19 ns, geomean 9.97x → 11.63x, every class improved. Correctness sweeps the source guard, the destination guard AND both at once; 200k fuzz; 14x14 source/destination alignments. Does NOT reach memcpy's 318 GB/s and cannot — memcpy is told the length, this has to find the terminator as it goes and may not read or write past it |
| [228](changes/228-lstrcata/) | `lstrcatA` (append) | `kernelbase.dll!lstrcatA` | **LANDED** — bit-exact, **4.15–4.52x** geomean over twelve consecutive runs, up to **26.15x** appending 64 bytes onto 4000 (the accidental-quadratic shape lstrcat is usually used in), worst class **1.15x**. Contract re-measured rather than inherited from 227 even though the copy half is identical: THREE failing pointers, not two, because lstrcat READS the destination before writing it (an unterminated destination returns NULL, 80 of 80). And NO EARLY EXIT ON AN EMPTY SOURCE — appending "" leaves the buffer byte-identical, but writing a 0 over a 0 is indistinguishable from not writing, and a PAGE_READONLY destination settles it: lstrcatA(readonly, "") returns NULL, so the store really happens. UNPARKED IN TWO STAGES. First the benchmark: its one-store restore landed on the buffer the next call was about to load 32 bytes from, and a wide load cannot forward from a just-retired narrow store — it waits for it to drain, while the shipped byte loop forwards cheaply — so the restore was charged to us; rotating the destination took "8 onto empty" 0.54x → 1.05x with no change to the asm. That left the row a COIN FLIP against the gate (0.88x–1.04x over ten runs, failing about one run in three), which is not something to land on a favourable run, so the short path was rewritten: an EMPTY DESTINATION answered by one byte load and one branch, with the page clamp computed UNDER that load (the ORDER is the gain — clamp-first delayed the only long-latency op and cost 0.2 ns on every short row); the copy LEADING WITH A SINGLE 32-BYTE CHUNK before the 64-byte pairs, which also stopped the pair loop recomputing the clamp every 32 bytes and took "4000 onto empty" 1.54x → 2.92x; and the 1..32-byte tail as TWO OVERLAPPING MOVES instead of a 16/8/4/2/1 ladder, removing four conditional branches from the shortest call. That ladder was BIMODAL, not merely slow — the same executable alternated run to run between 4.24 and 4.90 ns while the live export sat at 4.69–4.71 every time — and collapsing it removed the slow mode as well as the average. REJECTED, twice measured: overlapping the two independent scans (a long destination makes the speculative source mask stale — 0.45 ns bought at 8 bytes, 9 ns lost at "64 onto 4000"), and hoisting the page clamp ABOVE the destination load rather than under it |
| [229](changes/229-lstrcpyw/) | `lstrcpyW` — **10 → 131 GB/s** | `kernelbase.dll!lstrcpyW` | **LANDED** — **3.43x** geomean (up to **12.8x**). The wide half of lstrcpy is a 16-byte SSE2 loop at 10.01 bytes/ns. Unlike the PARKED lstrcatA (228), it is ALSO expensive at short lengths — 16.51 ns for 64 characters — so the shortest class TIES at 1.02x rather than regressing, which was the prediction made before the benchmark was written. THE SPLIT CHARACTER is the rule that shapes this one, and the narrow sibling could not even ask about it: when a destination has an ODD number of writable bytes the last character cannot be stored whole. Measured at every odd width 1..11 — 1 byte writable modifies 0, 3 modifies 2, 5 modifies 4 — the export writes WHOLE CHARACTERS ONLY and never leaves half of one. So the page clamp is computed in bytes and ROUNDED DOWN TO EVEN (`and r9d, -2`), one instruction that is the only thing making an odd-aligned destination behave. Without it an implementation passes every ordinary corpus, returns the right NULL, and leaves ONE EXTRA BYTE in the caller's buffer — nothing crashes, no return differs — so correctness AND the live run sweep every destination width from 1 to 201 BYTES, odd and even, and half the benchmark's destinations are odd-aligned so the clamp is on the measured path too. Otherwise 227's design in 16-bit elements: chunks clamped to min(source page remaining, destination page remaining), vpminuw folding each 64-byte window, the clamp hoisted out of the loop, and a 16/8/4/2 tail ladder with NO byte step because the count is always even by construction. All 65535 non-zero code units verified, surrogates included **A TGL VARIANT NOW EXISTS** -- [`impl_tgl.asm`](changes/229-lstrcpyw/impl_tgl.asm). On bench #3 the parent PARKS (`4 chars` 0.81x-0.90x, `8 chars` 0.95x-0.97x) and the variant LANDS, in FIVE INTERLEAVED A/B PASSES -- alternating builds in one session, because the machine had been benchmarking for hours and a sequential comparison would not have been trustworthy: parent 2.995x-3.120x PARKED 5/5, variant **3.199x-3.318x LANDS 5/5**, `4 chars` -> 1.20x-1.34x, `8 chars` -> 1.50x-1.58x, no row WORSE. Correctness PASS every run on the parent's own gate, split-character guard-page sweep included. **WHAT THE PARENT PAYS FOR FOUR CHARACTERS IS EVERYTHING IT DOES BEFORE LOOKING AT ONE**: cp_loop computes a two-page clamp (two ANDs, two subtracts, a CMOV, a round-down) and cp_done pays a VZEROUPPER. The short path answers first with ONE test covering BOTH pointers -- OR can only set bits, so `(src|dst) & 4095 <= 4064` implies each is 32+ bytes from its own page end -- then two 16-byte loads and ONE MASKED STORE (`tzcnt`/`bzhi`/`kmovd`/`vmovdqu16 {k1}`) of exactly the characters that exist. No tail cascade, no clamp, and NO VZEROUPPER, because every register on that path is 128-bit and the upper state is never dirtied. **SIXTEEN BYTES WAS NOT ENOUGH AND A 32-BYTE ymm WOULD HAVE COST THE SAVING**: the first draft probed one xmm, `4 chars` went 0.96x->1.17x and `8 chars` STAYED WORSE at 0.93x, because 8 characters plus the terminator is 9 = 18 bytes; a ymm probe would owe a VZEROUPPER on every return, paid on exactly the short strings the path exists for, so two 128-bit halves cover the same 16 characters and owe nothing. **AND THE FINDING WORTH KEEPING -- A LOOP'S ALIGNMENT IS NOT A PROPERTY OF THE LOOP**: with the short path working, the A/B showed the WIDE rows losing a quarter of their speed (`1024 chars` 5.59x-5.82x against the parent's 7.54x-7.85x) while the 64-byte loop was untouched. The parent has NO ALIGN directive anywhere -- its loops landed where they landed -- and putting thirty instructions in front of cp_64 moved every label after it. One `ALIGN 16` restored it and then some: 1024 chars -> 7.74x-8.90x, at or above the parent in four of five passes. A fast path added in front of a hot loop can cost more in the loop it did not touch than it saves in the case it was written for, and the only way to see it is to measure the rows it should not have affected. |
| [230](changes/230-lstrcatw/) | `lstrcatW` (wide append) | `kernelbase.dll!lstrcatW` | **LANDED** — bit-exact, **5.90–6.41x** geomean over twelve consecutive runs and up to **27.83x** on "64 onto 4000" (the survey's WORST number, 1643 ns → 56.10), worst class **1.09x**. THE SCAN USES THE PAGE CLAMP, NOT 225's ALIGN-DOWN, because this function accepts an ODD-ALIGNED destination: aligning down to 32 would put the 16-bit lanes out of step with the characters, and a zero character at an odd offset spans two lanes with NEITHER lane zero — the terminator would be missed outright. Odd alignments are driven throughout correctness, not just at the guards. WHOLE CHARACTERS ONLY at a page edge (19 odd widths matched that model, 0 matched byte-wise) — and the probe that measured it had to be REWRITTEN: counting changed bytes reported ODD at every width, because the destination's own terminator is two ZERO bytes and appending 'A' over them changes only the low one. Two improvements landed before it parked: an empty-destination fast path (8 onto empty 0.57x → 1.30x) and leading each loop with a single 32-byte block before the pairs, which helped the LONG cases too since vpminuw folds the halves and the pair hit path must re-compare the one it landed in. UNPARKED IN TWO STAGES, exactly as 228. First the benchmark: for the short-onto-short rows the restoring store sits INSIDE the first 32-byte block the scan loads, and a wide load cannot forward from a just-retired narrow store, so rotating the destination took "8 onto 8" 0.97x → 1.47x with no change to the asm. That left "8 onto empty" a coin flip (0.86x–1.12x, failing about one run in three), fixed by two things: computing the append's page clamp UNDER the shortcut's load instead of before it (clamp-first measured 4.24–4.36 ns → 4.48–5.08, i.e. 1.10x → 0.93x — both orderings are recorded at the site), and replacing the 2..32-byte tail ladder with TWO OVERLAPPING MOVES, four fewer conditional branches. The ladder was BIMODAL: 4.24 ns on some runs and 4.90 on others, same executable, same data, while the live export sat at 4.69–4.71 every time — and in the slow mode "8 onto 8" was FASTER than "8 onto empty" despite strictly more work, which is what ruled out the code and pointed at branch-predictor state |
| [231](changes/231-strcatbuffa/) | `StrCatBuffA` (bounded append) | `shlwapi.dll!StrCatBuffA` | **LANDED** — **4.27x** geomean (up to **14.0x**). THE SURVEY'S USUAL DIAGNOSTIC SAYS NOTHING HERE: at 0.83x the wide cost it looks unpenalised, but that only means the WIDE form is slow too (108 ns), so the A/W ratio is uninformative and the absolute 90.14 ns is what matters — the largest left among the narrow siblings. IT IS NOT lstrcat WITH A BOUND BOLTED ON. cch is the TOTAL buffer size, and THE DESTINATION SCAN IS BOUNDED BY IT: if no terminator is found within the first cch bytes the function writes NOTHING AT ALL — it does not truncate and it does not append. That ONE rule also explains what looks like a second one (a destination longer than the bound is left alone: its terminator is outside the scanned window), a unification that only appeared after the first model — which capped the write position and so TRUNCATED such a destination — disagreed on 10660 of 52111 cases. It NEVER writes at or beyond index cch (0 violations over 31x31x41). AND IT ALWAYS STORES THE TERMINATOR once the scan succeeds, even appending nothing — invisible in RAM (a zero over a zero), which is why a 52111-case model matched without it; a PAGE_READONLY destination separates them, and the same trick showed the NULL-source check precedes the store. NO SEH WRAPPER, MEASURED: when the caller LIES about cch this export FAULTS rather than swallowing (37 of 37), the opposite of lstrcpy/lstrcat — so it is plain assembly with no second call, which is most of why the short classes land here and did not in the parked 228/230. Chunks are still page-clamped so it faults at the SAME byte. An empty destination skips the scan entirely (8 into empty: 0.79x → 2.30x). Correctness is exhaustive over dst x src x BOUND (152971 cases) plus a lying-cch sweep where both sides must fault having written identical bytes |
| [232](changes/232-pathremovebackslasha/) | `PathRemoveBackslashA` | `shlwapi.dll!PathRemoveBackslashA` | **LANDED** — **2.99x** geomean (up to **12.9x**). THE DRIVE-LETTER SET IS WHERE THE NARROW FORM DIVERGES FROM THE WIDE ONE, and it is exactly why the rule was re-measured instead of translated. Change 171 pinned the WIDE set by an exhaustive 65535-code-unit sweep: ASCII letters PLUS the Latin-1 letters. Sweeping all 255 byte values here gives 0x41..0x5A and 0x61..0x7A — 52 values in two runs, ASCII ONLY. So "\xC0:\" is NOT a protected root while L"\u00C0:\\" is, and an implementation that inherited the wide set would wrongly protect 78 byte values. Both the correctness corpus and the live corpus carry 0x80 on purpose so that difference is reachable. THE RETURN IS ALSO NOT WHAT A READER WOULD GUESS: always psz + max(n-1,0), a pointer to the LAST CHARACTER (not the terminator, not the start), returned whether or not anything was stripped — so every test compares the returned OFFSET as well as the buffer. One trailing backslash goes unless the remainder would be a bare root (m==0, or "\\", or "X:\"), and sweeping all 255 values as the trailing character shows EXACTLY ONE is ever removed: 0x5C. A forward slash is not a separator, so "a/" is left alone and "C:/" is not a protected root. Change 225's page-safe scan plus four compares; the scan is the whole cost. The two shortest classes are near a tie partly because this is an in-place benchmark whose per-iteration memcpy restore, paid by both sides, is a large share of the time at three characters |
| [233](changes/233-pathquotespacesa/) | `PathQuoteSpacesA` (quote a path with spaces) | `shlwapi.dll!PathQuoteSpacesA` | **LANDED** — **3.34x** geomean (up to **9.1x**). Exactly ONE byte value counts as a space (0x20 — a TAB does not), and THE LENGTH CAP IS 257, measured by sweeping 1..400: the last length that quotes is 257 and the first that does not is 258. 257 is a peculiar number belonging to a DIFFERENT function, so confirming it rather than assuming it was the point. On failure the buffer is untouched (0 of 143 over-long cases modified a byte), an already-quoted path is quoted AGAIN, and NULL returns 0. 0 mismatches over 87381 strings. ONE DELIBERATE DIVERGENCE, DOCUMENTED, ON A PATH THAT FAULTS: a buffer too small for the result faults rather than being swallowed (37 of 37), and dumping what the shipped function had written first shows indices 1..8 — a contiguous run from the LOW end, which a strict highest-byte-first shift CANNOT produce because its first write would be the faulting one. That is a chunked memmove whose 8-byte head store lands before the tail store faults. Ours shifts from the high end in 32-byte chunks and leaves different bytes behind. Not reproduced on purpose: the chunk schedule is not a contract, is visible only to a caller that wrapped a call it got wrong, and would change with any servicing update — so correctness asserts BOTH SIDES FAULT (77 of 77) and compares nothing else, while every case that RETURNS is bit-exact. One fused pass finds the terminator and any preceding space from two vpcmpeqb per block, trimming the space mask to the bits before the terminator so a space past the end cannot trigger quoting |
| [234](changes/234-pathfindnextcomponenta/) | `PathFindNextComponentA` | `shlwapi.dll!PathFindNextComponentA` | **LANDED** — **9.07x** geomean (up to **28.3x**). The worst per-byte ratio of the unconverted narrow siblings: 4.75x the wide cost for HALF the bytes. Writes nothing, no wrapper — the whole job is one scan for the first byte that is either the terminator or a separator, found with two vpcmpeqb and a vpor per block so a single extraction resolves both. THE DOUBLED-SEPARATOR QUIRK IS THE RULE THAT INVITES A WRONG IMPLEMENTATION: when the byte after the first separator is ALSO one, advance exactly ONE more — never the whole run. Measured with leading runs of 1..6: the offset goes 1, then 2, and STAYS 2 however long the run gets. "Skip the run of separators" is the obvious thing to write and it is correct on one and two backslashes and WRONG FROM THREE ONWARD — which a realistic path corpus, with single separators between components, could never catch. So correctness enumerates runs of length 1..10 at seven lead positions and the live corpus does the same. NULL is kept distinct from a pointer to the TERMINATOR because the export uses both (empty string → NULL; no separator → the terminator). Exactly one byte is a separator (0x5C; a forward slash is not). 0 mismatches over 349525 exhaustive strings. The cost is the DISTANCE to the first separator, not the string length — the four "separator at 2" rows all take the same time at 16, 64, 254 and 4000 bytes. Read-only bench, so neither side pays a per-iteration restore and the 2.28 ns fixed cost shows through cleanly |
| [235](changes/235-pathisfilespeca/) | `PathIsFileSpecA` | `shlwapi.dll!PathIsFileSpecA` | **LANDED** - **55.14x** geomean (up to **141x**). The last of the twelve narrow shlwapi siblings, and the largest ratio in the set. THE SURVEY UNDERSTATED IT BY TWO ORDERS OF MAGNITUDE: its 4.38 ns was measured on the shared subject "C:\Program Files\...", whose COLON SITS AT INDEX 1, so it timed the earliest possible exit. Measured properly the shipped export walks the string at a near-flat 2.9 ns PER BYTE with essentially no fixed cost (3.07 / 2.95 / 2.93 / 2.91 ns/byte at 12, 64, 254 and 4000) - about eight cycles a byte, the profile of a per-character step rather than a byte compare; it does stop at the first separator (254 with a backslash at 2 costs 7.74 ns, not 744). So the case that matters is the ordinary one - a bare file name with NO separator, which is exactly what a caller asks this question about and which forces the whole string to be scanned. Ours is one forward pass for any of THREE bytes - the terminator, 0x5C, 0x3A - as three vpcmpeqb and two vpor per 32-byte block, so a SINGLE EXTRACTION ANSWERS ALL THREE and whichever comes first decides: terminator TRUE, either separator FALSE. No second pass, no length. 48.6 bytes/ns at 4000 against the export's 0.34. THE EMPTY STRING IS TRUE - the one case a natural model gets wrong, and the ONLY mismatch in all 488281 enumerated strings when the probe first ran with "non-empty" in its rule; it is not a special case in the assembly either, since a string with no characters has its terminator in the first block. TWO separators, not one: 0x3A is the one a reader forgets and forgetting it is invisible on any corpus of realistic file names, so both are swept independently at every position rather than testing "a separator". A forward slash is NOT one. 0 mismatches over 488281 exhaustive strings; 493323 live cases. Read-only bench, so neither side pays a per-iteration restore and the 1.87 ns fixed cost shows through cleanly. This change's OWN PROBE was found carrying a literal newline inside a string literal - it did not compile, so the .exe on disk was an older build still printing the PRE-CORRECTION rule; a probe result is only evidence if the binary that produced it was built from the source in the tree |
| [236](changes/236-pathcommonprefixa/) | `PathCommonPrefixA` | `shlwapi.dll!PathCommonPrefixA` | **LANDED** - **75.80x** geomean (up to **258x**). First of a NEW family: shlwapi functions whose WIDE form was never converted either, so nothing could be inherited. 9.40 ns PER BYTE at 254 characters - about 27 cycles a byte, flat and linear from 16 to 2048, the worst per-byte cost left anywhere in shlwapi; the survey that found it (discovery/shlwapi_path3.c) times every candidate on BOTH an early-exit and a full-scan subject, which is the fix for the mistake that understated change 235 by two orders of magnitude. THE COMPARISON IS NOT BYTE-WISE AND THIS NEARLY BECAME A SECOND StrStrA: enumerating all 256x256 ordered byte pairs found 61 equivalence classes, and one of them is 0x5E ('^') == 0x88 - EXACTLY the conflation that made StrStrA unconvertible, where a single 0x88 satisfied an UNBOUNDED RUN of 0x5E. So it was tested rather than assumed: 16387064 combinations of "x\<v>\z" against "x\<w1><w2>\z" produced ZERO expansions, no byte is ignorable, and N copies of 0x5E match one 0x88 only for N=1 - the conflation is STRICTLY PAIRWISE here, which is the whole difference between this change and the abandoned one. The fold is then a closed rule: 0x61-0x7A, 0xE0-0xF6, 0xF8-0xFE fold by -0x20, plus five singletons (0x88->0x5E, 0x9A->0x8A, 0x9C->0x8C, 0x9E->0x8E, 0xFF->0x9F) - three range tests and four masked subtracts, never a case-mapping API. THE CUT was ISOLATED before being modelled: trunc(P) == PathCommonPrefixA(P+"x", P+"y") turns a two-argument function into a one-argument one, and enumerating all 9841 strings of {a,backslash,colon} to length 8 gives 9147 "last separator, dropped", 567 that KEEP it (last separator at INDEX 2 - positional, so "aa\" and "::\" keep it exactly as "C:\" does) and 127 that collapse to 0; the counts are closed forms, 9*(1+2+4+8+16+32)=567 and 1+2+4+8+16+32+64=127, which is how we know the rules are complete and not approximate. TWO DEFECTS IN THE SHIPPED EXPORT, REPRODUCED: a common prefix of exactly 2 is REPORTED as 3 while only two characters are written (no invented backslash, no overread - the count just exceeds the string, so a caller who trusts it walks past the terminator), and when the RESULT reaches MAX_PATH the copy is refused while the count is not (259 writes 259+NUL = exactly 260 bytes; 260 writes a bare terminator). NULL writes NOTHING AT ALL, unlike the no-common-prefix case which writes a terminator - three distinctions that only a POISON FILL reveals, so every comparison in correctness and in the live driver covers the whole 400-byte window. THE MAX_PATH RULE GOT PAST SIX PROBES: pcpa6.c validated the model over 3.65 MILLION pairs with 0 mismatches and was still wrong, because its longest sweep ran to 250 characters - an exhaustive corpus is only exhaustive over the dimension it enumerates, and length was not one of them. Method: raw bytes compared FIRST (two paths that agree usually agree exactly), the 44-instruction fold computed only on a block where they differ - 254 identical costs 18.64 ns against 32.85 ns differing in case, so the fold costs 0.056 ns/byte and nothing when it is not needed; last-separator index carried in a register so the cut needs no second pass; the one-byte page-edge step folds through the SAME sequence at 128-bit width so the rule exists once. 2418131 live cases |
| [237](changes/237-pathisprefixa/) | `PathIsPrefixA` | `shlwapi.dll!PathIsPrefixA` | **LANDED** - **111.16x** geomean (up to **282x**), the highest geomean of any shlwapi change here. 9.20 ns PER BYTE against 2.46 for the wide form - 3.7x the wide cost for HALF the bytes, and within a hair of PathCommonPrefixA's 9.40, which is what suggested the relationship this change is built on. MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED: PathIsPrefixA(a,b) == (PathCommonPrefixA(a,b,NULL) == strlen(a)) holds over 87067561 pairs from a fold-carrying alphabet, 29822521 more, all 64516 ordered byte pairs, AND lengths 250..600 - zero disagreements in every one. So the scan, fold, component cut and reported-count fixup are change 236's, and this is that code with the copy removed and one bounded length test added; the fold was nevertheless RE-DERIVED for this export (376 of 64516 ordered pairs equivalent = the 254 diagonal plus 122 folded, exactly 236's 61 two-member classes), because inheriting a shared rule is how the eight-change SPACE bug happened. THE LENGTH-TWO DEFECT IS VISIBLE IN THE ANSWER HERE, not just in a count: since PathCommonPrefixA reports 3 for a common prefix of 2, A TWO-CHARACTER PATH IS NOT A PREFIX OF ITSELF - lengths 0, 1 and 3..10 are all TRUE, so it is a hole at exactly one length - and A LONGER PATH CAN BE A PREFIX OF A SHORTER ONE: IsPrefix("xy\","xy") is TRUE (k=2, b exhausted, a continuing with a separator, so the count is 2, the fixup reports 3, strlen is 3), while length 4 is not. Both were PREDICTED from the composition and then measured, with closed-form counts. The reversal's closed form is NOT the obvious one and my first version of it was wrong: I asserted the alphabet size squared (36) and the sweep reported 100, because b need only be FOLD-equal to a's first two characters, so each position contributes its fold-CLASS size - (2+2+1+1+2+2)^2 = 100, and the same formula gives 4^2 = 16 over the fold-free alphabet where 16 was measured. The assertion was mine; the code was right. The length test is NOT strlen: computing it outright would read PAST a caller's terminator in the fixup case (a[3] on a two-character path), so it walks only from k to n - at most ONE byte, only when the fixup fired - and stops at the terminator. Read-only bench, 4.74 ns fixed cost; "255 of 4000" and "255 of 256" cost the same 11.1 ns because the work is the length of the PREFIX, not of either path; the fold costs 0.113 ns/byte and nothing when the raw bytes agree. Every row's TRUE/FALSE label verified against the live export rather than assumed. 2421150 live cases with both anomaly counts ASSERTED |
| [238](changes/238-pathmakeprettya/) | `PathMakePrettyA` | `shlwapi.dll!PathMakePrettyA` | **LANDED** - **26.85x** geomean (up to **132x**). 1900.95 ns for 254 characters - 7.48 ns PER BYTE, about 21 cycles - against 444.80 for the wide form: 4.3x the wide cost for HALF the bytes, the widest narrow/wide gap in the survey. THE FUNCTION IS NOT "LOWERCASE THE PATH": it is TWO different maps on TWO different parts, gated by a predicate narrower than either, with a bound on one of its two scans and not the other. It REFUSES on any of exactly 26 byte values ('a'..'z') and that scan is UNBOUNDED (a lowercase letter at index 560 of 600 still vetoes, and a refusal writes NOTHING); INDEX 0 is UPPERCASED, not skipped (34 values move); index 1 onward is LOWERCASED (60 values); the rewrite is bounded to 259 characters AND TRUNCATES, writing a NUL at index 259; the RETURN means "no ASCII lowercase was present", NOT "something changed" ("123456", "" and "\\\\" all return 1 while changing nothing). THE ASCII/CP1252 ASYMMETRY IS THE TRAP: the refusal set is ASCII-ONLY while BOTH case maps cover the full CP1252 range, so 0xE0 (a-grave) is a lowercase letter that does NOT veto and IS rewritten - an implementation using the code page's notion of lowercase would refuse on 30 values too many. That same asymmetry is the ONLY way index 0's behaviour is observable at all, since any ASCII lowercase letter there refuses the whole call. Both table sizes are closed forms (26+23+7+3+1 = 60 and 23+7+3+1 = 34, the uppercase map being 26 shorter because 'a'..'z' can never REACH index 0), and NEITHER table is change 236's - PathCommonPrefixA's comparison fold conflates 0x5E with 0x88 and neither map here touches either byte. THREE PROBE-DETECTOR FAILURES IN ONE CHANGE, all the same mistake and the function right every time: the first probe watched buf[0] for a lowercase letter and reported that all 255 values veto, contradicting its own output three lines earlier; it never asked whether index 0 was UPPERCASED; and the third reported index 259 as "left alone" because a not-lowercased test cannot tell "unchanged" from "REPLACED BY A TERMINATOR". Method: pass 1 finds the lowercase letter and the terminator in one 32-byte block (bzhi discards hits at or past the terminator) and yields the LENGTH; pass 2 lowercases 0..end-1 in blocks then overwrites index 0 with the uppercase map applied to the byte SAVED BEFORE the pass - safe because THE LOWERCASE MAP IS IDEMPOTENT, its outputs disjoint from its inputs, so it keeps the vector loop aligned to the string instead of offset by one. Pass 2 needs NO page check: it is bounded by the length pass 1 measured. AND THE BENCHMARK ONLY RESTORES WHERE IT MUST: a refusal writes nothing, so the first version's unconditional memcpy reported "4000, refused at 1" at 1.04x - memcpy racing memcpy, not the function; with the restore dropped on those rows (and the setup ASSERTING the buffer is byte-identical) the same row reads 2.90x and the refusal branch's real shape appears, 132x when it vetoes at index 200 |
| [239](changes/239-pathmatchspeca/) | `PathMatchSpecA` | `shlwapi.dll!PathMatchSpecA` | **PARKED** - NO ASSEMBLY WRITTEN. The grammar could not be pinned, and a matcher that is right on 97.6% of patterns is not a smaller version of bit-exact, it is a different thing that would be wrong SILENTLY on inputs nobody thinks to test. Four probes established plenty: EXACTLY two metacharacters (0x2A and 0x3F, no third among all 255 byte values), ';' separates alternatives (so "a;b" vs "a;b" is FALSE), LEADING spaces are stripped per alternative and trailing ones are not (" a" matches "a", "a " does not; a tab is not stripped), the fold is 60 classes of pure CP1252 case pairs with NO 0x5E/0x88 conflation - A THIRD DISTINCT ANSWER FROM THIS ONE DLL after 236's 61 classes WITH that pair and 238's 60-value one-directional map - backtracking is correct on every shape that breaks a naive matcher, NULL returns 0 in all three positions, nothing overreads, and there is no length bound to 720 characters. What it could NOT establish is the wildcard semantics themselves: the full model asserted against the live export FAILED 7647 TIMES OUT OF 325000, and printing the truth table instead of summarising it shows why - a TRAILING run of >=2 stars requires a character ("a*" matches "a", "a**" does not, yet "**a" DOES), EXACTLY ONE '?' may match zero and never two (N question marks match lengths N-1 and N, which is not DOS_QM where a whole trailing run vanishes), and a '.' changes what the empty subject matches ("*.*", "*.?", "?.*" and "?.?" all match "" while "**", "??" and "*?" do not). Every hypothesis explaining one is contradicted by another row - the signature of ad-hoc preprocessing inside the shipped code, not a grammar recoverable from outside. RESUME THE WAY CHANGE 194 DID: that one's ERANGE behaviour also fitted no probed rule and was settled by READING THE DISASSEMBLY, which made two contradictory observations obvious at once. At 3.25 ns/byte this was also the LOWEST-value of the four targets the survey found (9.40, 9.20, 7.48, 3.25), so forcing it was the wrong trade. The four probes are committed so the work restarts from the truth table |
| [240](changes/240-pathcchremovefilespec/) | `PathCchRemoveFileSpec` | `kernelbase.dll!PathCchRemoveFileSpec` | **LANDED** - **4.87x** geomean (up to **15.9x**). A smaller win than 235-238, and the reason is MEASURED rather than assumed: this one starts at 0.348 ns/byte where those started at 3.25 to 9.40, so the ceiling was lower before a line was written. TWO SURVEYS PICKED IT. discovery/ucrt_ntdll_sweep.c is a NEGATIVE RESULT worth as much as a positive one - everything still unconverted in ucrtbase and ntdll is ALREADY VECTORISED in the shipped DLL (strchr 0.023 ns/byte, strstr 0.037, strlen 0.045, wcslen 0.012, nothing above 0.07), the opposite of shlwapi's per-character narrow functions. discovery/kernelbase_pathcch.c found the real gap: kernelbase has TWELVE converted functions against ucrtbase's 75 and ntdll's 68, and most of its untouched PathCch* predicates are O(1) and not worth taking (PathIsUNCEx is 1.98 ns FLAT from 15 to 1000 characters). FOUR RULES, AND THREE WERE FOUND BY ISOLATING A DERIVED QUANTITY rather than reasoning about the code. (1) THE PROTECTED ROOT IS NOT PathCchSkipRoot'S ROOT - measured as the FIXED POINT of the function itself, applying it until S_FALSE - because SkipRoot INCLUDES the root's trailing separator and this function's protected prefix does not, a consistent difference of one on every UNC path ("\\srv\shr\dir": root 9, SkipRoot 10), with 793 disagreements over 21845 strings and SkipRoot declining outright on 15355 more; they agree on drive and rooted paths, which is exactly why borrowing looked safe, and it is change 232's mistake in a new costume. The server/share parse also has a clause no documentation produces: an EMPTY SHARE falls back to the end of the SERVER, which is what "\\a\" -> 3 and "\\\" -> 2 require. (2) A DRIVE LETTER IS 114 VALUES, NOT 52 - ASCII plus the CP1252 accented letters, 0xD7 and 0xF7 absent and 0xDF present - derived by sweeping ALL 65536 WCHAR VALUES, because the function is WIDE and 1..255 is not a sweep; this is change 232 seen from the other side, where the NARROW sibling took ASCII only. (3) IT CLEARS A SLOT PER REMOVED SEPARATOR, not one terminator at the cut, and the extra slot depends on the root's TYPE rather than on whether the root ends in a separator - found by DUMPING THE BUFFER after two probes had reported "a\\" -> model "a" | live "a" as a failure while printing the same string on both sides; with the differing index reported it was instantly clear (INDEX 2: model 005C, live 0000). (4) cch BOUNDS THE HIGHEST INDEX WRITTEN, neither the result nor the input: measured as min_cch(P) it equals hi+1 on all 87381 strings, so "C:\dir\file.txt" (15 chars) SUCCEEDS at cch=7 while "\\srv\shr" needs 10 to say S_FALSE. Model validated over ~5.8 MILLION cases, HRESULT and whole buffer, 0 mismatches, with no live sibling in it. METHOD: the shipped function walks FORWARD per character tracking the last separator; this one does one vectorised wcslen then a BACKWARD vectorised scan that finds the separator in its FIRST 32-byte block and never reads the rest - which is why the ratio climbs from 1.21x at 16 characters to 15.88x at 4000. The backward scan needs NO page check, reading only inside [root, n) which the wcslen already proved is mapped. AND ONE CLASS REGRESSED FIRST: computing the length before checking cch put "cch too small" at 0.83x, which would have parked the change; the fix is sound rather than cosmetic - the answer is never shorter than the root, so cch <= rootlen can be refused before any wcslen - and that row is now 1.90x. The live driver's "rejection path ran in bulk" assertion also FAILED at first with 0 E_INVALIDARG, because cch = len+1 is always sufficient; the assertion caught a gap in the corpus, which is why the counts are asserted instead of printed |
| [241](changes/241-pathcchaddbackslashex/) | `PathCchAddBackslashEx` | `kernelbase.dll!PathCchAddBackslashEx` | **LANDED** - **5.76x** geomean (1.92x to 10.03x), shared with `PathCchRemoveBackslashEx` below. **THIS CHANGE WAS PARKED FOR FIVE RUNS BY A BENCHMARK ARTEFACT, NOT BY THE FUNCTION.** The gate is "no size class below 0.97x" and `add 16` sat reproducibly at 0.90x while thirteen of fourteen classes were 1.92x to 10.07x - so it was parked, honestly, with the contract fully pinned and a note that closing "the last ~2 cycles" was a measurable experiment rather than a research question. It was: the experiment showed there were no cycles to close. THE RESTORE'S ADDRESS WAS THE MEASUREMENT. These rows restore the buffer with a single 2-byte store - already the minimal restore, already the lesson change 238 taught - and then call again ON THE SAME BUFFER. Our first act is a 64-byte vector load covering that address, and a wide load overlapping a just-retired narrow store CANNOT use store-to-load forwarding: it waits for the store to drain. The shipped implementation reads one character at a time and forwards from it cheaply. Measured with identical work on both sides and only the restore's ADDRESS different: at 16 characters, same buffer ours 7.92 ns live 6.45 ns (0.81x), rotated ours **2.73 ns** live 6.64 ns (**2.43x**); at 64, 8.90 -> 3.67 while live moved 16.16 -> 16.32; at 260, where the restored address lies outside the first block the scan loads, 9.02 -> 10.22 and the effect vanishes. **Live moves by 3%, ours by 2.9x.** That also explains the symptom nobody could place: our time looked FLAT from 16 to 260 characters, which is a stall, not work. So each row now holds four buffers and restores the one the PREVIOUS call dirtied - the same single store, the same single call, one address apart - and the bench PRINTS both measurements every run so the artefact stays visible instead of being quietly designed out. This is change 238's lesson at one remove: a restore can be minimal, can cost almost nothing, and can still replace the measurement - not by taking time but by creating a DEPENDENCY that penalises one implementation's access pattern. ONE REAL OPTIMISATION CAME OUT OF RE-EXAMINING IT and is kept because it is right rather than because it moved the row: "does it already end in a separator" was a load of `[n-1]`, which cannot issue until the length scan produces `n`, so it added ~6 cycles of pure serial delay; both questions are now answered from the SAME vector compares, the terminator mask giving `n` while the separator mask - shifted by one character and indexed by the terminator's own bit position - says whether the character before it was a separator, computed in parallel rather than after. The 64-bit mask combine this change had already tried and REVERTED (it cost the 4000-character row 10.07x -> 7.69x, because the shl/or sat on the critical path of a loop that runs many times) is used in that fast path ONLY, where it runs once and replaces two dependent movmskb chains with one. TWO EXPORTS IN ONE CHANGE because they are one shape: a length scan then O(1) work plus two output parameters. FIVE WAYS THEY DIVERGE FROM CHANGE 240, WHICH IS THE SAME FAMILY, and every one would have been wrong if 240's rule had been inherited: (1) THREE FUNCTIONS, THREE cch CEILINGS - 240 rejects above 0x8000, AddBackslashEx rejects cch > 0x7FFFFFFF+n (bisected, and matching that formula exactly at n = 1, 4, 7, 10, 13, 16 and 19 - it is the REMAINING count reaching STRSAFE_MAX_CCH), and RemoveBackslashEx has NO CEILING AT ALL, accepting SIZE_MAX at every length; (2) that ceiling applies ONLY on the path that WRITES - a path already ending in a separator accepts SIZE_MAX too; (3) the two functions return DIFFERENT ERROR CODES for the same too-small cch; (4) THE PROTECTED PREFIX IS THE STRUCTURAL PREFIX ONLY - "C:\", "\", "\\" and "\\?\C:\" keep their separator while "\\srv\", "\\srv\shr\" and "\\?\UNC\s\h\" lose it, so THE SERVER AND SHARE ARE NOT PROTECTED, which is neither 240's root nor PathCchSkipRoot's; measured as the function's own FIXED POINT, 0 disagreements over 97656 strings; (5) NULL FAULTS in both, where 240 returns E_INVALIDARG - so there is no value to match and the honest match is to read the pointer too, which costs nothing because the scan's first load does it. Also: the out-parameters are written on the FAILURE path (NULL/0), so the harness seeds them with a 0xDEAD sentinel rather than zero, and `end` is reported EVEN WHEN THE CALL DECLINES, pointing at where the terminator WOULD go ("C:\" reports +2 while returning S_FALSE). Model validated over ~175000 cases on all four observables, 0 mismatches; correctness PASSES over 654000+ three-way calls including the drive letter over all 65536 wchar values. THE OTHER FOUR OPTIMISATIONS, all kept, took the geomean from 2.80x to 4.55x before the harness fix: zero pushes and zero calls (both fit the seven volatiles; RemoveBackslashEx gets the budget by noticing n is DEAD once e is known), the out-param clearing moved off the hot path, the restore reduced to ONE 2-byte store, and 64 BYTES PER ITERATION. Live substitution ran OUR assembly inside both real exports 65639 times each, all four observables compared, prologues restored byte-identical - with ppszEnd compared as an OFFSET when it points into the buffer and as a VALUE when it does not, because the failure paths write NULL and that is the same answer at two different buffer addresses |
| [241](changes/241-pathcchaddbackslashex/) | `PathCchRemoveBackslashEx` | `kernelbase.dll!PathCchRemoveBackslashEx` | **LANDED** - **5.76x** geomean with `PathCchAddBackslashEx` above, same change, same `impl.asm`. Its rows were never the problem - 1.86x to 9.97x throughout - but they moved with the harness fix too (16 characters 1.86x -> 3.37x, 64 characters 3.19x -> 4.55x), which is the cleanest confirmation that the artefact was the restore's address and not anything about the appending path: the same store, the same wide load, the same stall, on a different function. It differs from its sibling in every dimension that could have been inherited: NO cch CEILING AT ALL (it accepts SIZE_MAX at every length, where AddBackslashEx refuses above 0x7FFFFFFF+n), a DIFFERENT ERROR CODE for the same too-small cch, and it never writes past the terminator - which is why its live proof includes a page-guard sweep with the path ending at a PAGE_NOACCESS boundary. Its register budget comes from noticing that `n` is DEAD once `e` is known, which is what let it reach zero pushes and zero calls like its sibling |
| [243](changes/243-pathcchcanonicalizeex/) | `PathCchCanonicalizeEx` | `kernelbase.dll!PathCchCanonicalizeEx` | **LANDED** - **13.12x** geomean (up to **28.2x**), dwFlags 0. THE FIRST CHANGE IN THIS PROJECT THAT BLACK-BOX PROBING COULD NOT FINISH. Three probes pinned 99.63% of 797161 enumerated paths and then stalled on one residual family - live returns `\\` where every component-list model returns `\` - and SIX SUCCESSIVE THEORIES each fitted all of it but one case: a dropped `.` eating its preceding separator dies on `\\.` -> `\\`, eating its following separator dies on `aa\.` -> `aa`, a trailing `.` becoming an empty component dies on the same case, joining components around a root string dies on `.\\\.` -> `\\` against `.\\.\` -> `\`, an underflowing pop appending a separator dies on `\..\` -> `\`, and a pop clamped at a protected root dies on `\\srv\..` -> `\` eating a LEADING separator. Two inputs with the SAME surviving component list and DIFFERENT answers is proof that no component-list model can exist, so probes/algebra.c printed the entire closed subspace over `{ \ . }` to length 7 to make that visible, and then THE DISASSEMBLY WAS READ - the resume path change 239 documented and change 194 used. capstone is installed here, so a 60-line PE-export disassembler was enough: follow the one-instruction jmp thunk at RVA 0xF5780 to the body at 0x10C30 and read ~1100 instructions. IT SETTLED THE QUESTION IN ONE READING, AND THE TRUTH IS SIMPLER THAN ALL SIX THEORIES: **there is no component list, no stack and no root copy**. It is ONE LINEAR WALK with a write cursor, dispatched on the LENGTH of each component - 0 EMITS ONE SEPARATOR (which is why doubled separators survive canonicalisation while dot components do not, and why the leading separators of a rooted or UNC path need no special handling at all: they are just empty components), 1 that is `.` skips itself AND the separator after it (and with none after it, REMOVES ONE CHARACTER from the output unless the output is a root), 2 that is `..` walks back - stepping over the last character UNTESTED, then putting the cursor ON the separator it finds so the separator goes too - and anything else is copied verbatim. FIVE "ANOMALIES" FALL OUT OF WHERE THE CURSOR HAPPENS TO BE: `C:..` -> `C:\` is not a root plus a pop but ONE component whose trailing dots the finish strips to `C:`, which the two-character rule completes (hence `C:..\b` unchanged - there `b` is last); `C:a\..` -> `\` because `C:a` is an ordinary component with no root anywhere; `\\srv\..` -> `\` because the backward walk passes the share separator and stops ON the second LEADING separator, consuming it; `\\srv\..\..` -> `\\` because the output is then `\`, the separator before the second `..` emits itself making it `\\`, and PathCchIsRoot(`\\`) refuses the pop - AN UNDERFLOWING POP MAKES THE PATH LONGER; and `a\..\b` -> `\b` against `..\b` -> `b` because a pop that removed something still owes a separator while a refused one does not. **THERE IS NO PROTECTED ROOT IN THIS FUNCTION AT ALL** - `C:\a\..` leaves `C:` behind and the drive is RECONSTRUCTED by a positional test on the output (exactly two characters whose second is `:`), which is why `\:` comes back as `\:\` and why three changes of reasoning about clamping could not fit the measurements. ONE DECISION WAS INVISIBLE FROM OUTSIDE and is now named: the calls at 0x11483 and 0x118B1 consult a predicate on the OUTPUT BUILT SO FAR, and probes/isroot.c calls that internal helper directly and finds it IDENTICAL TO THE EXPORTED PathCchIsRoot over 8587 strings, 0 differences - so the implementation reproduces a documented rule, never an address. FOUR THINGS THE DISASSEMBLY CORRECTED IN THE FIRST PASS, all then measured: flag 0x08 IS NOT A NO-OP (it suppresses the trailing-dot strip: `C:\z..` is `C:\z` at flags 0 and `C:\z..` with 0x08 - the first pass reported "none observable" because its test path did not end in dots); a `*` BEFORE A TRAILING DOT STOPS THE STRIP (`C:\a*..` -> `C:\a*.`); TWO LENGTH LIMITS, NEITHER OF THEM cch - with flags 0 a RESULT of 260 characters or more is ERROR_FILENAME_EXCED_RANGE HOWEVER LARGE cch IS, and one component of 257 or more is the same error, so a 1000-character path is not a long input for this function but an ERROR ROW, and the cap applies to the RUNNING OUTPUT so an overflow is permanent even when later `..` components would have shrunk it back; and THE TWO FINAL FIXUPS ARE BEST-EFFORT - cch 1 on `""` returns S_OK and `""` rather than failing to fit `\`, and cch 3 on `C:` returns S_OK and `C:`. A DRIVE LETTER IS 114 VALUES - ASCII plus the Latin-1 letters, 0xD7 and 0xF7 absent, 0xDF present - measured over ALL 65536 code units and NOT IsCharAlphaW (47455 accepted, 47342 disagreements) nor C1_ALPHA; the same set change 240 found, re-derived here rather than inherited, which is what caught the oracle's one untested assumption. THE IMPLEMENTED DOMAIN IS dwFlags == 0 (what PathCchCanonicalize passes) BECAUSE FLAG 0x01 IS NOT A POST-STEP BUT A DIFFERENT BACKWARD WALK - `C:a\..` is `\` at flags 0 and `C:a\` with 0x01, `\\srv\shr\a\..\..\..` is `\` against `\\srv\` - i.e. a second contract; nonzero flags TAIL-JUMP to the original body, resolved through the export's jmp thunk, so they behave identically BY CONSTRUCTION, and the live driver passes 48 nonzero-flag cases THROUGH the patched thunk to prove the fallback reaches it. METHOD: an AVX2 pre-scan looks for the only thing that can complicate the walk - a `.` at a component start, found as the two-character pattern `\.` with BLOCKS OVERLAPPING BY ONE CHARACTER so it can never straddle a boundary and no carry is needed - and when there is none and the input is at most 256 characters (one test covering both the MAX_PATH result cap and the per-component cap) the answer is a verbatim vector copy; otherwise the walk dispatches on the FIRST CHARACTER before measuring anything, because only an ordinary component needs its end found and every second component in a path is a separator. Against a shipped implementation that makes an INDIRECT CALL PER COMPONENT and copies one wchar_t at a time with a bounds test per character, that is 14.25x at 16 characters rising to 27.5x at 250, 28.2x on doubled separators, and 4.8x to 6.2x on the dot-component rows that take the per-component walk. THREE OPTIMISATIONS, EACH MEASURED: an overlapping-move ladder instead of a per-character copy tail (a 7-character component was 7 loop iterations, now two 8-byte moves) took the geomean 10.84x -> 11.54x; a SCALAR PROBE of eight characters ahead of find_sep's vector path, because the scans are serially dependent through the read pointer and ~20 cycles of vector LATENCY per short component is worse than eight predicted-not-taken branches, took it to 11.92x; and dispatching on the first character plus a length-based PathCchIsRoot whose two cheapest rejections come first took it to 13.12x. TWO BUGS THE GATES CAUGHT: the UNC shape test started its second scan one character too far, so `\\\\` looked like a root and a trailing dot removed nothing; and carrying the skip distance in r10 ACROSS the isroot call - a register isroot's own header documents as clobbered - spun the walk forever, found as a correctness process at 160 seconds of CPU in section 1. Gates: correctness 7474384 cases with 0 mismatches three ways (ours, an independent oracle, and the live export) including every cch from 0 to 24 on 135 shapes, all 128 flag values, 6.7 MILLION enumerated strings, page-edge inputs one character before a PAGE_NOACCESS boundary, a canary proving nothing is written past cch, and NULL faulting in both with the fault UNWINDABLE (which is what change 241 got wrong); the oracle itself validated first over 5.84 million cases against live; ABI PASS; and live substitution ran OUR assembly inside the real export 87596 times, all matching, prologue restored byte-identical |
| [242](changes/242-pathcchappendex/) | `PathCchAppendEx` | `kernelbase.dll!PathCchAppendEx` | **LANDED** - **12.27x** geomean (up to **26.3x**), dwFlags 0, shared with `PathCchCombineEx` below. **SOLVED BY COMPOSITION, NOT BY REDERIVATION.** Both functions are EXACTLY a JOIN followed by `PathCchCanonicalizeEx`, and change 243 had just solved that canonicalisation to 0 mismatches over 11.77 M paths and landed it at 13.12x - so the whole remaining contract is the JOIN, a rule over two arguments with no canonicalisation logic of its own. Measured against `PathCchCanonicalizeEx(join(base, more))` on the LIVE export over **789,770 pairs, 0 mismatches**: a 33-shape corpus crossed with itself, every string to length 4 over `{ \ . a : }` crossed, every string to length 3 over `{ \ ? U C a : . / * }` crossed, and 63 pairs against EVERY cch from 0 to 30. This is the payoff of the isolation habit this family keeps rewarding - change 236 turned a two-argument cut into a one-argument `trunc(P)`, change 240 measured a protected root as its own fixed point, and change 243 was CHOSEN over these two precisely because it takes one path instead of two. THREE JOIN RULES ARE MEASURED RATHER THAN NATURAL: (1) **the seam never doubles a separator, and it avoids that by STRIPPING the one `more` carries** rather than by skipping the one it would insert - `"" + "\a"` comes back as **`a`**, with the separator GONE, which no "insert only if needed" rule can produce; (2) **the drive test happens AFTER the strip** - `"\" + "\a:"` is **`a:\`**, so the separator was removed and what was left was then recognised as drive-qualified and replaced the base, where testing first would have joined it; (3) **`\\?` is not a root** - `"\\a"`, `"\\."`, `"\\\a"`, `"\\"` and even `"\\\"` all replace the base, but `"a" + "\\?"` is **`a\?`** and `"" + "\\?a"` is **`?a`**, because an INCOMPLETE extended prefix is not a root of any kind, while `\\?\` and everything under it replaces again. cch COMPOSES TOO, which was the dimension most likely to break it because **Append works IN PLACE**: its cch describes a buffer holding the base on entry and the answer on exit, with the joined string in between LONGER than either when `more` carries a `..` - swept over 63 pairs x every cch 0..30 it agrees on all 1953 triples, so both inherit change 243's MAX_PATH result cap, its `ERROR_FILENAME_EXCED_RANGE`-versus-`ERROR_INSUFFICIENT_BUFFER` split and its best-effort final fixups. TWO THINGS THAT COULD NOT BE INHERITED, both measured: **these two CHECK their pointers where `PathCchCanonicalizeEx` FAULTS** (a NULL destination is `E_INVALIDARG`, a NULL source on either side reads as empty, and Combine refuses when BOTH sources are NULL), and **a cch outside the range is refused WITHOUT TOUCHING THE BUFFER** where the canonicaliser empties it - visible only on Combine, whose destination starts as poison, because on Append the base sits in the buffer and hides it. METHOD: **the joined string is never materialised.** Canonicalisation is a streaming walk over a read pointer, so the walk runs over the effective base, then switches its read pointer to the effective `more` and keeps going; the pops reach back into the base's output, the trailing-dot strip lands on the last component of the WHOLE join, and the final fixups see the whole answer, all through the shared write cursor. A scratch buffer would have had to be 64 KB to be correct, because a long base whose `more` pops it away still has a short answer. For Append the output buffer IS the base, so that segment is walked IN PLACE - safe because canonicalisation only ever drops characters, so the write cursor never passes the read cursor. THREE BUGS THE GATES CAUGHT, each a specific lesson: (1) **a dot component at the end of the base is followed by the SEAM**, so `"." + "a"` is `a` and not `\a` - the lookahead and the advances had to become STREAM-aware rather than segment-aware, which is the one place a two-segment walk is not simply a walk; (2) **the overlapping-move copy ladder is unsafe in place** - change 243's tail loads the last 32 bytes AFTER storing the head, and with source and destination overlapping by one character that reads bytes the head store already overwrote, which duplicated a chunk of a 32-character path; every load now precedes every store in its case; (3) `sa_boundary` zeroed `rax` as a default return value and then read `[rax-2]`, faulting at address -2. Plus a link-time one worth recording: MASM makes PROC symbols PUBLIC by default, so `find_sep`, `copy_n` and `isroot` collided with change 243's identically-named helpers the moment the live driver linked both - `OPTION PROC:PRIVATE` with four explicit exports. Gates: correctness **1,071,571 calls with 0 mismatches THREE WAYS** (ours, an oracle that is this change's join composed with change 243's validated model, and the live exports) over a crossed corpus x every cch 0..20 x the extremes, two crossed enumerated alphabets, the length caps, all 128 flag values, NULL in every position, and a canary proving nothing is written past cch; the oracle itself validated first over 231,666 cases; ABI PASS; and live substitution ran OUR assembly inside both real exports 3549 times each, all matching, prologues restored byte-identical - with the corpus a 34x34 CROSS PRODUCT because the join's rules live between the two arguments |
| [242](changes/242-pathcchappendex/) | `PathCchCombineEx` | `kernelbase.dll!PathCchCombineEx` | **LANDED** - **12.27x** geomean with `PathCchAppendEx` above, same change, same `impl.asm`, dwFlags 0. COMBINE DIFFERS FROM APPEND IN EXACTLY ONE PLACE across all 789,770 probed pairs, and it is a rule with three parts: a `more` with two or more leading separators ALWAYS replaces here (so `"a" + "\\?"` is `\\?` for Combine where Append joins it to `a\?` - Combine has no `\\?` exception); a `more` with exactly ONE leading separator prepends **base's ROOT WITHOUT its trailing separator**, because `"C:\a" + "\b"` is `C:\b`, so the drive root contributes `C:` and NOT `C:\` - prepending `C:\` would leave a doubled separator and change 243 proved doubled separators SURVIVE canonicalisation, which makes the difference visible in the answer; and **a relative base is REFUSED** with `E_INVALIDARG` while an EMPTY base is not, acting as a root of length 0 so `"" + "\a"` is `\a`.
