The root it prepends is its own computation, not `PathCchSkipRoot`'s and not change 240's or 241's: `\\?\` plus a drive gives 6 characters, `\\?\UNC\server\share` runs through the share, a UNC base gives server-and-share with trailing separators trimmed **down to one** so `\\` contributes `\`, a drive gives 2, one leading separator gives 0, an empty base gives 0 - and `\\?` or `\\?\` with neither a drive nor `UNC\` after it has NO ROOT AT ALL, so `"\\?" + "\a"` is `E_INVALIDARG`. That last one is the SAME exception that stops `\\?` from replacing on Append's side: one rule, two places. Implemented as the same two-segment walk, with segment 1 BOUNDED to the root rather than terminated - which is why the walk carries an end pointer at all, and why a component's end, its lookahead and its advance all have to respect it |
| [244](changes/244-hashdata/) | `HashData` (the shlwapi byte hash; `shlwapi!HashData` is a jmp thunk into this body) |  `kernelbase.dll!HashData` | **LANDED** - **2.58x** geomean over five runs (up to **3.97x**), worst class **2.10x**. THE FIRST TARGET FOUND BY SWEEPING WHAT THE PROJECT HAD NEVER MEASURED: `discovery/shlwapi_url_str.c` was written for the three shlwapi families the earlier surveys skipped -- the URL functions, the formatters/parsers, and the remaining path predicates and writers -- and this came out of it at **0.157 GB/s** with no locale, no code page, no grammar and no allocation in it. `probes/cost.c` then measured the whole TWO-DIMENSIONAL cost surface and found it flat at **0.42 ns per (source byte x digest byte)** for every digest of six bytes or more, RISING to 2.02 ns at a one-byte digest where the shipped loop's memory round trip becomes the limit. CONTRACT ENTIRELY MEASURED, including the 256-entry substitution recovered IN CLOSED FORM -- a one-byte source into a one-byte digest is a single substitution, so 256 calls give all 256 entries, and 0 of 256 differ from the table in the shipped binary. The seed h[j]=(BYTE)j is observable on its own because cbData==0 consumes nothing, and it WRAPS (h[256]=0 at cbHash 300); the source order was fixed by all 65536 two-byte sources (last-byte-first 65536, first-byte-first only the 256 palindromes); cbHash==0 writes NOTHING and cbData==0 reads NOTHING, both proved against PAGE_NOACCESS pointers rather than a poison fill. WHY IT IS FASTER: digest byte j depends only on itself and the source byte, so the digest bytes are INDEPENDENT CHAINS, yet the shipped loop walks ONE LANE AT A TIME THROUGH MEMORY -- load h[j], RELOAD src[i], xor, load the table, store h[j], about eight uops for one byte of progress. This holds the lanes in REGISTERS, twelve at a time, which is the widest the register file allows once the table base, the source pointer and the current source byte are accounted for. TWO EXTRA KERNELS BECAUSE "ALWAYS TWELVE" REGRESSED A CLASS: a pass costs the same for two lanes or twelve, so a one-byte digest threw away twenty-six of twenty-seven uops per source byte and measured **0.72x** at "16B -> 1"; cbHash 1..4 now get their own LEAF kernels with no saved registers at all (0.72x -> 2.22x, geomean 1.87x -> 2.59x), and a last group of four or fewer gets a four-lane kernel -- cbHash 16 is exactly one full group plus four, so that is half the common case and took "4K -> 16" 2.67x -> 2.91x. THE OVERLAP FALLBACK IS REQUIRED, NOT DEFENSIVE: probes/overlap.c ran every one of 2401 relative placements of a 24-byte source against a 20-byte digest, and the grouped shape matched 760 -- EXACTLY the disjoint ones -- because the shipped inner loop re-reads src[i] per lane, so a digest write landing on it changes what the remaining lanes consume. Overlapping ranges therefore fall back to a byte-for-byte emulation, and that same measurement is what licenses grouping at all. No vector form helps: a 256-entry byte substitution costs sixteen shuffles plus blends per sixteen lanes against sixteen loads, vpgatherdd is worse, and T is NOT affine over GF(2)^8 so the composition cannot be folded |
| [245](changes/245-urlunescapew/) | `UrlUnescapeW` (the shlwapi `%XX` decoder; `shlwapi!UrlUnescapeW` is a jmp thunk into this body) | `kernelbase.dll!UrlUnescapeW` | **LANDED** - **5.38-5.89x** geomean over five runs (up to **14.24x**), worst class **1.34x**. THE COST IS SCAFFOLDING, NOT THE TRANSFORM: the same 1000-character URL through URL_UNESCAPE_INPLACE -- the unescape walk alone -- costs 529 ns of the 1575 the full call takes, and a memcpy of the same buffer costs 0.2 ns. The disassembly says where the rest goes: FIVE sequential O(n) walks plus a heap round trip -- ntdll!wcslen, a LocalAlloc(LMEM_ZEROINIT) with its capacity quadrupled in a loop, a copy-in at ONE WCHAR per five instructions, the walk, a scalar strlen OF THE RESULT, and a copy-out. The allocation is a row of its own: our cost FALLS from 23.2 to 16.1 ns across 63 -> 64 characters while the shipped cost is flat, because 64 is where its 65-WCHAR stack buffer stops fitting. CONTRACT ENTIRELY MEASURED: the hex set is 22 ASCII characters and nothing else, swept over ALL 65535 non-NUL code units in BOTH escape positions (22 accepted in each, the positions agree everywhere, ZERO non-ASCII) -- so unlike StrChrIW and the case-insensitive family this project scoped out, no locale is involved; %00 returns E_INVALIDARG with the destination AND *pcch untouched even mid-string; the size test is STRICT, equal is refused with E_POINTER and *pcch = result+1, destination untouched; URL_UNESCAPE_INPLACE is tested BEFORE all argument validation (the export's first real instruction is `bt r9d, 0x14`) and never writes *pcch; and of all 32 flag bits exactly TWO change the answer. TWO VECTOR PASSES, because both failure rules must leave the destination untouched, so the result length and the absence of %00 have to be known before the first store -- but the measuring pass is REPLACED by a %00 PATTERN SCAN whenever the buffer is bigger than the input, one compare pair per sixteen characters with no per-escape work, sound because a '%' can never be swallowed by a preceding escape (an escape's payload characters are hex digits and '%' is not one), so every '%' is an escape start and the text "%00" occurs iff a zero-valued escape does. It borrows ymm3 because all six VOLATILE vector registers were taken and xmm6 upward are non-volatile -- gate 3 would have caught the alternative. DELEGATED via a tail jump: URL_UNESCAPE_AS_UTF8 (it hands runs to MultiByteToWideChar(CP_UTF8) without WC_ERR_INVALID_CHARS, so "%FF%FE" becomes two U+FFFD -- hand-rolling that is the change-239 failure mode), any other unimplemented bit, and overlap with the destination ABOVE the source (a direct writer reproduces the shipped staging buffer only while the write cursor cannot pass the read cursor). THE BENCHMARK WAS NOT REPRODUCIBLE AND THAT IS HALF THE STORY: the escape-dense row first read 0.46x, and two plausible diagnoses were both WRONG -- inlining the per-escape path moved it 2371 -> 2378 ns, and walking the destination through all sixteen 256-byte page offsets gave 296-305 ns at every one, which is what exposed that our function does that row in ~300 ns and the BENCH was measuring its own .bss layout. Adding a diagnostic block ahead of the table moved that row to 289 ns and five others by up to 35%. The arenas are VirtualAlloc'd and page-aligned now, every subject and destination on a page boundary, and five consecutive runs agree within 10%. Correctness: 234168 cases three-way, comparing the HRESULT, the WHOLE destination against a sentinel fill with 16 characters past the capacity, AND *pcch -- and the first run's 266 failures were ALL in the ORACLE, none in the assembly |
| [246](changes/246-pathcanonicalizew/) | `PathCanonicalizeW` (the legacy shlwapi canonicaliser; `shlwapi!PathCanonicalizeW` is a jmp thunk into this body) | `kernelbase.dll!PathCanonicalizeW` | **LANDED** - **6.36-6.53x** geomean over five runs (up to **22.11x**), worst class **3.21x**, for **FOURTEEN INSTRUCTIONS** of new assembly. IT IS CHANGE 243 WITH A BOOL AROUND IT, and the disassembly says so in one line: `kernelbase!PathCchCanonicalizeEx` at RVA 0xF5780 is literally `jmp 0x10C30`, and `PathCanonicalizeW` at 0xF0F0 calls THAT SAME BODY with `mov edx, 0x104` (cch = MAX_PATH) and `xor r9d, r9d` (dwFlags = 0). PROVED BEFORE IT WAS BUILT, which is the rule change 242 established and the reason this cost an afternoon: probes/compose.c ran **451543 cases** comparing the BOOL, the WHOLE destination buffer and GetLastError against `PathCchCanonicalizeEx(dst, MAX_PATH, src, 0)` wrapped as the disassembly dictates -- the enumerated {\, ., a, :} subspace to length 7 and {\, ., a} to length 9, 45 shapes the Ex contract turns on, every input length 250..300 plus shrinking and over-long ones, and 400000 fuzz cases -- with **ZERO disagreements**. THE WRAPPER'S OWN CONTRACT: NULL dst or NULL src gives FALSE with last error 87, `*pszDst = 0` happens BEFORE pszSrc is validated (so `PathCanonicalizeW(dst, NULL)` returns FALSE having ALREADY CLEARED dst -- the one thing a careless envelope gets wrong, and both the probe and the live driver check it), and a failing HRESULT becomes its LOW WORD when `(hr & 0x1FFF0000) == 0x70000` or is passed through whole otherwise. AND IT PRODUCED A MEASUREMENT OF AN OLDER DECISION: this change's first correctness run compared the whole buffer, did not know that change 243 deliberately does not reproduce the bytes past the terminator (the shipped body canonicalises in place and leaves its own scratch behind the answer, so demanding those bytes would forbid ANY vectorised store), and failed on shapes as small as `"a."`. Quantified against the live export over 7215 enumerated cases at cch = MAX_PATH: **0** HRESULT differences, **0** in the result string and its terminator, **0** written at or past cch, and **2989** in the dead bytes between -- with the INDEPENDENT ORACLE diverging on exactly the same 2989, which is what says the dead region is a property of the model rather than a bug in the assembly. Those numbers are now in 243's RESULTS.md where the decision lives. The 259-character rows are the honest weak spot and were then MEASURED rather than hand-waved: it is a CLIFF AT 257, not a slope (256 chars 24.5 ns / 27.2x, 258 chars 175.7 ns / 3.83x, both S_OK), and it is a deliberate decision in 243 rather than a defect -- its fast path requires the input to be at most 256 characters because that ONE test covers both the per-component cap and the MAX_PATH result cap. Inputs of 257-259 can still succeed, so they take the scalar walk. An earlier draft called this "where a future pass would pay"; that is WITHDRAWN -- the window is exactly THREE input lengths and we are still 3.8x inside it, which does not justify re-running a 7.4-million-case gate on a landed change and re-verifying 242 and 246 behind it. Recorded in 243's RESULTS.md so nobody measures it twice |
| [247](changes/247-pathaddextensionw/) | `PathAddExtensionW` (append an extension if the path has none) | `kernelbase.dll!PathAddExtensionW` | **LANDED** - **3.27-3.49x** geomean over five runs (up to **7.25x**), worst class **1.54x** -- INCLUDING THE EMPTY PATH, which is the row a prototype of this function regressed on before it was written. THE DEFAULT EXTENSION IS `L".exe"`, NOT THE EMPTY STRING: the disassembly points at a static string whose bytes are `2E 00 65 00 78 00 65 00 00 00`, and the probe confirms it directly -- `"" + NULL` comes back as `".exe"`, so a NULL pszExt on an extensionless path REWRITES it. That is the one genuine surprise in the function. CONTRACT MEASURED: the append point is exactly PathFindExtensionW's over **55987** enumerated strings on the alphabet `. \ space a b :` to length 6 with ZERO disagreements (including that the appended text lands exactly at that pointer); the bound is on the **RESULT** -- n + extlen <= 259 appends, >= 260 refuses, and the boundary tracks the SUM rather than either operand; a refusal writes **NOTHING AT ALL** (a 300-character path comes back byte-for-byte unchanged); an empty extension returns TRUE and writes nothing, not even the terminator already there; an extension with no leading dot is appended verbatim; and an UNTERMINATED extension at a guard page FAULTS, because lstrlenW does not swallow it -- so the scan here is page-safe and faults on exactly the strings the shipped one faults on. COMPOSED ON CHANGE 132 DELIBERATELY: the append point is the one rule in this function subtle enough to get wrong, and this project ALREADY GOT IT WRONG -- 132 shipped with only the backslash stopping its backward scan and needed a SPACE too, wrong on 295513 of 2015539 enumerated strings -- so 132's assembly is linked alongside rather than re-derived, and every enumerated sweep here uses an alphabet CARRYING A SPACE, because one without it would validate the same mistake twice. THE FIRST THREE BENCH ROWS EXIST BECAUSE A PROTOTYPE FAILED THEM: a disassembly fan-out measured 1.34x-5.74x on change 132's size classes but a reproducible **0.87-0.94x at an empty path** and marginal rows at 2-6 characters, so starting the table at sixteen characters would have hidden exactly that; the assembly does not regress there and the reason is structural -- it composes on 132's lean finder and keeps the envelope to a single call, where the prototype paid for a C frame and a memcpy. The restore is ONE store on a ROTATED buffer, and the two refusal rows get none at all -- the bench ASKS THE FUNCTION which rows write rather than assuming. Two bugs in its own test, both mine and both recorded: the guard-page block first appended in place to a path ending at the last readable character (the append ran off the page), and the rewrite then planted a dot that a filler backslash shadowed, so the export appended and it segfaulted again |
| [248](changes/248-urlunescapea/) | `UrlUnescapeA` (decode %XX escapes in a URL, narrow) | `kernelbase.dll!UrlUnescapeA` | **LANDED** - **7.83-8.02x** geomean over five runs (up to **34.66x**), worst class **1.34x**. THE NARROW TWIN OF 245, AND WRITING IT AS "245 WITH char" WOULD HAVE BEEN WRONG THREE TIMES. The survey measured it at **1.85 ns/char against the wide form's 1.24** -- slower per character for half the data -- and the disassembly (RVA 0x49DB0) shows the same five walks plus a heap round trip, with NO CODE-PAGE CALL anywhere: no MultiByteToWideChar, no CPINFO, no DBCS helper, so unlike StrStrA (scoped out when a code-page fold conflated 0x5E and 0x88) this one really is byte-wise -- all 484 accepted hex pairs decode to va*16+vb and of the 255 non-NUL byte values exactly **22** are hex digits with **ZERO** bytes >= 0x80 accepted. THE THREE ASYMMETRIES, each measured rather than inherited: (1) **URL_UNESCAPE_AS_UTF8 is REFUSED**, not implemented (branchless at 0x49E37), so this needs no delegation for it where 245 did -- but only on the path that reaches the check, because INPLACE is tested BEFORE ALL VALIDATION and INPLACE|AS_UTF8 therefore unescapes; (2) **A FAULTING SOURCE IS SWALLOWED** -- the length comes from lstrlenA, which is SEH-wrapped, so an unterminated source at a PAGE_NOACCESS page returns `S_OK, cch=0` where **THE WIDE FORM FAULTS** (change 247 established that for lstrlenW), and an implementation without a `__try` would crash a caller the shipped function serves; (3) **%00 does not refuse on the non-in-place path, it TRUNCATES** -- `"a%00b"` gives S_OK with `"a"` and cch=1, because that path CALLS the walk at 0x49F20 and then IGNORES its HRESULT, while the in-place path TAIL-CALLS the same walk and so returns its E_INVALIDARG. One function, two paths, two answers for one input; the probe first asserted the wide form's rule here and was wrong. AND (3) IS WHY THIS IS SIMPLER THAN 245: a zero-valued escape merely ENDS the result instead of having to refuse before anything is written, so a buffer bigger than the source -- which is how anyone sizes one -- needs neither a measuring pass nor 245's three-character %00 vector scan, and **ONE pass writes the answer**. The exact-capacity rows (2.40x, 2.74x) are the honest worst case and exist because that route is a structural choice: a plain source cannot even reach the two-pass route, since its result length equals its input length. FOUR BUGS, three of them mine and all recorded in the files: `vzeroupper` in the length scan **zeroed the upper lane of the comparison vectors** that set_cmp had just built, so the in-place scan matched `%` only in the low 16 bytes of each block -- it passed **1085965 enumerated cases** silently, because a six-character string never reaches the vector path, and only the long-string rows caught it, against BOTH the oracle and the live export at once; a byte-at-a-time scan remainder made **63 plain bytes 2.5x SLOWER than 64** (27.17 vs 10.80 ns) and was the whole of the 16-byte in-place row, which came in at **0.86x and parked the change on gate 2**; the fix for that read ONE aligned block when a sub-32-byte remainder can straddle TWO -- **1100 mismatches**, every one an escape near a long string's end; and a "faster" decode with an explicit bound and two INDEPENDENT table lookups measured **SLOWER** (351->417 ns escape-dense) because the premise was wrong -- the second load depends on the first only through a BRANCH, which predicts, so it already issued in parallel. The one overlap direction a forward pass cannot do (destination ABOVE the source and inside it) is **STAGED rather than delegated**, because a delegated case cannot run under a live patch at all -- and staged rather than moved in place, because a `memmove` of n+1 bytes into a buffer the caller only promised *pcch of would write past it: a caller with a 6-byte buffer and a 200-byte source is entitled to E_POINTER. So **nothing sat out the patched pass**, unlike 245. Gate 1: **1096298** cases three-way bit-exact, including all 32 flag bits singly and all **1024 pairs** (which is what makes "every bit but 18 and 25 is ignored" a measurement rather than a reason for delegation machinery). Gate 4: **9228** cases live, 40 of them the swallowed fault -- unwinding out of our assembly and through our C `__except` **inside a patched export's frame** and still returning the shipped answer, the one case a correctness harness can pass while a live run fails |
| [249](changes/249-urlhasha/) | `UrlHashA` (hash a URL into a digest, narrow) | `kernelbase.dll!UrlHashA` | **LANDED** - **2.18-2.22x** geomean over five runs (up to **2.68x**), worst class **1.42x**. AN ENVELOPE, AND SAYING SO IS THE POINT: the shipped function is **22 instructions** -- two NULL checks, `call lstrlenA`, `call 0x0C0A10`, `xor eax,eax` -- and 0x0C0A10 is HashData's body, so this change is a **COMPOSITION OF TWO LANDED CHANGES** (225 for the length, 244 for the hash) plus six new instructions. probes/urlhash.c proved that BEFORE anything was written: `UrlHashA(url,h,cb)` is bit-identical to `HashData(url,strlen(url),h,cb)` over **369 cases with zero disagreements**; had it failed, the change would not exist. Composed rather than re-derived because 244's kernel is a MEASURED shape with three conditions that took a probe each (the seed WRAPS at 256, the source is consumed LAST BYTE FIRST, and the grouped form is wrong on all **1641 OVERLAPPING** placements because the shipped inner loop re-reads the source byte per lane) -- re-deriving any of it would invite a second, differently-wrong copy. The LENGTH is composed too, and specifically change 225's **SEH wrapper** rather than its bare core: lstrlenA swallows an access violation, so an unterminated URL at a PAGE_NOACCESS page returns **S_OK with the identity seed** (measured at every tail 1..48), and a bare scan would crash a caller the shipped function serves. **ONE PATCH, TWO EXPORTS**: `UrlHashW` is not a second hash but a wide-to-narrow converter that finishes with `call 0x12F750` -- a **DIRECT INTERNAL CALL to the very address the UrlHashA export names** -- so the wide export runs our assembly **without a byte of itself being modified**, proved live by the counter (486 cases) rather than asserted; the bench measures the part the patch does NOT reach (at a 1000-byte url, UrlHashW 2906 ns = UrlHashA 2146 + 760 ns of conversion). THREE CORRECTIONS TO THE SURVEY THAT REFUTED THIS TARGET: (a) "byte-identical to the export" was overstated -- diffed, the worker is that body MINUS the export's own two NULL checks and MINUS its `xor eax,eax`, though the table claim holds exactly, both `lea`s resolving to RVA **0x2A6010**; (b) the "inlined copy is 1.15-1.45x FASTER" measurement was right in direction -- re-measured, UrlHashA beats the HashData export by ~1.16x at 16-byte digests **while also paying for lstrlenA** (105.25 vs 92.01 ns; 26681 vs 22985 at 4096) and is level at 1-byte digests -- but the ~1.6x projection it produced was too pessimistic, since 244's kernel beats the inlined copy by about what it beat the export by; nothing in the two streams explains the gap, so it is PLACEMENT, not code; (c) the survey missed that the wide form calls the narrow one at all. THE ORACLE RECOVERS THE PERMUTATION TABLE AT RUNTIME (256 one-byte calls to the live HashData export, since `h[0]=T[0^b]`) so it shares no constant with our chain -- not 244's c_tab, not 244's own REF_T -- and recovers it through a DIFFERENT export than the one under test; it came back a permutation, 256 distinct entries of 256. Its own ordering bug was caught while writing it: the shipped envelope takes the length BEFORE the worker seeds, so seeding first would move the URL's terminator on an overlapping placement. Gate 1: **75624** cases four-way bit-exact (including all 65025 two-byte urls and every cbHash 0..300). Gate 2: the per-row diagnostic caught a bench bug -- a 4096-byte subject plus its terminator overflowed a 4096-byte slot, so the row labelled "4096 url" reported **urlLen=8192**; the verdict was unaffected but the row was a lie. Gate 4: **5097** cases live, twenty prologues, and this is **the only change in the harness that moves an export it never patched** |
| [250](changes/250-rtlipv6stringtoaddressexw/) | `RtlIpv6StringToAddressExW` (IPv6 `[addr%scope]:port`, wide) | `ntdll.dll!RtlIpv6StringToAddressExW` | **LANDED** - **2.46-2.50x** geomean over five runs (up to **4.96x**), worst class **1.14x**. **THE LAST MISSING MEMBER OF A SIXTEEN-FUNCTION FAMILY** -- Ipv4/Ipv6 x StringToAddress/AddressToString x A/W/ExA/ExW is sixteen exports and image/tree carried fifteen. It was left out ON PURPOSE: change [122]'s row says "`ExW` scoped out -- Unicode digits". **THAT READING IS FALSE, and probes/ip6exw.c settles it by sweeping all 65536 UTF-16 units through both positions**: the scope accepts 10 units and the port 10/10/22 at its three bases, with **ZERO >= 0x80 anywhere** -- U+0661, U+0665, U+FF10 and U+FF11 all refused outright. THE ENVELOPE IS PURE ASCII; the seventeen Unicode blocks change [166] had to implement live only in the ADDRESS, and the address here IS change 166's export -- the call at RVA 0x0C318E targets **0x0C33F0, which is RtlIpv6StringToAddressW's own RVA**, not a copy and not a shared worker. (The "octal" sweep's three runs are `0`-`7` plus `X` and `x`, which are not octal digits at all: they turn `0<u>` into a 0x prefix with an empty body, accepted as port 0.) CONTRACT MEASURED: the WHOLE STRING must be consumed -- the Ex form has no Terminator, so it REFUSES `::0x1` and `::1.2.3.0x5` where the W form accepts them, differing on exactly those two of fifteen shapes and on ZERO address bytes; `:port` only inside brackets; an empty port is 0; port <= 65535 and scope <= 2^32-1; *Port is NETWORK order. **AND COMPOSING A LANDED CORE INTO A NEW CALLER FOUND TWO THINGS IN CHANGE 166**, both now recorded there: (a) a **0.79x SPEED REGRESSION on `::`**, the shortest valid IPv6 address, which 166 had no row for -- its gap zero-fill was a BYTE LOOP clearing memory the prologue had already zeroed, and the whole `::` shift is dead work when nothing follows it; fixed, and **166's own geomean rose 3.348x -> 3.835-3.894x** (`::` 0.79x->1.08x, `fe80::1` 3.22x->4.16x); (b) an **OVERSTATED CLAIM** -- 166's RESULTS said it compared "STATUS, all 16 address bytes and *Terminator" but the harness compares the address ONLY on success, and measured over 55987 strings the shipped export leaves partial bytes on **17268** FAILING calls where 166 leaves none (0 status, 0 Terminator, 0 on any success). The obvious fix was TRIED AND MEASURED WORSE (17268 -> 18240, inverted), so it is recorded rather than papered over, and this change's harness reports the number in its own output. **THE BENCHMARK PARKED THIS CHANGE TWICE AND WAS WRONG BOTH TIMES**: first five saved registers where only the cursor must survive the call, then **4K ALIASING** -- every output slot was exactly 4096 bytes, so all 24 outputs a row owns began at page offset 0 and collided in one L1 set, making `::` (18.07 ns, core 5.64) appear slower than `[::1]:80` (12.80 ns, core 7.70). Two diagnostics kept in bench.c settled it -- the same rows timed in three ORDERS (the number followed the row, not the position) and 166's core timed in the same harness -- and staggering the slots made the rows monotonic in work. Gate 1: **631004** cases three-way, including all 65536 units in EIGHT positions. Gate 4: **263083** cases and **the first live proof this family has ever had** (121, 122 and 166 all landed without one) -- and patching `RtlIpv6StringToAddressW` **ALONE** drove the SHIPPED, UNPATCHED ExW through our core **7111** times |
| [251](changes/251-pathissamerootw/) | `PathIsSameRootW` (do two paths share a root) | `shlwapi.dll!PathIsSameRootW` | **LANDED** - **48.1-49.9x** geomean (up to **335x**), worst class **6.17x**. At **5.73 ns/char** this was the THIRD-HIGHEST per-byte cost of every shlwapi export not yet converted, behind only HashData ([244]) and PathCommonPrefixW ([167]) - and it was almost entirely [167] already: the disassembly (RVA 0x0CBC30) is `PathSkipRootW(a) != NULL && (PathSkipRootW(a) - a) <= PathCommonPrefixW(a,b,NULL) + 1`. The only thing in the way was THE ROOT SKIP, which is also what parks [163], and which [167]’s own RESULTS predicted would "unblock all three of the slowest remaining shlwapi functions" - a prediction that was WRONG about PathIsPrefixW ([177] needed no root parser at all) and RIGHT about this one. **THE ROOT PARSER WAS DERIVED AND REFUTED BEFORE ANY ASSEMBLY WAS WRITTEN**: read out of `PathCchSkipRoot` (RVA 0x02B2F0) and checked against the live export over **210720 cases with ZERO differences**. `PathSkipRootW` is that plus one rule (`a root of exactly 2 ending in ":" -> NULL`), measured over 1365 strings with 0 disagreements. THREE THINGS THAT LOOK LIKE SPECIAL CASES AND ARE NOT: `\\.\` is **not a prefix** (`\\.\PhysicalDrive0` is 18 because it is the ordinary UNC walk with server `.`; only `?` at index 2 is special); the **empty-share rule** is why leading backslash runs look non-monotonic (`\`→1, `\\`→2, `\\\`→3, `\\\\`→3 and 3 for every longer run) - [163] recorded that as "no single rule fits" and it is ONE rule, the `cmove` at 0x2B4C4, consuming the separator after the SERVER even when the server is empty and the one after the SHARE only when it is not; and **`\\?aa:` is an ERROR** even with a drive at index 4, because the prefix is the FOUR characters `\\?\` - the model said 6 until the sweep said otherwise, 24 of 210720 cases, all that shape. ONE TRAP IN THE IMPLEMENTATION: the volume prefix folds case with `c | 0x20`, exact for letters (only `V`/`v` reach `v`) but **NOT for the brace** - `[` | 0x20 is `{`, so folding it would accept `Volume[`; the brace is compared exactly and `[` is in the perturbation corpus. THE WIN IS NOT THE ROOT SKIP: the shipped `PathCchSkipRoot` is **FLAT in the input** (5.16 ns at 250 chars vs 5.21 short), so all 604 ns is PathCommonPrefixW walking component by component - which [167] replaced - and the root parser here is deliberately SCALAR. Ours also SHORT-CIRCUITS where the shipped one does not: it calls PathCommonPrefixW BEFORE testing the root for NULL, so a relative path pays for the whole walk and discards it; that is unobservable and it is the entire 335x row. Gate 1: **422462** cases in two independently-checkable halves (the root parser 210807, the function 211655 with 24586 TRUE). Gate 4: TWO exports patched separately - the root skip first, then the function - 90178 cases, 576 of them exercising the short circuit |
| [252](changes/252-rtlfindunicodesubstring/) | `RtlFindUnicodeSubstring` (find a substring, optionally case-insensitively) | `ntdll.dll!RtlFindUnicodeSubstring` | **LANDED** - **21.96-23.07x** geomean over five runs (up to **74.5x**), worst class **1.20x**. Found by surveying the 191 uncovered ntdll `Rtl*` exports; at **0.755 ns/byte** case-sensitive and **1.181** case-insensitive it was the most expensive of the 13 measured BY A FACTOR OF SIX - 6.0 and 9.4 MICROSECONDS to scan a 4000-character string for an absent 8-character needle. The shipped code (RVA 0x498E0) is a naive O(n*m) scan that shifts its window by ONE character and, case-insensitively, **makes two function calls per character comparison**. THE GO/NO-GO WAS THE FOLD, the same question that decided [167]: a table pointer in `rcx` is the shape of `RtlUpcaseUnicodeChar`, `call qword ptr [rax+0xF0]` would be the NLS sort machinery that puts `StrCmpLogicalW`/`StrChrIW`/`StrStrIW` out of reach - but shape is evidence, not proof, so the export was asked directly over ALL 65535 code units: **0 disagreements**, and all 973 units whose upcase differs match their partner. The core is a two-anchor AVX2 block filter whose every load is inside the counted buffer BY CONSTRUCTION (`i+15 <= n-m` puts the far read at `n-1` exactly), so no page-safety clamp exists - and correctness.c TESTS that rather than repeating it, running the whole search against a `PAGE_NOACCESS` page butted onto the end of `Length`. **TWO ROWS OF THE FIRST BENCHMARK FORCED REWRITES.** (1) The insensitive filter folded to ASCII and had to treat every non-ASCII unit as a candidate - unavoidable for a fold, since no arithmetic joins U+00E0 to U+00C0. It was correct (a superset the scalar verifier settles) and passed every gate, yet on **Cyrillic** it admitted all 4000 positions: **2.33x**, against 16x on ASCII. That is not an adversarial input, it is most of the world’s text. The fix was to stop folding the haystack and ENUMERATE THE NEEDLE: `probes/classsize.c` measured the ordinal table’s case classes as **63590 singletons, 973 pairs, NOTHING LARGER**, so membership is at most two compares, known before the loop - EXACT, not a superset. Cyrillic went **5145 ns -> 165 ns (2.33x -> 73.98x)**, geomean 8.563x -> 14.472x. "At most two" is a property of a table this code does not own, so it is RETURNED, not trusted: `wia_casemate_init()` reports the largest class it saw and gate 1 fails if it is not 2. (2) A needle shaped `a......a` inside a run of `a` makes the textbook anchors (0 and m-1) THE SAME CHARACTER, admitting every position: 5664-7192 ns, **0.91x-1.17x** - it crossed the 0.97x gate AT RANDOM from run to run, and a gate that reports a different verdict on the same code is not a gate. The obvious third anchor was rejected before being written (three more ops in EVERY block, ~30% of a 3.2-cycle loop, to rescue one shape); instead the far anchor is CHOSEN - the last position whose character differs from `needle[0]`, one O(m) walk per call, **nothing per block**. That row went to **167.75 ns (44.24x)** with the common row unchanged, and it generalises further than its own case: `ababac` in `ababab...` has distinct first and last characters that are both everywhere, and the last DIFFERING character lands on the `c`, which is nowhere. Insensitively, "differs" means "in a different case class" - picking `A` against `a` would admit exactly what the first test already did. Gate 1: **1097325** cases three-way vs an oracle that folds by CALLING the live `RtlUpcaseUnicodeChar` per character (so an unbuilt table cannot be mirrored), with the corpora split BY WHICH PATH THEY REACH - the lesson of [248], where 1085965 enumerated cases passed in silence while the vector path was broken. Gate 4: 60000 cases, 0 differ, counter 60000, restored byte-exact - and the post-restore pass earned its place immediately, reporting 14285 differences with our counter at ZERO, the shipped export disagreeing with ITSELF because the harness’s generator carried PRNG state across passes. WHO CALLS IT, stated because it bounds the live proof: `ntoskrnl.exe`, `afd.sys`, `dxgkrnl.sys`, `dam.sys`, `CAD.sys`, `VerifierExt.sys` - **all kernel mode** - plus only `wldp.dll` (two call sites inside an internal helper 0x15DE past the nearest export) and `ci.dll` (imports the name, ZERO direct call sites), so neither yields a second export the way [249] or [250] did . **CORRECTION (2026-09-16, found while probing [254])**: an earlier version of this row and of 252’s own header justified that clause with "U+017F ordinally upcases to the ASCII S". **That is wrong** — `RtlUpcaseUnicodeChar(U+017F) = U+017F`. The NT ordinal table is far NARROWER than Unicode case folding: U+017F, U+0130, U+0131, U+00DF and U+00B5 all map to themselves and **nothing in 0x80..0xBF folds at all**, so no non-ASCII unit upcases INTO ASCII and that argument never held. The clause was still required for the U+00E0/U+00C0 reason, and **nothing in the shipped code changes** — the version that shipped enumerates the anchor’s case class exactly and never used that filter |
| [253](changes/253-strcat/) | `strcat`, `wcscat` (append a NUL-terminated string) | `ucrtbase.dll!strcat`, `wcscat` | **PARKED** - correct and **3.4x-7.6x** on a long destination, but **0.65x-0.90x** appending a few bytes. Found by a mechanical ucrtbase sweep (enumerate exports, subtract the 75 covered, drop the `_o__` aliases / `_l` locale variants / `_mbs` code-page family, measure every survivor): at **0.200 and 0.101 ns/byte** these were the most expensive pure byte loops left, and **3.9x worse than `strncat` and 33x worse than `memcpy` for the same work**. The disassembly (RVA 0x0ED700) is **SWAR, not SIMD, in BOTH halves** - the classic `0x7efefefefefefeff` has-zero trick at eight bytes an iteration, no AVX anywhere. CONTRACT probed: returns `dst` on every path; **NULL FAULTS** both arguments; and an EMPTY SOURCE writes exactly one terminator and nothing past it - which is the whole design constraint, because unlike [252] a copy makes every stray byte written a corruption **no return value can reveal**. So the tail writes exactly L bytes through an overlapping store pair, and gate 1 compares **the entire 4096-byte destination buffer**, not the string, with a `PAGE_NOACCESS` page on the read side AND the write side: **374464 cases, 0 failures**. **THE PARK IS GATE 2, AND THE FIRST DIAGNOSIS OF IT WAS WRONG.** Believing the overhead was the two calls and the `VZEROUPPER`, a fast path was written that inlined both and used only VEX-128 (a routine that never writes a 256-bit register needs no `VZEROUPPER` at all - a real saving, not a skipped instruction). It made **no difference**: 7.71 -> 7.96 ns, inside the noise. The real cost is the **vector-to-GPR round trip**: `VPCMPEQB -> VPMOVMSKB -> TZCNT` is most of ten cycles of pure LATENCY, `strcat` needs TWO of them serialised by the branch between, and the shipped SWAR answers a four-byte append in about three cycles while the vector version is still waiting on its first mask. A third version went pure SWAR for short strings and handed the destination end to the vector path instead of recomputing it - 0.63x -> 0.74-0.87x, and `empty + 32 B` not at all, because **at that point we are running the shipped algorithm and can only tie it, minus our own dispatch**. Microsoft’s SWAR is not an oversight: it is the right instrument below ~64 bytes and the wrong one above ~200, and this change only has the second half - the same wall [099] hit. **Where the destination is LONG, which is the pattern real code suffers from, it wins outright: `4000 B + 16 B` = 7.57x, `1000 B + 16 B` = 5.80x.** Unparking needs a three-tier byte/SWAR/AVX2 implementation whose first two tiers re-implement what ucrtbase already does |
| [254](changes/254-findstringordinal/) | `FindStringOrdinal` (Win32 ordinal substring search, four modes) | `kernelbase.dll!FindStringOrdinal` | **LANDED** - **6.22-6.55x** geomean over six runs (up to **24.8x**), worst class **1.07x**. The one member of this family that is NOT collation, which is why it is reachable when `StrStrIW`, `StrChrIW`, `StrCSpnIW` and `StrCmpLogicalW` are not. The shipped code (RVA 0x0A1E90) is a naive O(n*m) scan shifting its window by ONE character, plus - insensitively - an inline ASCII fold and a **three-level trie walk per character** above 0xC0. **THE FOLD TURNED OUT TO BE CHANGE [252]’s TABLE, WHICH WAS NOT OBVIOUS**: the disassembly SHORT-CIRCUITS everything below 0xC0, which looked like it had to differ from `RtlUpcaseUnicodeChar` - but asked over all 65536 code units the answer was **0 differences**, because **nothing in 0x80..0xBF folds at all**. So the short-circuit is an optimisation, not a different table, and 252’s case-partner table (max class size 2) transfers exactly. That same probe **corrected a factual error in 252’s own header** (U+017F does NOT upcase to ASCII S). **THE REFUSAL CONTRACT is where the surprises were**, all measured: `SetLastError(0)` ON ENTRY (success and an ordinary miss both leave 0); NULL checked BEFORE the lengths; flags default to FROMSTART, and exactly one FIND_ bit with nothing else or ERROR_INVALID_FLAGS; and **`bIgnoreCase` is an UNSIGNED compare against 1 - a "BOOL" that rejects 2 AND rejects -1** with ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER, so `test r8d,r8d / jnz` would be wrong on an input real code produces. Strings are **counted, not terminated**: with cchSource=5 over {a,b,0,c,d} a needle of {0,c} is FOUND at index 2. **FIND_FROMEND runs the two-anchor filter BACKWARDS** - blocks from the end, highest candidate via BSR not TZCNT - which is the half with no precedent in 252, and it is not "scan forwards and keep the last hit": `FROMEND 4000, hit @3900` answers in the first block it looks at, **14.01 ns against 96.61, at 571 GB/s**. **THREE THINGS THE BENCHMARK FORCED, one a wrong diagnosis.** (1) `SetLastError` is a CALL and was the entire budget of FIND_STARTSWITH - 0.92x and 0.84x on the two "no" rows; the shipped code does not call it either (its helper is `RtlSetLastWin32Error`, whose whole body is a store to `gs:[0x68]`), and one store instead took those rows to 1.07x/1.08x, verified because gate 1 compares `GetLastError()` three ways. (2) Calling the verifier PER POSITION is right for a block and wrong for a tail - thirteen indirect calls on a 16-character search, **0.44x**; inlining the tails and hoisting the first character (25 of 26 positions are rejected by it) gave 0.82x then 0.94x - better, still not enough. (3) The fix was to **stop walking the tail at all**: when fewer than sixteen START positions remain the far anchor cannot be read, but the NEAR one often can, so one vector compare plus `BZHI` replaces the whole scalar walk - **4.24 ns, 2.48x**, and the guard is `i+16 <= n`, NOT `i <= limit`, which is weaker and would read past the buffer. Gate 1: **288235** cases comparing the index AND the last error, against an oracle that folds by CALLING the live `RtlUpcaseUnicodeChar` and implements FROMEND the OTHER WAY ROUND (forwards, keeping the last hit) so a backward off-by-one cannot be mirrored - plus a guard page. Gate 4: 50000 cases, index and last error, counter 50000, restored byte-exact **A TGL VARIANT NOW EXISTS** -- [`impl_tgl.asm`](changes/254-findstringordinal/impl_tgl.asm), **7.00x -> 7.66x, LANDS in 5 of 5 runs**, correctness PASS every run (index AND last-error exact vs the oracle and live kernelbase). On bench #3 the parent PARKS on exactly one row, `STARTSWITH 4000, yes` at **0.72x** -- and that row had NEVER BEEN REPORTED, because revalidate.ps1 matched bench rows with a one-token label and this bench labels its rows in words, so the sweep parsed ZERO rows here and published it as LANDS geo=6.987 with no regressions. **THE CAUSE IS NOT THROUGHPUT, WHICH IS THE OBVIOUS AND WRONG READING OF "4000"**: bench.c line 112 builds that row with a 4000-character haystack and an EIGHT-character needle, and FIND_STARTSWITH compares at position 0 only -- so the whole call is a validation and an eight-character compare, and the 4.7 ns gap IS the compare. The parent's fo_verify walks one character at a time: eight iterations of a seven-uop body with a taken branch each, about twenty cycles against one wide compare. **AND THE PARENT IS RIGHT TO BE SCALAR WITHOUT AVX-512**: a vector compare of an m-character needle wants a whole register, m may be smaller than that AND may sit at the end of the haystack, so it needs a bounds test and a scalar fallback -- which is most of what was being avoided. A MASKED load faults on neither, so the tail needs no bounds test at all: `vmovdqu16 zmm0{k2}{z}` + `vpcmpw` + `kortestd`, with `bzhi` building the mask from the characters that really exist. Only the case-SENSITIVE verifier changes; fo_verify_ci resolves through the case-mate table and is untouched. The row goes 16.69 ns -> 7.58 ns -- faster than kernelbase's 12.03 rather than level with it -- and `STARTSWITH 4000, no` improves too, because the same compare serves both. |
| [255](changes/255-rtlfindlongestrunclear/) | `RtlFindLongestRunClear` (the longest run of clear bits in a bitmap) | `ntdll.dll!RtlFindLongestRunClear` | **LANDED** - **4.71-5.05x** geomean over five runs (up to **40.5x**), worst class **2.19x**. **The most expensive thing left in ntdll per byte - TWICE [252]’s target.** Found by surveying the bitmap family (discovery/ntdll_bitmap.c) at ~1.03 ns/byte on a 64 Kbit map, and the decisive observation is that **the cost is NOT per-run**: eight clear runs cost 8423 ns and two hundred cost 8689, so the ~1 ns/byte is the SCAN - 65536 bits examined ONE AT A TIME. RVA 0x0E3240 is nine instructions around `RtlFindClearRuns(bitmap, buf, 1, TRUE)`, and that BOOLEAN is also why the same function measured **80x apart** in the survey: with FALSE it stops as soon as its array fills (144 ns having seen 0.2%% of the bitmap), with TRUE it must examine everything. CONTRACT probed: **the FIRST run wins a tie** (so the best updates on a STRICT improvement, and candidates within a word must be considered first-to-last); no clear bits gives 0 with `*StartingIndex` **written as 0**, not untouched; and **the slack past `SizeOfBitMap` is MASKED** - 40 declared bits with bits 36..63 clear reports FOUR. **THE INNER SEARCH IS THE PART WORTH READING.** The obvious way to find the runs inside a word is a loop per run, and it is exactly the trap: `0xA5A5A5A5` has a clear run every two bits - SIXTEEN THOUSAND of them - so a per-run loop would finish no faster than the bit-at-a-time code it replaces. Instead `x = ~w; while x: yprev = x; x &= x >> 1; ++k` leaves k = the longest run’s LENGTH and `yprev` with a bit set at the START of every run of that length, so `TZCNT(yprev)` is the first of them - the exact tie-break the contract requires. **The loop runs k+1 times, NOT once per run**: three iterations per word on that bitmap however many runs it holds. One `POPCNT` skips it entirely when `64 - popcnt(w)` cannot beat the best already found. **MOST OF THE SPEED IS TWO VECTOR SKIPS**, because a live allocation bitmap is nearly FULL and a fresh one is entirely CLEAR: four all-ones words resolve the carry once and four all-zero words extend it by 256, both rejected in one `vpcmpeqq` + mask extract. Measured in order: a call per word 3.633x -> inlining the two trivial shapes 3.776x -> the all-ones skip 4.402x -> the all-zero skip **4.938x**, taking `all set` to **40.46x** and `all clear` from 1.77x to **22.98x**. **THE HAZARD IS NOT THE DECLARED SIZE**: an `RTL_BITMAP` buffer is an array of ULONG, so a 96-bit bitmap occupies TWELVE BYTES and a 64-bit read of the second pair touches four bytes the caller never allocated - so 64-bit reads happen only while two ULONGs remain, and gate 1 tests that against a `PAGE_NOACCESS` page at ODD ULONG counts. Gate 1: **1113911** cases comparing the length AND the written index, including an **EXHAUSTIVE sweep of all 65536 16-bit bitmaps at every declared size**, and tie corpora at every length and gap 1..9 - because a bitmap with a unique longest run cannot test the index at all, and an implementation updating on ">=" would be right on every length and wrong only on ties. The worst shape for this approach (alternating 32-bit blocks, so the inner loop runs 33 times per word) is **measured, not omitted**, at 2.34x. Gate 4: 40000 cases, length and index, restored byte-exact |
| [256](changes/256-rtlfindsetbits/) | `RtlFindSetBits`, `RtlFindClearBits` (find a run of N set/clear bits) | `ntdll.dll!RtlFindSetBits`, `RtlFindClearBits` | **LANDED** - **2.72-2.92x** geomean over four runs (up to **13.08x**), worst class **1.04x**. **THIS CHANGE WAS PARKED AT 0.563x** with a note saying what would be needed to unpark it; this is that. **AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT MOTIVATED IT WAS PUBLISHED WITH THE WRONG EXPLANATION, corrected here (`probes/topbit.c`)**: the survey had the pair FIVE TIMES APART for the same failing full scan (0.132 vs 0.026 ns/byte) and this row used to say that gap "is not a subject artefact ... they are separate code". The measurement reproduces; the reason does not. Both exports skip words with the SAME seven-instruction loop differing by one `not` (`RtlFindClearBits` 0x0D0390, `RtlFindSetBits` 0x1113DF), and both continue skipping WHILE THE SIGN BIT IS SET - and `RtlFindSetBits` inverts the word, so the two loops are driven by OPPOSITE top bits of the same data. 0xA5A5A5A5 has bit 31 set, so every 64-bit word of the survey's subject has bit 63 set. Rotating the pattern one bit to 0x5A5A5A5A **SWAPS the two timings** (SetBits 1000.50 -> 211.00 ns, ClearBits 212.00 -> 923.00) on the same density and the same failing search, while the N>=128 path - which scans for a whole word of the wanted value - does NOT swap, as the control. So neither export is badly written: **both have a fast path of about one cycle per 64-bit word and a slow path of about five, and the top bit of every word decides which one runs** - a data dependence no caller can see, on a search whose answer does not depend on it at all. That makes the target clearer, not smaller. **WHAT REPLACED THE PER-WORD SCAN**: a generic scanner does ~30 instructions per word whatever the data is and the shipped code does 5-7, so tightening it went 0.396x -> 0.563x and no further. It had to stop looking at most of the bitmap. **A run of L consecutive ones contains a COMPLETE ALIGNED BLOCK of B bits whenever L >= 2B-1**, so B is the largest power of two with N >= 2B-1 - pairs for N 3..6, nibbles 7..14, bytes 15..30, words 31..62, dwords 63..126, qwords beyond - and ONE `VPCMPEQ` plus a mask extract rejects 32 bytes. On 0xA5A5A5A5, whose every byte is 10100101, no aligned pair, nibble, byte, word or qword is all ones, so the whole 8 KB falls to 256 vector steps. **AND THE CARRY PROBLEM THAT MADE THIS LOOK HARD DISSOLVES.** The parked note said skipping a chunk is only sound if runs crossing into and out of it are accounted for. It is not. Call the LOWEST all-ones aligned B-block of a run its WITNESS: a run cannot extend B or more bits below its own witness, because the aligned block immediately below would then also be all ones and would be the witness instead. So every qualifying run has a witness and STARTS WITHIN B-1 <= 63 BITS OF IT; witnesses appear in run order, because runs are disjoint; and a skipped region contains no witness, so nothing has to be carried across the skip. No running carry, no per-chunk bookkeeping, no boundary state. **THE REBUILD MEASURES ONE RUN RATHER THAN SEARCHING**: a witness belongs to exactly one run, so it counts ones to the left (never more than 63, one load) and ones to the right - inline, because with the general scanner in that path every row that found its answer immediately measured 0.67x-0.84x while every row that had to scan was already 2x-13x better. The general scanner is still there and still exact, for N below three (a sub-byte witness sits anywhere inside its byte, so there is nothing to measure from), near either edge where the masks apply, and for the last stretch too short for a 32-byte load. Two shapes get their own answer: the run starting exactly where the search does (one load, before any machinery exists), and a long run counted 256 bits at a time by the same compare the filter uses - asking for a thousand set bits of an all-ones bitmap is a SUCCESS that still has to walk a thousand bits, and one word per step left that row at 0.37x. The filter reads the buffer RAW and may say yes when the answer is no, which is safe: masking only ever CLEARS bits, so all-ones-after-masking implies all-ones-before, and false negatives cannot happen. Gate 1: **4138966** cases, 0 mismatches, every corpus sweeping the hint - and the two NEW corpora are the ones that test this version, since the older six top out at 2048-bit maps and N=69 so the qword block is never chosen. **Corpus 7 caught a real bug**: with a sub-byte witness the run need not cover the byte's first bit, and measuring from there reported three set bits at 37 (witness PAIR at 38) as 159. Gate 2 floor is the honest one - `CLR 64, sparse` at **2.09x** is the shipped FAST path, one cycle per 64 bits, while its mirror on the same data is the shipped slow path and goes 9.81x. THREE BENCH ROW LABELS were corrected with the code: `CLR 64, realistic` was labelled NOT FOUND and finds at 1267, `found at 60000` finds at 59999, and the CLR wrap row's hole starts at 99. Gate 3: 81 changes, 0 violations, and this driver arms its sentinels PER CALL (the mechanism change 258 added after showing the whole-thunk form can be masked) - mutation-tested both dynamically and by the static scan. Gate 4: both exports patched separately with their own counters, 30000 cases each, the corpus carrying BOTH 0xA5A5A5A5 and 0x5A5A5A5A so each export is driven down its own fast path and its own slow one |
| [257](changes/257-rtlnumberofsetbits/) | `RtlNumberOfSetBits`, `RtlNumberOfClearBits`, and both `...InRange` forms | `ntdll.dll!RtlNumberOfSetBits`, `RtlNumberOfClearBits`, `RtlNumberOfSetBitsInRange`, `RtlNumberOfClearBitsInRange` | **LANDED** - **2.34-2.53x** geomean over four runs (up to **3.50x**), worst class **1.28x**. Four exports on one core. **THE SHIPPED CODE IS NOT MISSING THE RIGHT INSTRUCTION** - it already uses `popcnt rax, rax` (RVA 0x0F2F33) with a byte table for the ragged ends. At 0.050 ns/byte it is about **two cycles per 64-bit word, which is exactly what a SERIAL ACCUMULATOR CHAIN costs**: POPCNT has ~3 cycles of latency against 1/cycle of throughput, so `total += popcnt(w)` into one register can never beat its own dependency. The room is in removing the chain, and the cheapest way is to leave the general-purpose registers entirely: `vpshufb` a nibble table twice per 32 bytes, `vpaddb` the halves, `vpsadbw` against zero to sum eight byte-counts into each 64-bit lane, `vpaddq` into four INDEPENDENT accumulators. Nine instructions per 32 bytes with no carried dependency. Win64 leaves only ymm0-ymm5 usable and this needs exactly six, so nothing spills. CONTRACT probed: the range is **(start, LENGTH)** not (start,end); the range forms **REFUSE rather than clamp**, returning 0xFFFFFFFF for a zero length or `start+length > SizeOfBitMap` (verified over all **111x111** combinations); and `clear == size - set` EXACTLY over 20000 random bitmaps, which is what lets one core serve all four. A **single-word leaf fast path** (no frame, no saved registers, no call) handles bitmaps of <=64 bits and ranges inside one word - a 1-bit bitmap measured **0.58x** before it existed. **THE BUG THIS CHANGE IS REALLY ABOUT**: the first version PASSED correctness at `/Od` and died with an ACCESS VIOLATION at `/O2` before printing a line. The four stubs pass a selector to a shared framed body, and it was put in **r13 - a NON-VOLATILE register - BEFORE the prologue saved it**, destroying the caller’s r13. /Od spills everything so nothing was live there; /O2 does not. The selector now lives in the VOLATILE r10. **The ABI gate catches it, and that was CHECKED rather than assumed**: rebuilding with the clobber reintroduced reports `ABI: FAILED ... clobbers 1 non-volatile register(s): r13` while the same object faults the correctness harness. (An earlier attempt at that verification appeared to show the gate MISSING it - that was a broken test setup whose text substitution silently did not apply, not a gap in the gate.) `build.bat` now states that the correctness gate must be built OPTIMISED, because building it at /Od would have hidden a real ABI violation behind a compiler that spills everything. Gate 1: **3494554** cases including an **EXHAUSTIVE** sweep of all 65536 16-bit bitmaps x sizes 1..16, **every** (start,length) 0..130 over a 128-bit map, and a `PAGE_NOACCESS` page at ULONG counts 1..41. The four density rows in the bench all cost the same, which is the POINT of having them: counting has no data-dependent branch, so a row that moved with density would mean one of the two implementations had a path nobody had noticed. Gate 4: all four exports patched ONE AT A TIME, 30000 cases each, own counter each, all restored byte-exact |
| [258](changes/258-rtlfindclearruns/) | `RtlFindClearRuns` (up to N runs of clear bits, optionally the longest) | `ntdll.dll!RtlFindClearRuns` | **LANDED** - **3.55-3.67x** geomean over four runs (up to **13.26x**), worst class **1.12x**. The function change 255's target turned out to be a WRAPPER around: `RtlFindLongestRunClear` is nine instructions around `RtlFindClearRuns(bm, buf, 1, TRUE)`. **ONE EXPORT, TWO COSTS, EIGHTY TIMES APART** on one BOOLEAN - 144.83 ns unsorted against 14399.10 sorted on the same bitmap - because unsorted stops the instant the array fills (0.2% of a bitmap with a run every two bits) and sorted cannot: to know the longest it must see them all. They are not one function with a flag, they are two problems, and this is two scans. **THE ORDER RUNS ARE FOUND IN IS NOT THE ORDER THEY OCCUR IN, and the first draft of this change got it wrong.** `probes/contract.c` concluded that unsorted "returns the FIRST runs found, in order"; the first half is right and the second is wrong, and it missed it because every run in that probe sat in a byte of its own - the ONE arrangement where the two orders agree. ntdll scans a byte at a time through four byte tables (`0x17FBD0` trailing zeros, `0x192560` leading zeros, `0x180570` the LONGEST run in a byte, `0x180558` the bits at and above n - each dumped from the live image and checked against its claimed meaning over all 256 byte values): the run carried in from earlier bytes is emitted first, the run at the TOP becomes the carry and is not emitted here at all, and the runs strictly INSIDE the byte are emitted by repeatedly taking the LONGEST, ties to the lowest position. So a byte with a one-bit run at 1 and a two-bit run at 3 reports **(3,2) before (1,1)**, and with room for one run the shipped export returns (3,2) - not (1,1). `probes/enumorder.c` pins that against the live export over **1567328** cases with 0 disagreements. **AND THE SORTED FORM IS UNAFFECTED**, which is not obvious and is proved rather than assumed: sorted output is a STABLE sort of the found order by descending length, so the found order can only show through between runs of EQUAL length - and for equal lengths the two orders coincide (two runs of length L at s1<s2 end at s1+L<s2+L, so they complete in that order of bytes; inside one byte equal-length interior runs are ordered by position, and a carry run starts at or below the byte's first bit while an interior run starts above it). That is what lets the sorted scan work 64 bits at a time while only the unsorted one walks bytes. **SORTED**: per word the low-end run (carry + `TZCNT`) is complete and emitted, the high-end run (`LZCNT`) becomes the carry, the interior runs are enumerated - and three things keep that affordable, two of which replaced code that was already written. Once the array is full only a run LONGER than the shortest kept matters and the minimum only rises, so `x &= x >> k` with k DOUBLING marks every long-enough run in ceil(log2 L) steps (six for a run of 64 where the linear form takes 64) and **the lowest marked bit IS that run's start** - a run [s,e) of length >= L marks s..e-L and nothing below s - so there is nothing to backtrack, which the first version did with an invert, a `BZHI` and an `LZCNT` on the critical path. A run is stripped from the mask in THREE cycles, not eleven: adding the lowest set bit (`blsi`) carries THROUGH the run and stops just past it, so `and` with the original leaves the mask without it, where the obvious tzcnt/shift/invert/tzcnt/shift form is six dependent operations and this loop is latency-bound. And the insertion **walks down from the end**: finding the place from the front costs a pass over the array for a run that belongs at the BACK, and on `0xA5A5A5A5` two thirds of runs are single bits that lose to everything kept - worth **1.4x** at cap 16 on its own. **UNSORTED**: byte at a time because the ORDER is defined byte at a time, but ONE 8-byte table load answers the whole byte - trailing zeros, leading zeros, and its interior runs ALREADY IN EMISSION ORDER - where the shipped code does two lookups, a mask and a shift-until-it-fits loop per interior run; and a block of 0x00 or 0xFF is skipped whole at 64 bits AND at 32, the 32-bit rung alone taking the alternating-ULONG shape from 1.02x to 3.78x. **THE 33-BIT ROW IS WHY THOSE OPTIMISATIONS EXIST**: it started at 0.81x and then straddled the gate AT RANDOM - 0.96x, 0.98x, 1.02x, 1.03x on the same binary - which is a row that fails on the next machine, not a row to document. Splitting the cost apart showed it was NOT fixed overhead (ours is 3.2 ns against the shipped 7.2 on a bitmap with no runs) but growth with the CAPACITY, i.e. the insertion. Gate 1: **558277** cases, whole-array comparison INCLUDING a canary past the returned count, exhaustive over all 65536 16-bit bitmaps x 4 capacities x both forms - the only corpus dense enough to put two runs of different lengths in one byte, and the one that caught the emission order. `SizeOfRunArray = 0` with a run present **faults the shipped export**, so it is excluded from every corpus and ours returns 0 instead. **GATE 3 FOUND THAT THE ABI GATE COULD BE MASKED**: `tools/abi-check` arms sentinels, calls a compiled C thunk, and compares afterwards - but if the compiler used r15 for one of the THUNK's own loop variables, the thunk saved and restored it, undoing an implementation's damage before the comparison. Deleting `push r15` and its `pop` from this impl.asm still reported PASS, and that build's thunk begins with eight pushes including r15. Fixed twice, both mutation-tested: `wia_abi_call4` arms the sentinels around the individual CALL (the gate now reports `clobbers 1 non-volatile register(s): r15`), and `tools/abi-audit.py` - which covered only xmm6-xmm15 - now covers the eight non-volatile GPRs across all **529** .asm files and flags the same mutation. The static scan is file-scoped and does NOT catch ORDERING (change 257's r13 stub bug had every push and pop present), which the dynamic gate does; neither is sufficient alone. Gate 4: both forms patched SEPARATELY with their own counters, 30000 cases each, whole array compared, restored byte-exact |
| [259](changes/259-rtlarebitsset/) | `RtlAreBitsSet`, `RtlAreBitsClear` (is every bit in this range set/clear?) | `ntdll.dll!RtlAreBitsSet`, `RtlAreBitsClear` | **LANDED** - **3.46-3.63x** geomean over six runs (up to **12.32x**), worst class **1.00x**. **THE FIRST BITMAP SURVEY ASKED THIS PAIR A QUESTION THEY COULD ANSWER IMMEDIATELY** and filed them as not worth pursuing: it measured 1.60 ns and 2.20 ns, and **both rows returned 0, meaning NO** - and a range check that answers no stops at the first bit that disagrees, which on those subjects was inside the first word. Those rows timed a two-word function. Asked the EXPENSIVE question, where the answer is YES and every bit therefore has to be examined, `discovery/ntdll_bitmap2.c` gets **745.65 ns / 0.099 ns/byte** and **741.05 / 0.099** - and the middle loop is SIX INSTRUCTIONS PER 32-BIT WORD (`add rdx,4 / mov eax,[rdx] / cmp rdx,rbx / jne / cmp eax,r8d / je`), which at about two cycles per four bytes is exactly the 0.099. **THERE IS NO TRICK TO FIND HERE AND NOTHING SUBTLE TO BEAT**: the shipped code is a correct, tight, word-at-a-time loop. It is reading four bytes at a time and `VPTEST` reads thirty-two - `VPTEST ymm, all-ones` sets CF only if every bit is one and ZF only if every bit is zero, so each form is two instructions and a branch per 32 bytes. **NO ACCUMULATOR**, deliberately: an accumulated AND across several chunks would test less often, but this function has an early exit that matters - NO is its cheap case - so testing per chunk keeps the `NO at bit 0` rows at 1.15x-1.58x instead of turning them into regressions. THE CONTRACT WAS PROBED RATHER THAN READ OUT OF THE BRANCHES, which is the point: every rule is visible in the disassembly, and a rule read out of a branch is a guess about what the branch is for. The second argument is a **LENGTH** not an end index ((100,10) TRUE, (100,109) false); **LENGTH ZERO IS REFUSED** - (0,0) over an all-ones bitmap is FALSE, not the vacuous truth a caller would assume; **a range past `SizeOfBitMap` is REFUSED, not clamped** - over an all-ones buffer declared 100 bits, (0,100) is TRUE and (0,101) false, and the bits out there really are ones so a clamping form would have said TRUE; a start at or past the size is false; and the slack is out of bounds rather than merely unset. **THREE THINGS CAME OUT OF MEASUREMENT AND EACH REPLACED CODE THAT WAS ALREADY CORRECT**: a range of 64 bits or fewer is ONE masked compare of whichever width reaches it (through the general path it was about forty instructions to examine two words, and the short rows STRADDLED THE GATE - 0.88x on one run of a binary and 1.36x on the next); `BZHI` builds the end mask in one instruction instead of four, its "leave the value alone at index 32" behaviour being exactly the whole-word-in-range case; and **a range too short for one vector step never touches a vector register**, because a function that has executed a VEX instruction must VZEROUPPER before returning and on a two-word range that instruction is a measurable part of the whole call - those rows were 0.75x-0.93x with one unconditional VZEROUPPER, so the middle and tail are written twice and only the vector path pays. Reading past the buffer cannot happen: the range is refused unless start+length <= SizeOfBitMap, so the last word touched is inside the ULONG array by construction. Gate 1: **4161304** cases - and since **A PREDICATE HAS ONLY TWO ANSWERS**, an implementation that always said no would agree with the live export on nearly every random range over a random bitmap, so every corpus is built to produce BOTH answers and the harness COUNTS THEM AND FAILS IF EITHER IS ZERO (the live exports said YES 72689 times for SET, 45621 for CLEAR). The corpus that catches a mask one bit too wide is ONE WRONG BIT moved across a long uniform range: the answer must flip exactly when that bit is in range. Gate 2: the two 64-bit CLEAR rows are a **genuine tie at 1.00x** and that is the honest result - a 64-bit range is one masked compare on both sides and there is nothing in eight bytes to vectorise. **AND ONE ASYMMETRY IN AN EARLIER DRAFT WAS NOT WHAT IT LOOKED LIKE**: the table once showed SET 64 Kbit at 65 ns against CLR 64 Kbit at 103 ns for code differing by one branch condition, and timing both forms over ONE SHARED BUFFER gave 66.80 and 69.28 - the same - so the gap belonged to code placement, not to the form, and it disappeared when the short path was restructured. Gate 3: 82 changes, 0 violations; this is a LEAF with no prologue and no saved registers, so it must not touch a non-volatile register at all, and its driver arms per call - mutation-tested by adding a single `mov r15, 1`. Gate 4: both exports patched separately with their own counters, 30000 cases each, three of the five corpus shapes UNIFORM so the answer is YES often enough to prove the loop ran |
| [260](changes/260-rtlcopybitmap/) | `RtlCopyBitMap`, `RtlExtractBitMap` (a bit-granular copy between bitmaps) | `ntdll.dll!RtlCopyBitMap`, `RtlExtractBitMap` | **PARKED** - correct (**59536** cases, 0 mismatches) and **3.36-3.41x** geomean, but **two rows of twenty-one** are 0.90x and 0.94x. **THE LARGEST SINGLE ANOMALY IN THE BITMAP FAMILY, AND IT IS NOT A SEARCH**: `discovery/ntdll_bitmap2.c` timed the same 8 KB copy at **101.05 ns (0.012 ns/byte) for target 0 and 1846.65 ns (0.225) for target 3** - EIGHTEEN TIMES, for three bits of offset. The aligned copy is RtlCopyMemory at memory speed; the shifted one is ~18 instructions per 32-BIT WORD, and the cost is not the shifting but that **the destination word is written, read back and written again** (`mov [r8],eax` / `and r13d,[r8]` / `mov [r8],r13d` at 0x13E468..0x13E484). A shifted copy needs none of that: 32 bits of output are one funnel shift of two adjacent input words, and eight such pairs are three vector instructions (`vpsrld` / `vpslld` / `vpor` over two unaligned loads four bytes apart). **THE CONTRACT WAS PROBED WITH A POISONED DESTINATION**, because these MUTATE and "the copy worked" is indistinguishable from "the copy worked and also cleared the rest of the word" unless the whole buffer is compared: COPY reads the source from bit 0 and writes AT TargetBit while EXTRACT reads AT TargetBit and writes from bit 0 (one core serves both); **`RtlCopyBitMap`'s FOURTH ARGUMENT IS IGNORED** - it is a three-argument function, r9d is overwritten at 0x13E34A before it is ever read, and 0/1/16/0xFFFFFFFF give byte-identical results; the count is `min(src size, dst size - target)`, and **that subtraction is done in 32 bits and tested in 64**, so a target past the destination does NOT refuse - it wraps to a huge count and copies the whole source past the declared size (with a 64-bit destination, target 64 copies nothing and target 65 writes four bytes at byte 8). Reproduced deliberately: an implementation that "fixed" it would not be a replacement. Every bit outside the range is preserved. Gate 1: **59536** cases, whole-destination comparison, a guard page after the SOURCE and after the DESTINATION, and **53720 of them actually wrote something** (the harness counts, since a corpus that copied nothing would prove only that four functions agree about doing nothing). **THE READ BOUND IS THE LARGER OF THE SOURCE ARRAY AND WHAT THE COPY NEEDS** - the corpus caught that, an EXTRACT of 15 bits from bit 18 of a 15-bit source, where the shipped export reads past SizeOfBitMap and KEEPS what it finds, so a bound taken from the declared size substitutes zeros and disagrees. **TWO CORPORA WERE WRONG BEFORE THEY WERE RIGHT, AND BOTH MISTAKES WERE THE HARNESS'S**: the guard-page corpora asked for a wrapped count against a guarded buffer, which faults for the SHIPPED export too; and the randomised corpus reported **2277 "mismatches"** that were a wrapped copy running out of one scratch array into the next, the harness comparing its own damage. Gate 2: the shifted rows are **9.7x-16.1x**, the aligned rows 1.11x-1.54x (four 32-byte moves per iteration: with one it was 0.79x, with two 0.94x), and **the two that fail are copies small enough that the call itself is most of the cost** - `COPY 64 bits at target 3` spans 67 bits, one word past what the frameless short path handles, so it takes the general path and ntdll wins on fixed cost; `EXTRACT 20 bits` takes the short path (4.76 -> 3.37 ns) and is still 6% behind. Extending the short path to three words was tried as a two-pass loop and **measured 7.11 ns against the general path's 5.73** - carrying per-pass state through the shadow slots cost more than the frame it avoided - and was reverted. **What would close it**: a three-word short path that reads the 96-bit source window ONCE and takes two slices of it for two 64-bit masked merges of words (w,w+1) and (w+1,w+2), both entirely inside the copy. That is a design, not a tweak, which is why it is written down rather than attempted a third time **A TGL VARIANT NOW EXISTS** -- [`impl_tgl.asm`](changes/260-rtlcopybitmap/impl_tgl.asm), **2.91x -> 3.43x**, correctness PASS every run, and it is **still PARKED** -- which is the point of recording it. **THE PARENT IS FAR WORSE ON TIGER LAKE THAN ON THE PART IT WAS WRITTEN FOR**: not two rows of twenty-one but EIGHT, including 0.62x on the byte-aligned 64 Kbit copy and 0.60x on its EXTRACT twin. Every losing row is one where the shipped code reaches RtlCopyMemory and every SHIFTED row wins 9x-13x, so the loss was never in the bitmap work -- it was the copy. TWO CAUSES, BOTH MEASURED. (1) **ERMS**: probes/erms.c times the 4x32-byte YMM loop against rep movsb in the three shapes this block sees and the crossover is between 1024 and 2048 bytes in all three (8192 bytes: 128.0 vs 55.0 aligned, 143.6 vs 108.6 byte-aligned, 128.0 vs 89.4 word-aligned), so WIA_ERMS = 2048 is measured, not guessed -- the same effect change 296 measured on this part. That alone gave 3.212x. (2) **A SHIFT THAT IS A WHOLE NUMBER OF BYTES IS NOT A SHIFT**: the two worst rows are `target 8`, which is not zero, so they never reached the unshifted path at all -- but r=8 means dst_byte[k] = src_byte[k+1], a byte copy from a byte offset, and the parent was paying a funnel shift per word to compute a value equal to its own input. Testing `r & 7` cleared both, EXTRACT included. No new source bound was needed: the existing clamp already guarantees rax + 32*ecx <= srcsize - 4 and this reads from rax + r/8 <= rax + 3. **AND A THIRD CAUSE, FOUND BY FIXING THE TOOLING**: after those two it was 3.43x with ONE row still WORSE -- `COPY 64 bits, target 3` at 0.87x-0.90x -- and this was first written up as not worth chasing, because the `20 bits` rows at 0.95x-0.99x would park it anyway. THAT WAS WRONG: those rows are below 1.0 but the bench marks them `~tie`, and the gate counts only `WORSE`, so they never parked anything. Repairing tools/revalidate-variants.ps1 (see below) made the sweep print the regressed set for the first time -- exactly one entry -- and the call was obviously the other way. The frameless `bb_short` path covered a span of 64 bits and a 64-bit copy at target 3 spans 67, missing by three bits; the span now reaches 96 (THREE destination words) while the payload stays at most 64, because sh_have holds the source in one register and it is the span that grew. Two details make it cheap: MASK BEFORE SHIFTING (the parent shifts then masks, and `shl rax, cl` discards the bits that travel past bit 63), and neither mask needs a shift -- once the span passes 64 every bit from cl to 63 is inside the payload, so the low mask is exactly `-1 << cl` and the high one is the low `span - 64` bits, with the third word's value one SHLD into a zeroed register. `COPY 64 bits, target 3` 0.87x -> 1.15x-1.22x. **FIVE RUNS: 3.619x, 3.538x, 3.592x, 3.462x, 3.547x, correctness PASS every run, NO row marked WORSE in any of them -- so this variant LANDS and 260 is unparked on bench #3.** The parent stays the implementation of record on benches #1 and #2. |
| [261](changes/261-rtlfindnextforwardrunclear/) | `RtlFindNextForwardRunClear`, `RtlFindLastBackwardRunClear` (the next run of clear bits, forward or backward from an index) | `ntdll.dll!RtlFindNextForwardRunClear`, `RtlFindLastBackwardRunClear` | **LANDED** - **4.62-4.88x** geomean over six runs (up to **12.17x**), worst class **1.21x**, and **not one class even ties**. The shipped forward scan is SEVEN INSTRUCTIONS PER 32-BIT WORD (`not`/`test`/`jne`/`cmp`/`ja`/`mov`/`add`/`add`/`jmp` at 000DB3B0), four bytes an iteration at about two cycles = the 0.100 ns/byte the survey measured; one `VPCMPEQD` looks at thirty-two. THE CONTRACT IS THE PART A CALLER GETS BACKWARDS: both forms CLIP AT FromIndex IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS - asked from 105 inside a run of 100..119, forward answers start=105 length=15 and backward answers start=100 length=6 - and "nothing found" still WRITES the start pointer, with THREE different values (SizeOfBitMap forward, 0 backward, FromIndex itself when FromIndex is past the end), so an implementation returning the right length and the wrong start passes any test that only reads the return value; length and start are compared everywhere here, poisoned first. **A FAULT IN THE SHIPPED EXPORT, FOUND BY THE GUARD PAGE**: `RtlFindLastBackwardRunClear` tests its starting bit with `bt qword ptr [r9], rax` at 000F65FF - a SIXTY-FOUR-BIT read of the buffer base - so at an ODD ULONG count the last four bytes lie past the array and ntdll faults on a one-word bitmap at the end of a page, while ours and the oracle walk away; the corpus therefore asks the backward form only at even ULONG counts and documents why. The first draft won 7x on every long row and measured **0.59x-0.94x on every short one**, and all of it was structural: (1) the word that hit IS ALREADY IN THE MASK - `VPMOVMSKB` gives four bits per dword and a compare result is uniform per lane, so the mask is eight NIBBLES and `not`+`TZCNT>>2` is the hit index (`LZCNT` backward), where the draft went back to the block base and re-walked up to eight words; (2) THE SCALAR WALK MUST NOT RE-ENTER THE VECTOR LOOP - the draft's scalar step ended in `jmp f_loop`, so every word after a hit rebuilt ymm1, loaded 32 bytes, hit again and VZEROUPPERed again to advance ONE word, which is why a 32-word bitmap measured 11.73 ns against ntdll's 8.80; (3) nothing reloads SizeOfBitMap in a loop - the last word's slack mask is built ONCE in the prologue instead of being rebuilt from a spill on every word of both scans, a six-instruction SERIAL chain that was costing twelve cycles of latency on a call that answers out of the first word; (4) one word is answered by **change 258's carry strip** with no second scan at all - complement, `BLSI` the lowest bit, ADD it back so the carry runs through the run and stops on the first zero above it, so start and end come out of two INDEPENDENT three-instruction chains and THE CARRY OUT is exactly the signal that the run may continue into the next word. **AND THEN THE TWO SMALLEST FORWARD ROWS STILL READ 1.00x, BECAUSE THEY WERE MEASURING THE HARNESS**: three genuine fixes each moved the long rows and left those two flipping between 0.94x and 1.13x ACROSS RUNS OF THE SAME BINARY, so probes/floor.c timed an EMPTY `NTAPI` stub through the same path - 2.32 ns, **eighty per cent of the 2.92 ns measurement**, with the real comparison being 0.60 ns against 0.64. Rows of 32 words or less are now timed as SIXTEEN calls over seven walking FromIndex values (checked to give ONE answer before the row is timed, else it would be timing a mixture), and the same code reads 1.28x-2.20x. Adding the ABI case exposed a hole in gate 3's static half too: `tools/abi-audit.py` passed a file containing a deliberate `mov r12d, 7` because its instruction regex is anchored and this tree writes `f_f1:   mov r12d, 7` - LABEL AND INSTRUCTION ON ONE LINE - so it was blind to every loop head, branch target and early-exit stub in the repository; fixed, and the other **534** .asm files still pass with the blindness removed. 777215 correctness cases (exhaustive over all 65536 16-bit bitmaps x FromIndex, both exports), 30000 live cases per export patched one at a time |
| [262](changes/262-rtlfindsetbitsandclear/) | `RtlFindSetBitsAndClear`, `RtlFindClearBitsAndSet` (find N set/clear bits and mutate them) | `ntdll.dll!RtlFindSetBitsAndClear`, `RtlFindClearBitsAndSet` | **PARKED** - correct (**1747413** cases, 0 mismatches, ANSWER AND WHOLE BUFFER, three-way) and **2.08-2.13x** geomean with fifteen rows of eighteen better (up to **12.24x**), but three are 0.92x, 0.96x and 0.96x. A SEARCH AND A MUTATION IN ONE CALL, and the first of these to write anything. THE SEARCH HALF IS CHANGE 256 AND THAT WAS MEASURED, NOT INHERITED: probes/equiv.c asked `RtlFindSetBitsAndClear(bm,N,hint) == RtlFindSetBits(bm,N,hint)` (and the clear-side mirror) over a planted-run sweep plus 240000 randomised and 40000 wrapped calls - ZERO disagreements, 227509 found / 241435 not found / 14336 at N=0, all three arms exercised rather than merely available, read-only asked FIRST on an untouched copy since the mutating one destroys the evidence. Inheriting a rule because it looks like the same rule is how the eight-change SPACE bug happened, so it was tested the way change 237 tested its relationship to 236. THE MUTATION, probed by diffing the WHOLE buffer and printing the changed bits as runs: EXACTLY N bits are written at the returned index, NOT the whole run found (asking 8 inside a run of 20 at bit 40 clears 40..47, which is why a second call answers 48 and a third answers NOT FOUND); not-found writes NOTHING anywhere; N=0 returns an index (the hint rounded down to a multiple of eight) and ALSO writes nothing, the case an implementation is most likely to corrupt with a loop that runs at least once; the wrapped answer mutates too. EVERY BENCH ROW HAD TO BE BUILT TO RESTORE ITSELF, because a harness that calls a mutating function fifty thousand times measures a subject that changed underneath it - and restoring inside the op is NOT the fix (change 142's memcpy restore REPLACED its measurement, and change 261 showed what a constant shared by both sides does to a ratio). So rows are either NOT FOUND (writes nothing by contract, perfectly repeatable, and the survey's own subject) or a PAIR THAT IS ITS OWN INVERSE (over an all-ones bitmap, and-clear then clear-and-set returns it bit-identical while both halves do a real search AND a real mutation) - and the subject table VERIFIES both properties before timing rather than asserting them. WHY THE THREE ROWS LOSE, measured apart in probes/split.c rather than guessed: our wrapper is a FLAT 0.79 ns (frame, call, return, parameter shuffle) that does not grow with the bitmap, but change 256's search has a FIXED COST OF ABOUT 7 ns, nearly flat from 128 bits to 1 Kbit, because its aligned-block filter has a setup a small bitmap cannot amortise - its per-byte rate is superb (0.019 ns/byte at 8 Kbit) and it wins 2-12x from 512 bits up, but below that it merely TIES the shipped code, and 0.79 ns on top of a tie is a loss. That is why the 64-BIT FAST PATH exists and is the part already fixed: 64 bits or fewer is answered in ONE 64-BIT REGISTER with no call at all - change 258's doubling run-mark, BZHI to terminate runs at SizeOfBitMap, SHRX plus two TZCNTs for "scan from the hint then from the beginning" - and that row went from **0.71x to 1.42x** (its all-ones pair 1.08x to 1.88x). Its 64-bit value is built from TWO 32-BIT LOADS, because change 261 caught the shipped backward scan faulting on exactly that overread. The third failing row is a CLIFF AT N=65, and splitting it against the same search READ-ONLY shows MOST OF IT IS THE SEARCH: our search alone goes 2.43 ns at N<=64 to 5.03 at N=128 (+2.6), which is change 256's code at the point where its aligned filter block doubles, while the self-inverting pair moves +4.7 ns per call - so ~2.6 is the search and ~2.1 is the mutation and wrapper.
