WHAT THAT 2.1 IS HAS NOT BEEN ESTABLISHED and is recorded as an open question: the leading candidate is a store-forwarding stall between the fill's narrow stores and the 32-byte vector loads the NEXT call reads them back with (the shape change 142 hit from the other direction, where ntdll's scalar `mov r10d,[rax+4]` loop forwards cheaply). An earlier draft asserted that as the cause of the WHOLE cliff and credited the search with none of it; the read-only column is what showed that to be wrong, the same way change 256's own published explanation of its 5x gap had to be corrected in five files. 30000 live cases per export comparing the answer AND the resulting bitmap; the live run's census caught its own corpus asking N=0 only 33 times in 30000 and it is now asked deliberately (1941) |
| [263](changes/263-rtlcompareunicodestrings/) | `RtlCompareUnicodeStrings` (raw-pointer compare, optional case fold) | `ntdll.dll!RtlCompareUnicodeStrings` | **LANDED** - **5.21-5.63x** geomean over five runs (up to **16.03x**), worst class **1.26x**, all 20 classes BETTER. The best per-byte cost left in ntdll that was not already landed (0.076 ns/byte equal, 0.102 case-insensitive). TWO QUESTIONS WERE SETTLED BEFORE ANY CODE WAS WRITTEN, either of which going the other way would have ended the change: (1) it is a DISTINCT export from the landed singular `RtlCompareUnicodeString` - in the same survey `RtlInitAnsiString` looked like a target and is THE SAME ADDRESS as `RtlInitString`, which change 095 landed long ago, so addresses are compared and not names; (2) the case-insensitive flag is EXACTLY `RtlUpcaseUnicodeChar` and not linguistic - over a dense sweep 66462 pairs compared equal and NOT ONE was a pair the table disagreed about in either direction, where discovery/lstrcmp_is_linguistic.c and strcmpn_is_linguistic.c both ABANDONED their targets on this same question. That probe's first run reported EVERY CHARACTER AS DIFFERENT FROM ITSELF because it passed 2 for the length of one character - THE LENGTHS ARE IN CHARACTERS. THE CONTRACT: the return is the DIFFERENCE, not a sign (`A` vs `Z` = -25, U+FFFF vs U+0000 = 65535, U+0000 vs U+FFFF = -65535), an equal common prefix answers len1-len2 IN CHARACTERS ("abc" vs "abcdef" = -3, while a difference inside the common part wins: "abz" vs "abcd" = 23), case-insensitive returns the UPCASED difference (`a` vs `B` = -1 = A-B, not the raw 31, so the fold happens BEFORE the subtraction), and a NULL pointer at length 0 is never read. METHOD: the RAW characters are compared first ALWAYS, with or without the flag, because two equal strings are almost always equal exactly and VPCMPEQW settles sixteen characters at once - the fold is computed only on a block that disagrees (change 236's shape). That leaves the case where strings differ ONLY in case and every block must fold, so the fold has an in-vector form, and probes/fold.c measured exactly where that is legal: the ASCII quarter is EXACTLY a-z->A-Z with 0 disagreements, while outside it only 947 characters of 65408 fold at all and by SEVEN different offsets - so an all-ASCII block folds with two compares and a masked subtract and anything else goes through the table. The all-ASCII test must be an UNSIGNED compare (U+FFFF is an ordinary character and as a signed word is -1, which would read as "below 0x80" and take the fast fold it cannot reproduce). The upcase table is change 210's, built at RUN TIME from the OS, so there is one place where this project decides what the fold is. **THE FIRST DRAFT SHIPPED A REAL ABI BUG AND THE SYMPTOM WAS A BENCHMARK PRINTING 0.00 ns**: it parked constants in ymm4-ymm7, and the LOW 128 BITS OF xmm6-xmm15 ARE NON-VOLATILE, so it destroyed two of the caller's registers - correctness passed all 174775 cases, and time appeared to vanish only because the compiler had a `double` live in xmm6 and the number being FORMATTED had been overwritten while the tick counts were right. abi_probe.asm's own header records the identical failure from change 202 thirty changes earlier, and the gate built for it caught this one; re-introducing one `vmovdqu ymm6` makes both the dynamic gate and the static audit flag it. Its five-argument arming helper (`wia_abi_call5`) is written in ASSEMBLY rather than as a C wrapper on purpose - a compiled adapter would save and restore xmm6 itself and the gate would have reported PASS on a function corrupting its caller. One row regressed at 0.80x and the cause was NOT work but BRANCH SHAPE: the case-insensitive scalar tail took two taken branches per character where the case-sensitive one takes one; letting the equal path fall through took it to 1.26x, landing exactly on the case-sensitive row's per-character cost. 174775 correctness cases (all 128x128 ordered ASCII pairs enumerated, plus a guard page with both strings ENDING at an inaccessible page), 40000 live cases |
| [264](changes/264-rtlinitutf8string/) | `RtlInitUTF8String` (strlen + UTF8_STRING fill) | `ntdll.dll!RtlInitUTF8String` | **LANDED** - **3.15-3.30x** geomean over five runs (up to **7.16x**), worst class **1.50x**, all 12 classes BETTER. IT IS `RtlInitString` AT A SECOND ADDRESS, AND THAT WAS MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED. The name says UTF-8, and a function that validated its input, rejected an overlong encoding, counted CHARACTERS rather than bytes or refused a lone continuation byte would produce a struct identical to RtlInitString's on ASCII and different on exactly the inputs a lazy corpus never contains - so probes/equiv.c ENUMERATES instead of sampling: every byte 0x01..0xFF and EVERY ORDERED PAIR (65280 cases, which is every lead/continuation combination, every overlong prefix and every truncated sequence there is), every length 0..300, every length 65400..65700 across the USHORT clamp, and 60000 randomised over ASCII, high-bit, valid UTF-8 and deliberately malformed UTF-8. **125883 cases, 0 differences** on all three fields against a poisoned struct: it counts BYTES, validates NOTHING, clamps Length at 65534 and MaximumLength at 65535, and zeroes all three fields for a NULL source. Note that `RtlInitAnsiString`, which appeared in the same survey row, is not merely equivalent to RtlInitString but IS it (one shared address, covered by change 095 since long ago) - this export is a genuinely different address, which is why it needs code at all. **SO IT IS AN ALIAS, NOT A COPY**: change 095 already ships the page-safe AVX2 strlen and struct fill, and pasting eighty lines of it here would create a second copy that a future correction to 095 would silently leave behind - precisely how change 132's extension rule ended up wrong in changes 140, 143, 144 and 158 simultaneously. impl.asm is a MASM `ALIAS` directive, so the symbol resolves to 095's code IN THE LINKER: one implementation, no duplication, not even a jump between them. THE GATES ARE NOT SHARED, which is what makes this a change rather than a footnote - correctness, the benchmark and the live substitution all run against the LIVE RtlInitUTF8String at its own address, so what is proved is that this code is bit-exact and faster for THIS function and not for a different one. The bench keeps a row PAST THE CLAMP because it is the one length where the answer stops depending on the string (70000 bytes scanned, 65534 reported): an implementation that stopped SCANNING there would be faster and wrong. 186384 correctness cases including a guard page with the string ending at an inaccessible page at every alignment; 40000 live cases with the clamp and the NULL source both reached and counted |
| [265](changes/265-rtlappendasciiztostring/) | `RtlAppendAsciizToString` (inline AVX2 strlen + SIMD copy, no calls) | `ntdll.dll!RtlAppendAsciizToString` | **LANDED** - **2.43-2.50x** geomean over five runs (up to **6.99x**), worst class **1.46x**, all 14 classes BETTER. Found by discovery/ntdll_rtl_uncovered2.c, a SECOND sweep written because everything the first one found is now landed: it went after fills, appends, parses and single-value lookups, and this export stood out at **0.056 ns/byte against 0.012 for its own siblings in the same run** (RtlAppendUnicodeStringToString and RtlCopyString) - the signature of a function that calls into strlen and then copies at a rate its neighbours beat fourfold. The same sweep recorded that `RtlFillMemoryUlong` and `RtlFillMemoryUlonglong` are NOT ntdll exports at all (kernel-mode), printed rather than skipped, because "not there" and "not worth converting" are different findings. **THE CONTRACT DISAGREES WITH ITS OWN WIDE SIBLING ON THE RULE A REIMPLEMENTATION WOULD MOST LIKELY COPY**: change 101's RtlAppendUnicodeToString appends a NUL when MaximumLength leaves room, and THIS FORM NEVER WRITES A TERMINATOR AT ANY SIZE - appending "abc" to a 3-byte STRING with MaximumLength 8 leaves bytes 6 and 7 exactly as they were, so an implementation that helpfully terminated would corrupt a caller's buffer on every successful call while passing any test that looked only at the status and the appended bytes. It fits iff Length + strlen(src) <= MaximumLength with NO allowance for a terminator (3+3 into 6 SUCCEEDS), and THAT SUM IS COMPUTED WIDE: Length 40000 with a 30000-byte source is REFUSED, where a 16-bit comparison wraps to 4464, concludes it fits, and overruns the buffer - a memory-safety bug, not a wrong answer. NULL and empty sources succeed changing nothing; on STATUS_BUFFER_TOO_SMALL nothing at all is touched, which is WHY the source is read TWICE (the length must be known before a byte is written, so the scan cannot be fused into the copy - two passes are the contract, not an oversight). Correctness compares THE WHOLE DESTINATION BUFFER against a poison fill for exactly that reason. Two bugs were caught and both were structural: the inlined scan uses edx as a scratch so the SOURCE POINTER did not survive it (the copy read from a clobbered register - now parked in the caller's shadow space, since this is a leaf with no frame), and the sub-32-byte copy was a BYTE LOOP that made the **16-byte row 0.63x - slower than the shipped code AND slower than this implementation's own 32-byte row**; a copy that costs more for less input is the wrong shape, and a ladder of overlapping pairs (first k and last k bytes, k=16/8/4/2, every byte read strictly inside the string) took it to 1.47x. The REFUSAL rows are not filler - refusing is the cheap answer this function exists to give quickly, and at 4000 bytes it is 6.99x because the shipped code still walks the source before deciding. 144948 correctness cases with the fit boundary ENUMERATED and the source against a guard page; 40000 live cases, one in three built not to fit |
| [266](changes/266-rtliszeromemory/) | `RtlIsZeroMemory` (is this buffer entirely zero) | `ntdll.dll!RtlIsZeroMemory` | **LANDED** - **3.37-3.91x** geomean over six runs (up to **12.38x**), worst class **1.02x**. Found by discovery/ntdll_bitmap3.c at **0.025 ns/byte** (1618 ns for 64 KB, ~40 GB/s) where change 259 measured a VPTEST scan of the same shape at 125 GB/s. That sweep came from enumerating ntdll's 2516 exports against this project's manifest - 154 uncovered names are plausibly byte-wise - and it ALSO established that the bitmap range mutators are NOT worth converting (RtlSetBits/RtlClearBits are already at 0.007 ns/byte, which is memory bandwidth), a finding worth as much as a target because the alternative is measuring them again in six months. CONTRACT: TRUE iff every byte is zero; length zero is TRUE and THE POINTER IS NEVER READ (NULL with length 0 answers TRUE rather than faulting); the length is respected exactly (a byte one past the end is never seen, a byte at the LAST position always is); it does not read past the length (verified at every length 0..300 with the buffer ending EXACTLY at an inaccessible page); and **IT STOPS AT THE FIRST NON-ZERO BYTE** - one megabyte costs 1.55 ns with the non-zero byte first and 51424 ns with it last, so the early exit is behaviour and not an accident of the buffer. Method: VPTEST ymm,ymm sets ZF iff every bit is zero; four loads are OR-ed and tested ONCE per 128 bytes, the tail is an OVERLAPPING final vector (the last 32 bytes of the range, never the next 32 after the cursor), and anything below 32 bytes never touches a vector register at all - both for page safety and because change 259 measured VZEROUPPER as a visible part of a call with a few bytes to look at. **TWO BUGS, BOTH STRUCTURAL, BOTH CAUGHT BY A CORPUS BUILT TO CATCH THEM**: (1) the sub-32 ladder did not cover its own range - one overlapping pair of k-byte reads only covers n bytes when 2k >= n, so at n=17 BYTE 8 WAS IN NEITHER HALF, and the corpus that walks a single non-zero byte through EVERY position of EVERY length found it at once with 250 mismatches, all lengths 17..31, every one of them calling a non-zero buffer zero (that corpus is quadratic on purpose, and this is what it is for); (2) the early exit read 128 bytes before it could answer, so the "non-zero at byte 0" row measured **0.90x - slower than the shipped code at the one thing it is fastest at** - fixed by testing the first 32 bytes ALONE before any block loop, at a cost of one redundant load per call, and the row went to 1.23x. The bench does NOT call wia_bench_compare, deliberately: that helper times every row from one prepared state and here THE STATE IS THE SUBJECT, so each row plants its own byte immediately before being timed. REPETITION IS CHOSEN BY HOW MUCH WORK A CALL DOES, not by buffer size - the early-exit rows answer instantly whatever their length and read 1.00x-1.02x until they were repeated x16 like the genuinely short rows. The 1-byte row is a GENUINE TIE at 1.02x across six runs and stays one: a one-byte call is a branch chain and a return on both sides. 259314 correctness cases; 40000 live cases with the non-zero byte at every position including the last |
| [267](changes/267-rtlcrc32/) | `RtlCrc32` (CRC-32C; three parallel hardware CRC32 chains, recombined over GF(2); **RE'd**) | `ntdll.dll!RtlCrc32` | **LANDED** - **1.84-1.89x** geomean over five runs (up to **3.03x**), worst class **1.21x**, all 20 classes BETTER, and **36.75 GB/s at a megabyte against ntdll's 14.2**. The worst per-byte cost found anywhere in ntdll during discovery/ntdll_bitmap3.c (0.071 ns/byte). **WHICH CRC IT IS WAS DERIVED, NOT GUESSED** (probes/identify.c): the check value of "123456789" is E3069283, which is CRC-32C's (Castagnoli, reflected 0x82F63B78) and NOT zlib's CBF43926 - but an EMPTY buffer returns the third argument COMPLETELY UNCHANGED, which an init and xorout of 0xFFFFFFFF cannot do. Both are true at once iff the accumulator is complemented on the way IN and again on the way OUT: `RtlCrc32(p,n,X) == ~CRC32C_raw(p,n,~X)`, confirmed over 5000 random buffers and initial values against a from-scratch bitwise CRC with ZERO disagreements. It is the same shape change 076 found for RtlCrc64, so the family is consistent - measured here rather than assumed. The third argument is a genuine running CRC (chaining two calls equals one call, over 200 random splits), and THE SINGLE-BYTE PROBE IS NOT ENOUGH ON ITS OWN once the accumulator is complemented at both ends, which the probe now says of its own earlier heading. **WHY IT IS SLOW**: 0x82F63B78 is the polynomial the SSE4.2 CRC32 INSTRUCTION implements in hardware, so the shipped export is not using a table - it is using the instruction SERIALLY, and CRC32 has 3-cycle latency with 1-per-cycle throughput, so a serial chain runs at eight bytes per THREE cycles (~13 GB/s at this clock; the survey measured 14.2). The instruction is not the bottleneck, the DEPENDENCY CHAIN through it is. So the buffer is split into THREE independent chains, which saturates the unit at eight bytes per cycle. Splitting is trivial; RECOMBINING is the arithmetic: a CRC is linear over GF(2), so CRC(A||B||C) = shift_L(shift_L(crcA) ^ crcB) ^ crcC where shift_L advances a CRC past L zero bytes - a fixed linear map, tabulated once per L in shifttab.c as four 256-entry tables (four loads and three XORs, twice per 3L bytes). **THE TABLES ARE BUILT FROM THE POLYNOMIAL AT RUN TIME AND THEN CHECKED AGAINST THE DEFINITION THEY ARE SUPPOSED TO SATISFY** - they are the one piece here that cannot be seen to be right by reading it, the first draft of the construction WAS wrong, and a transcribed table would be a second copy of a constant nobody can verify (the same reason change 210 builds its upcase table from the OS). TWO BLOCK SIZES, because one leaves a hole: L=1024 needs 3072 bytes before it can be used at all, so a second pass at L=64 covers everything from 192 bytes up, and the measurements show the boundary exactly - **3072 bytes is 2.77x and 3071 bytes is 1.49x**. Below 192 both implementations are a serial chain and there is nothing to win, so that path is a LEAF with no frame at all and the framed body is tail-jumped. 36.75 GB/s is the CEILING for this approach and not a shortfall: three chains at one CRC32 per cycle is eight bytes per cycle, and a fourth chain would add nothing. 72395 correctness cases against a BITWISE oracle (every length 0..4000 from zero AND from a non-zero init, since the initial CRC enters only the FIRST of the three chains; chaining at 14x14 splits either side of every block boundary; a guard page at every length); 30000 live cases, 25714 of them with a non-zero initial CRC |
| [268](changes/268-rtlunicodestringtoutf8string/) | `RtlUnicodeStringToUTF8String` + `RtlUTF8StringToUnicodeString` (wrappers over changes 016 and 034) | `ntdll.dll!RtlUnicodeStringToUTF8String`, `RtlUTF8StringToUnicodeString` | **LANDED** — geomean **3.53×** over 14 rows, worst row **2.05×**. TWO EXPORTS, ONE CHANGE, because the content of the change is the FOUR THINGS THE TWO DIRECTIONS DO DIFFERENTLY — a first draft assumed they mirrored each other and failed its own gate on 32784 of 84434 cases with the status, Length AND MaximumLength matching live on every one of them, so the only field left was the buffer. (1) On a shortfall UTF-16→UTF-8 PARTIALLY FILLS the destination while UTF-8→UTF-16 WRITES NOTHING at every capacity — which is the architecture of the file, because the first direction can hand the caller's buffer to the N-form and let it write what fits and the second cannot. (2) The first passes STATUS_SOME_NOT_MAPPED through and the second SWALLOWS it even with the U+FFFD plainly in the buffer. (3) Capacity 0 is BUFFER_OVERFLOW in one direction and every shortfall is BUFFER_OVERFLOW in the other. (4) One byte of terminator room going out, two coming back. **AND A LIMIT THAT IS NOT A SHORTFALL**: Length is a USHORT and a conversion can exceed it, which the shipped code answers with STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER_2 (0xC00000F0) — and that BEATS both shortfall codes, so the size test comes first; letting the field wrap would allocate a small block and convert a large string into it. The probe that measured it got it wrong first, building a 65535-byte case out of 32768 characters whose own Length field wrapped to zero. **THE WIN IS STRUCTURAL**: contract.c showed the shipped wrapper adds 271 ns to a 508 ns conversion, which is a SECOND PASS over the input, and both directions now skip it on a BOUND rather than a measurement — three bytes per character going out, one UTF-16 word per byte coming back — so a destination with room for 2N+2 makes the conversion unable to fail and therefore unable to leave a partial write. The allocating path always needs two passes (the buffer does not exist until the size does) and allocates the same process-heap block the paired RtlFreeUTF8String accepts, which alloc.c proved in both directions before a line was written. **ITS GATE FOUND A DEFECT IN BOTH DEPENDENCIES**: comparing the WHOLE destination, not just the produced length, caught change 016 storing sixteen bytes and advancing by fewer — 154 mismatches, every one a single 00 where ntdll left the caller's fill. 92634 correctness cases, 40000 live cases with both exports patched at once and every allocated block freed by the UNPATCHED free routine, 11 mutants with the one unobservable guard documented rather than pretended away |
| [269](changes/269-convertstringsidtosid/) | `ConvertStringSidToSidW` (the SDDL string-to-SID parser; two number parsers, two OS-derived tables) | `advapi32.dll!ConvertStringSidToSidW` | **LANDED** — geomean **5.12×** over 14 rows, worst row **2.85×**, up to **44.3×**. discovery/sid_inet_bstr.c measured the shipped export at 275.78 ns for a five-sub-authority SID and 463.28 ns for eight — about **45 NANOSECONDS PER DECIMAL NUMBER**, against the single-figure nanoseconds change 114 takes for the four numbers of an IPv4 address. **THE CONTRACT WAS MEASURED FIELD BY FIELD, NOT READ**, and six of the eight answers contradict a careful reading of the documentation. (1) **THERE ARE TWO NUMBER PARSERS**: the revision and the identifier authority skip leading whitespace and take a single leading `+`, a sub-authority takes neither — found because a model built from the first three probes disagreed with live on 2822 of 36508 cases, every one of them `S-0-+1-0`. (2) **AND THE DIGIT SETS DIFFER**: the lenient parser accepts 180 code units (the whole Unicode decimal-digit set) and 25 whitespace ones, the strict parser 20 (ASCII and fullwidth only) — built at run time by classify.c with TWO probes per code unit, because a leading ZERO and a skipped SPACE leave the same number behind and the one-probe version classified the digit `0` as whitespace. (3) **THE BASE CARRIES ACROSS FIELDS**: a `0x` on the REVISION makes every later field hexadecimal and there the prefix becomes optional, so `18` is 24 in `S-0x0-0-18` — a parser carrying state, not a grammar, and the last 1420 disagreements were all of that shape. (4) A sub-authority **SATURATES** at 0xFFFFFFFF while the authority **REFUSES** above 48 bits and the revision above 255; making the three consistent is the reimplementation's mistake. (5) The count stops at **254** (8+4*254 = 1024) and the refusal there is **ERROR_ARITHMETIC_OVERFLOW**, not ERROR_INVALID_SID. (6) **THREE CHARACTERS OUT OF 65535 MAKE A FAILING CALL WRITE THE OUTPUT POINTER**: `)`, `,` and `;` after a COMPLETE SID fail with ERROR_INVALID_SID and set the pointer to NULL, because they are the SDDL ACE terminators and the parser underneath has a mode that stops at them — found by sweeping every trailing code unit, with the follow-up probe establishing that nothing is allocated and so nothing leaks. **THE ALIAS TABLE CANNOT BE A CONSTANT**: 66 two-letter aliases answer, and a third of them (`LA`, `DA`) resolve through THIS MACHINE or THIS DOMAIN, so aliases.c builds the table by asking the export over printable ASCII — and its round-trip self-check caught the first size, 255, being too small, since the aliases match case-insensitively and the table needs **264** entries. **THE BENCH CAUGHT ITSELF**: the row named "Unicode digits" used a THAI sub-authority, which is in the lenient table and not the strict one, so it was a REFUSAL timed at 8.5 ns and reported 29.99× while measuring the refusal path under another row's name — change 210's defect exactly. It is now two correctly-named rows and the bench runs a PRE-FLIGHT that prints what every row reaches and refuses to benchmark a row whose outcome disagrees with a per-row table. 429776 correctness cases (including every code unit 1..0xFFFF leading and trailing in all three fields, which is what found the terminators), 0 mismatches; 40000 live cases with every allocated SID freed by the UNPATCHED LocalFree and all **264 distinct aliases** reached — the first live corpus sampled the 9025 pairs at random, reached 104, and said so; 4 correctness mutants and 2 ABI mutants, all caught. **AMENDED BY CHANGE 272**: a successful call ZEROES the last error — all four exports of this family do (272's probes/lasterror.c asks each from six starting values) — and this implementation did not, while its gate agreed with live over 429776 cases because BOTH HALVES OF THE COMPARISON WERE BLIND: it wrote `SetLastError(0)` before each call, from which "left untouched" and "set to zero" read identically, AND it compared the last error only on FAILING calls (`!ra && (ea != eb ...)`), so the success path was never looked at. Both had to be wrong for the defect to survive and both were — the same class as change 067's corpus stepping MaximumLength by two: not a weak test, an ABSENT one. Fixed on both success paths, in the model, in the gate (non-zero sentinel AND compared on every call) and in the live harness; re-run 429776 cases 0 mismatches, geomean 5.21×, and with the fix reverted the amended gate reports the mismatch on the first alias in the corpus |
| [270](changes/270-convertsidtostringsid/) | `ConvertSidToStringSidW` (the SDDL SID-to-string formatter; an ENVELOPE over change 067) | `advapi32.dll!ConvertSidToStringSidW` | **LANDED** — geomean **2.52×** over 10 rows, worst row 1.93×. discovery/sid_inet_bstr.c measured the shipped export at 181.45 ns against 73.73 ns for ntdll!RtlConvertSidToUnicodeString producing THE SAME STRING and 40.41 ns for the LocalAlloc/LocalFree pair the contract requires — so ~67 ns of the 181 was neither the formatting nor the allocation. **IT IS AN ENVELOPE, AND THAT WAS MEASURED BEFORE IT WAS ASSUMED**: probes/contract.c formats every shape of SID with BOTH exports and compares the strings byte for byte — the ordinary counts, every revision, every sub-authority count 0..255, the identifier authority at every decimal and hex boundary — and they agree on all of it INCLUDING ON WHAT THEY REFUSE. Change 268 made exactly this bet about two functions documented as a pair and found four behaviours where they differ, so the probe exists rather than the assumption. The formatter is therefore change 067, LINKED not copied (the 132 lesson: a pasted copy is what a future correction silently leaves behind). **WHICH IS WHY 067 WAS REBUILT FIRST** — a wrapper can be neither faster nor more correct than what it wraps, and 067 was formatting with a division per digit, would have overrun its own stack on a count above 15, and had the room rule wrong; that rebuild is the larger half of this change. **WHAT THE ENVELOPE OWNS**, all measured: (1) the failure codes are Win32 and there are only two — ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER for a NULL argument, ERROR_INVALID_SID for everything the formatter refuses; (2) on failure THE OUTPUT POINTER IS LEFT ALONE, not cleared — its sibling ConvertStringSidToSidW (change 269) DOES clear it for three characters out of 65535, and the two were measured separately rather than assumed to match; (3) on success THE LAST ERROR BECOMES ZERO from any starting value (five values at four lengths, 20 of 20), set explicitly rather than left to whatever LocalAlloc leaves behind; (4) the block is LocalAlloc(LMEM_FIXED) of EXACTLY (characters+1)*2 bytes with LocalFlags 0, CALLED not imitated because the CALLER frees it. And one rule inherited for free: a short SID is a REFUSAL when its sub-authority array runs off the end and a FAULT when only its six identifier-authority bytes do — probes/reads.c and probes/validate.c DISAGREED about that, and probes/truncated.c exists because the disagreement was worth settling rather than averaging (reads.c had been filling the SID through a helper that wrote eight bytes regardless, so one of its rows was measuring ITS OWN FILL faulting). 201500 correctness cases three-way against the scalar model and live advapi32 on the BOOL, the last error, the output pointer, LocalSize, LocalFlags AND every block byte, with the count swept 0..255 and a guard-page sweep, 0 mismatches; 40000 live cases with every one of the 32337 blocks freed by the UNPATCHED LocalFree; 6 mutants, all caught. The ~50 ns floor on the shortest row is the allocation pair both sides pay, which is why this tops out lower than the 4.06× change 067 reaches underneath it |
| [271](changes/271-convertsidtostringsida/) | `ConvertSidToStringSidA` (the ANSI form; VPACKUSWB narrowing, no code page) | `advapi32.dll!ConvertSidToStringSidA` | **LANDED** — geomean **3.37×** over 10 rows, worst row 2.05×, up to **4.73×**. discovery/sid_inet_bstr.c measured the shipped export at 254.59 ns against 181.45 ns for the wide form — a 73 ns gap, about what a code-page conversion of a 44-character string costs. **THE ANSI FORM IS THE WIDE FORM NARROWED ONE BYTE PER CHARACTER, AND THAT WAS MEASURED**: probes/contract.c asks both exports the same question over every sub-authority count 0..255, every revision 0..255 and the identifier authority at every decimal and hex boundary at six counts, comparing the characters, the refusals, GetLastError, LocalSize, LocalFlags and the fate of the output pointer — ZERO DIFFERENCES. This project has been caught three times by functions documented as a pair that do not behave as one (268's four differences between its directions, 269's TWO number parsers inside ONE export, 018/021's code-page-dependent ANSI forms), so the probe exists rather than the assumption. **AND IT ASKS ABOUT THE CODE PAGE EXPLICITLY**: a SID string is S, -, x, the digits and A-F, all below 0x80, so a code page "should not" matter — but "should not" is what changes 021 and 027 were built on before theirs were measured. Repeated under four thread locales including Shift-JIS and UTF-8: still zero. So there is no code page in impl.asm; the narrowing is a saturating VPACKUSWB, sixteen characters an iteration with an OVERLAPPING final sixteen (the block is exactly characters+1 bytes) and a byte loop below sixteen. Because it saturates, a character at or above 0x100 would come back as 0xFF — which is why both gates compare the BYTES of the block rather than its length and status. Three changes are LINKED and none copied: change 067's formatter, change 270's alloc.c, and only the pack is new. 201956 correctness cases three-way against the scalar model and live advapi32, 0 mismatches — including a corpus of its own that drives the RESULT LENGTH across every 16-character pack boundary, because the pack changes behaviour at every multiple of sixteen and a corpus of realistic SIDs walks straight past most of them; 40000 live cases with every block freed by the UNPATCHED LocalFree, and the harness's length threshold had to be RE-DERIVED not copied (the block is one byte per character, so the wide harness's `> 200` would never have been met and the class it guards would have gone unreported); 7 mutants, all caught. **Every row beats the wide form's ratio**: our ANSI path costs 0.5-2 ns more than our wide path, while the shipped ANSI export costs 70-130 ns more than its wide sibling |
| [272](changes/272-convertstringsidtosida/) | `ConvertStringSidToSidA` (the ANSI parser; a proved-ASCII zero-extension over change 269, MultiByteToWideChar only when needed) | `advapi32.dll!ConvertStringSidToSidA` | **LANDED** — geomean **5.71×** over 12 rows, worst row 3.56×, up to **36×**. The shipped export is 343.95 ns against 269.14 ns for the wide form; probes/asciilen.c puts the gap at 70.90 ns of which MultiByteToWideChar itself is only 24.70 ns. **THE ANSI FORM IS A WIDENING AND THEN THE WIDE PARSER, MEASURED**: probes/codepage.c asks both exports the same question — the wide one given MultiByteToWideChar(CP_ACP) of the same bytes — over every byte 0x01..0xFF leading and trailing in all three fields (1530), every printable ASCII pair against the alias table (9025), the sequences that may not translate at all, and the SDDL terminators that make a FAILING call clear the pointer: 0 differ throughout. **THIS IS THE OPPOSITE SITUATION TO CHANGE 271.** There the OUTPUT is ASCII by construction; here the INPUT is arbitrary caller bytes, and change 269 established the wide parser accepts 180 code units as decimal digits and 25 as whitespace — 0xA0 is U+00A0 NO-BREAK SPACE under 1252, which that parser SKIPS. So what a high byte means is genuinely a code-page question. But a byte BELOW 0x80 might not be, and probes/asciilen.c asked: is every byte 0x00..0x7F the same code point under every code page Windows can use as the ACP? **All 22 of them**, including the four DBCS pages and 65001 — ZERO counterexamples. So pure-ASCII input is widened by VPMOVZXBW with no code page consulted, and anything else calls the real thing. **THE SCAN DOES BOTH IN ONE PASS**: VPMOVMSKB extracts the top bit of every byte, which IS the ">= 0x80" test, so one 32-byte load gives the terminator mask and the non-ASCII mask; BZHI drops the bits at and past the terminator so a high byte AFTER the string does not force the slow path; the first block is loaded ALIGNED DOWN (change 225's rule). **THE TEMPORARY CANNOT BE FIXED**: the shipped export answers a MEGABYTE of junk with ERROR_INVALID_SID rather than crashing, and the parser consumes arbitrarily many digits before refusing — so past 1022 characters it is allocated, and the free PRESERVES THE LAST ERROR because the parser has already set it. **AND IT FOUND A DEFECT IN CHANGE 269** (see that row). 162219 correctness cases three-way against the scalar model and live advapi32, 0 mismatches — including 21 shapes at EVERY ALIGNMENT 0..63, every length 1000..1050 across the stack/allocation boundary, every length 0..200 ending exactly at a guard page, and a quarter of 150000 randomised cases built entirely from high bytes (83586 inputs reached the code-page fallback; the gate FAILS if that class is empty); 40000 live cases asserting that EVERY success zeroed the last error — the assertion 269's harness could not make; 7 mutants, all caught. The bench pre-flight caught its own "1024 characters" row being an OVERFLOW REFUSAL (500-odd sub-authorities against a limit of 254) wearing the name of the longest accepted input |
| [273](changes/273-inet-addr/) | `inet_addr` (the LENIENT IPv4 parser; four forms, three bases, a wrapping accumulator) | `ws2_32.dll!inet_addr` | **LANDED** — geomean **3.51×** over 13 rows, worst row 2.64×, up to 4.86×. **IT CORRECTS A CLAIM THIS REPOSITORY ALREADY HAD**: live_subst_ws2.c opened with "ws2_32's IP-conversion exports are ALREADY COVERED by landed ntdll changes" and "ws2_32 does not parse or format an IP address at all", proved by a counter that moves when ntdll!RtlIpv4StringToAddressA (change 114) is patched. The counter is right — inet_addr DOES call it, on every input — but the conclusion is not: probes/grammar.c asks the two the same ten questions and they disagree on six. `1.2`, `1`, `0x7f.1` and `010.1.1.1` are addresses to inet_addr and refusals to the ntdll export; `1.2.3.4x` is the other way round. inet_addr calls it and then, when it refuses, PARSES THE STRING ITSELF. **That harness could never have caught it**, because patching change 114 with a BIT-EXACT replacement leaves the composite answer unchanged whichever branch is taken — a harness that only asks "did the answer change" cannot tell which of two implementations produced it. Corrected in place; this change patches ws2_32!inet_addr itself. **THREE RULES THAT ARE NOT WHAT THE SOURCE EVERYONE QUOTES DOES**: (1) **THE OVERFLOW TEST IS "DID THE ACCUMULATOR GO DOWN"** — 32 bits, WRAPPING, and a digit is refused only when the wrapped result is STRICTLY LESS than the value before it. `0x112345678` is accepted as 0x12345678 and the textbook check `acc > (MAX-d)/16` REFUSES it; `0x7FFFFFFF0` overflows 32 bits and is accepted; `12345678901` wraps to 0xDFDC1C35 and is accepted; `99999999999` wraps DOWN and is refused. probes/accum.c swept all sixteen leading hex digits at nine and ten digits: accepted iff the leading digit is 0 or 1, which only this test predicts. It is one instruction. (2) **WHITESPACE ENDS THE ADDRESS AND THE REST IS NEVER LOOKED AT** — any of 09 0A 0B 0C 0D 20 once a digit has been consumed, so `1.2.3.4 junk` is 1.2.3.4 and `1 junk` is 0.0.0.1; leading whitespace is refused. The set came from sweeping every byte in twelve positions, not from isspace. (3) **THE SINGLE BYTE 0x20 IS AN ADDRESS** — `" "` alone is 0.0.0.0, two spaces are not, a tab is not, `" 1"` is not, `""` is not; probes/lonespace.c asked from every side and NO model of the grammar explains it. **THE PROBE THAT FOUND THE ACCUMULATOR RULE HAD TO BE WRITTEN TWICE**: the first asked what `0x` plus k digits returns and built the digits out of ONES — and 0x11111111 is byte-wise palindromic, so wrapping, truncation, saturation and byte-swapping all read the same. 307888 correctness cases three-way against the scalar model and live ws2_32, 0 mismatches on the first run, including every byte 0x01..0xFF in 17 positions, 9..24 digits with every leading digit in three bases, up to 300 leading zeros, and every length 0..200 ending exactly at a guard page; 40000 live cases patching ws2_32!inet_addr ITSELF with a corpus built from what the probes found rather than from plausible dotted quads; 8 mutants, all caught — including the textbook range check |
| [274](changes/274-sysallocstring/) | `SysAllocString` (an AVX2 length scan into oleaut32's own allocator) | `oleaut32.dll!SysAllocString` | **PARKED** — correct (5270 cases, 0 mismatches, guard page included) and **4-5× on every string of 256 characters or more**, geomean 1.67-1.78×, but it cannot clear 0.97× on the SHORTEST rows in a single run. **THE ALLOCATION IS NOT OURS TO MAKE, and finding that out cost two process deaths**: probes/contract.c built a BSTR by hand in exactly the documented layout — a four-byte count at [-4], the characters, an uncounted wide NUL — and SysFreeString on it does not raise an exception, IT TERMINATES THE PROCESS (the probe's first version wrapped it in __try/__except and still died, exit code 116); CoTaskMemRealloc on a REAL BSTR's base pointer kills it the same way. oleaut32 keeps a private cache of BSTR blocks and nothing outside it can produce one the caller may free, so the allocation is CALLED and what is left to own is the LENGTH SCAN. **probes/where.c says the scan is worth owning**: it times the export, the export with the OS's own scan factored out, and the allocator alone, at every length — the scan is **0.1997 ns per character**, about one character per cycle, where change 001's wcslen runs at 0.03. That is 779 ns to win at 4000 characters. **BUT AT ZERO CHARACTERS THE SHIPPED SCAN COSTS 0.10 ns** on top of a 13.25 ns allocator call neither side can avoid, so no implementation beats it there. The file is a LEAF THAT TAIL-JUMPS (a call frame would cost more than the scan it speeds up) with the first eight characters PEELED SCALAR (at four, the peel fell through and paid full vector setup for a sixteen-byte string — that row measured 0.98×). **WHY IT IS PARKED**: the short rows sit at 1.00× ± 0.03 and the gate needs 0.97× on every class in ONE run. Five consecutive runs of the same binary gave the four-character row 1.03/1.05/1.00/1.02/1.01× and a sixth gave 0.98×; raising the measurement to eight allocations per row (120 ns instead of 16) left the worst row at 0.99/0.98/0.98/0.99× with the build's own run printing 0.97×. That is change 225's "anything decided at this scale has to be decided across runs" reaching a gate allowed only one — and here the row is ~15 ns of ALLOCATOR and ~0.3 ns of scan, so it measures oleaut32's free-block cache, not anything this change wrote. Tuning the rep count until the dice landed is the opposite of what the gate is for. A later attempt needs either a rule exempting a class whose entire measured cost is an OS call this project does not own, or a way into oleaut32's allocator that does not exist |
| [275](changes/275-inet-ntoa/) | `inet_ntoa` (the IPv4 formatter; one dword store per field out of a pre-rendered table) | `ws2_32.dll!inet_ntoa` | **LANDED** — geomean **3.24×** over 8 rows, worst row 3.22× (2.24 ns a call against 7.25). **IT EXISTS BECAUSE CHANGE 274 ASKED THE SAME QUESTION AND GOT THE OTHER ANSWER.** Both are "our code around storage we do not own", and both were settled by measuring the storage FIRST: 274's BSTR block comes from oleaut32's private cache and SysFreeString on a hand-made one TERMINATES THE PROCESS, with a 13.25 ns allocator floor around 0.3 ns of our work — parked; here the answer lives in ORDINARY THREAD-LOCAL STORAGE, which an implementation can have its own of, and probes/floor.c timed the whole TLS access at 0.38 ns against a 7.47 ns export with the digit-table version at 1.43 ns. That margin was established before a line of assembly was written. **THE CONTRACT IS THE BUFFER**: probes/contract.c measured the same pointer on every call from one thread, a different one per thread, undisturbed by inet_addr/htonl/WSAGetLastError/inet_ntop, working before WSAStartup, and the text agreeing with "%%u.%%u.%%u.%%u" over ALL 1024 byte-and-position combinations. **THE PROBE CAUGHT ITSELF**: it asked for the length of 255.255.255.255 and then of 0.0.0.0 and printed 7 for BOTH, because they are the same buffer and the second call had already overwritten the first — which is why both gates copy every answer the instant it comes back. **ONE DWORD STORE PER FIELD**: the table holds every value 0..255 already rendered WITH ITS TRAILING DOT packed into four bytes, plus a step table, so the dots come free and the fourth is overwritten with the terminator. 549168 correctness cases three-way against the scalar model and live ws2_32, 0 mismatches — including ALL 65536 COMBINATIONS OF ADJACENT BYTE PAIRS, which is what settles a per-byte formatter whose fields are concatenated (exhaustive over one byte is not enough and over four is 2^32; any interaction between fields is an interaction between NEIGHBOURS), and four threads checking their own buffers; 40000 live cases plus 16000 more across four threads created under the patch and joined before its removal; 6 mutants, all caught — including the buffer made SHARED instead of per-thread, which only the multi-threaded corpora can see. The eight rows are flat to within 1.5%% in both columns, which is what this function should look like: a row that stood out would mean a data-dependent path that should not exist |
| [276](changes/276-varbstrcmp/) | `VarBstrCmp` (an identity and byte-equality fast path into the OS collation) | `oleaut32.dll!VarBstrCmp` | **PARKED** — correct (65016 cases, 0 mismatches, 12553 of them ERROR returns) and **24× on two equal 4000-character strings, 113× when both operands are the SAME POINTER**, geomean 2.33-2.40× — but the rows that must COLLATE cannot be won. **THE COLLATION IS THE OS'S**: probes/contract.c asked nine pairs where linguistic and ordinal comparison DISAGREE (`a` vs `B`, `co-op` vs `coop`, `can't` vs `cant`) and the export tracked CompareStringW every time, never the ordinal answer. **WHAT WAS LEFT LOOKED HUGE**: probes/gap.c found the export compares neither the pointers NOR the bytes — the same pointer twice costs 3208 ns at 4000 characters, equal content costs the same, and a memcmp of those 8000 bytes costs 149 ns; two equal strings pay 0.8 ns per character to discover they are equal. And it is SAFE: probes/reflexive.c swept every code unit 1..0xFFFF alone and inside a longer string, every surrogate and noncharacter, under every valid flag and several locales — 0 of 131070 placements compare as anything but EQ with themselves. probes/errors.c then found the two rules a fast path must not break: the EMPTY cases do not validate (`"" vs ""` with a bad locale is still EQ) while a NON-EMPTY pair always does, EVEN WHEN BOTH OPERANDS ARE THE SAME POINTER (`VarBstrCmp(x,x,...,0x40)` is E_INVALIDARG, not EQ); the accepted flag mask is DERIVED from the OS bit by bit rather than written down. **WHY IT IS PARKED**: the shipped wrapper's ENTIRE overhead over the CompareStringW it calls is **1.25 ns**, so every comparison that has to be collated has nothing to give. The file was made as lean as it can be — NO saved registers at all, CompareStringW called DIRECTLY rather than through a helper — and two revisions were made chasing exactly this (six pushes read 0.97×; a C helper layer was most of a 1.25 ns budget). Across six runs the worst row was 0.96/0.74/0.96/0.96/0.98/0.94× and the gate printed PARKED five times out of six. **THIS IS THE SECOND CHANGE THIS SESSION TO FAIL THAT WAY** (see [274]): in both, the gate is measuring an OS CALL THIS PROJECT DOES NOT OWN — a 13.25 ns allocator there, a 33 ns collation here — with about a nanosecond of our code beside it, and calling the result a regression. A future attempt needs either a rule exempting a class whose entire measured cost is such a call, stated so it can be CHECKED rather than asserted, or the acceptance that wrappers are not where this method pays. Tuning a rep count until the dice land is not on the list |
| [277](changes/277-charupperbuffw/) | `CharUpperBuffW` + `CharLowerBuffW` (per-character case mapping; no table access at all for ASCII) | `user32.dll!CharUpperBuffW`, `CharLowerBuffW` | **LANDED** — geomean **7.94×** over 16 rows, worst row 1.06×, up to **25.8×**, EVERY ROW BETTER. **IT WAS PICKED BECAUSE 274 AND 276 WERE PARKED**: both of those lost on rows whose entire cost was an OS call this project does not own, so discovery/rtl_integer_char.c was written to find targets with the opposite shape and measured these at 0.7782 and 1.1598 ns PER CHARACTER against change 015's 0.02. **probes/mapping.c CONFIRMED THERE IS NOTHING BEHIND IT**: the mapping agrees with ntdll!RtlUpcaseUnicodeChar on ALL 65536 code units (and the lower form with RtlDowncaseUnicodeChar on all 65536); it is NOT locale-aware, agreeing with LCMapStringW under the user, invariant, German AND TURKISH locales — Turkish being the one that would differ if linguistic casing were involved; and it has NO CONTEXT, every code unit mapping the same alone as inside a run, 0 of 65535. So the whole measured cost is a lookup. **THE CONTRACT AT THE EDGES** cost the probe its life: a NULL buffer with a NON-ZERO count FAULTS (the first version called it and died at exit code 5), count 0 touches nothing, an embedded NUL is mapped PAST because the count is what matters, and the return value is the count passed in. **NO TABLE ACCESS AT ALL FOR ASCII**: a 16-character block with no code unit at or above 0x80 is handled entirely in YMM registers by a range subtract, and only blocks containing a high code unit fall back to the table — which is what the forty-times gap is made of. tables.c builds both 64K tables BY ASKING THESE EXPORTS THEMSELVES and then CHECKS THE RULE THE VECTOR PATH DEPENDS ON, that below 0x80 the table agrees with the range rule, refusing to run if it does not or if the table came back as the identity everywhere. 816269 correctness cases three-way against the scalar model and live user32 on the return value AND every character INCLUDING THOSE PAST THE COUNT, 0 mismatches — the corpus that earns its cost is a code unit at EVERY OFFSET 0..31 of a 32-character buffer (599232 cases), because where inside its block a high code unit lands is what decides which path runs and no round-numbered corpus puts one at offset 17. **THE GUARD-PAGE TEST CAUGHT ITSELF FIRST**: it placed the export's copy at `q - 64`, which for any length of 33 or more OVERLAPS ours by two bytes, so CharUpperBuffW rewrote the first character of our input and every length from 33 up "failed" — the implementation was right and the test was measuring itself. 40000 live cases with both exports patched together; 8 mutants, all caught, including the vector loop rounding the count up to a whole block. The lower direction wins by more at every length because the shipped CharLowerBuffW costs 1.16 ns/char against CharUpperBuffW's 0.78 while ours costs the same either way |
| [278](changes/278-rtlintegertounicodestring/) | `RtlIntegerToUnicodeString` (integer formatting in bases 2, 8, 10 and 16; length-first, no division) | `ntdll.dll!RtlIntegerToUnicodeString` | **LANDED** — geomean **5.38×** over 14 rows, worst row 3.44×, up to **11.2×**, EVERY ROW BETTER (core ntdll). discovery/rtl_integer_char.c measured the shipped export at 32.14 ns for ten decimal digits, 18.66 for eight hexadecimal, 20.35 for eleven octal, 40.27 for thirty-two binary — AND 10.16 ns FOR A SINGLE DIGIT. That spread is the tell: 32 binary digits costing only twice what 10 decimal digits costs while ONE digit still costs 10 ns says the cost is mostly fixed and partly per-digit, which is a division loop behind a validation prologue — the exact shape change 067 found and fixed, whose rewritten formatter does a whole 32-bit number in 3.76 ns against the 12.77 the division-per-digit version took. **THE CONTRACT WAS MEASURED, AND TWO ANSWERS ARE NOT WHAT THE DOCUMENTATION IMPLIES**: only bases 0, 2, 8, 10 and 16 are accepted (0 means 10) and every other value 1..0xFFFFFFFF including 4, 32 and 36 is STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER — probes/contract.c asked 0..20 ONE AT A TIME rather than trusting the documented set; and **THE ROOM RULE IS MaximumLength >= Length+2**, where change 067 found the analogous rule in RtlConvertSidToUnicodeString is **Length+1**, at which that routine writes NO TERMINATOR AT ALL. Two routines in the same DLL, one byte apart, and the only way to know which is which is to ask each one — 067's own gate could not see its rule because its corpus stepped MaximumLength BY TWO, and this one sweeps every value. Also: no prefix, UPPERCASE hex, zero is "0" in every base, the value is unsigned, and ON FAILURE THE DESTINATION IS COMPLETELY UNTOUCHED — Length included. **TWO CONVERTERS, BOTH LENGTH-FIRST**: base 10 is change 067's method (digits10 from one BSR and a table, then digits emitted TWO AT A TIME BACKWARDS as a single dword store, dividing by 100 with a multiply proved exact over all 2^32); bases 2, 8 and 16 share one shift-and-mask loop because they are all powers of two, the shift being BSR(base). All five tables are generated by the assembler and checked back against their definitions. 1101731 correctness cases three-way against the scalar model and live ntdll on the status, Length, MaximumLength AND every byte of a poison-filled destination — on FAILING calls too — with 0 mismatches on the first run; the corpus that earns its cost is every power of every base, one either side, at EVERY room 0..80, because the length comes from a BSR and a table and the room rule is decided one byte at a time. 40000 live cases with both converters and all three outcomes driven; 9 mutants, all caught, including a refusal zeroing Length on the way out. **THE LIVE HARNESS HUNG ON ITS FIRST RUN** having printed only its header: the corpus draws an illegal base from `rnd() %% 40` one case in seven, and the helper that recomputes the needed length divides by it — `v /= 1` never decreases. Base 2 wins least at 3.71× because 32 digits is 32 iterations whatever it is written in; a nibble table would halve it |
| [279](changes/279-rtlintegertochar/) | `RtlIntegerToChar` (length-first decimal; multi-digit power-of-two stores; zero-padded field widths) | `ntdll.dll!RtlIntegerToChar` | **LANDED - SUPERSEDES [097](changes/097-rtlintegertochar/)** - geomean **2.57x** over 16 rows, worst row 1.41x, up to **6.28x**, EVERY ROW BETTER (core ntdll). **THIS EXPORT WAS ALREADY REIMPLEMENTED, AND THE LANDED VERSION IS WRONG.** Change 097 landed it at 1.39x; its capacity test is `cmp edx, r10d / ja overflow`, an UNSIGNED compare, so a negative length reads as the largest possible room. Swept against live over every negative length -1..-60 in five bases it differs in **171600 of 171600** cases - including **returning SUCCESS and writing to the caller's buffer on calls the export REFUSES with STATUS_BUFFER_OVERFLOW**. Its gate never asked: the corpus swept `Length 0..40` and drew its random lengths from `(s>>7)%%40`. **The same defect is live in change [100](changes/100-rtllargeintegertochar/)** (123000 of 123000 negative-length cases differ, all 125050 positive ones correct) and is flagged there, not fixed here. **WHAT 097 MISSED IS A DOCUMENTED FEATURE: A NEGATIVE `length` IS A ZERO-PADDED FIELD WIDTH.** probes/contract.c asked the two values the type allows but nobody passes and got two DIFFERENT answers - -1 REFUSED, -100 SUCCEEDED and wrote "00", which for the value 3735928559 is not the answer to anything - so `length` is neither compared as signed nor cast to unsigned (as unsigned, -1 is the largest room and would have succeeded). probes/negative.c then swept every negative length against a GUARD PAGE: -10 gives "3735928559", -11 "03735928559", -13 "0003735928559", -100 really does write a hundred characters and FAULTS on a short buffer, and INT_MIN is the one negative length that refuses because it cannot be negated. A positive length NEVER pads: value 7 at length 8 is `37 00`, not "00000007". **THE ROOM RULE IS `length >= digits` WITH THE TERMINATOR ONLY IF IT FITS** - change 067's rule, NOT change 278's `Length+2`-and-always-terminate: THREE FORMATTERS IN ONE DLL, TWO RULES. 694501 correctness cases three-way vs the scalar model and live ntdll on the status AND every byte of a 256-byte poison-filled buffer - on FAILING calls too - 0 mismatches, 304816 of the successes zero-padded. **THE FIRST RUN OF THAT CORPUS DIED OF AN ACCESS VIOLATION BEFORE PRINTING A SINGLE MISMATCH**: it contained length = INT_MIN+1, which is not a refusal but a field width of 2147483647, and the live export duly began writing two billion zeros into a 256-byte buffer. 40000 live cases; 11 mutants, ALL CAUGHT BY BOTH GATES - the first six differed ONLY IN THE BUFFER with the status identical on both sides, so **a gate comparing only the NTSTATUS would have passed all six**, and the last three never reached the corpus because the table check caught them. **TWO ROUNDS OF THE A/B AGAINST 097 WERE LOSSES THE ntdll GATE COULD NOT SEE**: the shift-and-mask power-of-two loop inherited from 278 was 0.70x on hex and 0.75x on binary against 097 (which writes them MSB-first) while still reading BETTER against the shipped export - fixed by emitting several digits per store (a byte is two hex digits, six bits are two octal digits, a byte is eight binary digits as ONE 8-byte store); then hex was still 0.93x because the digit count came from a table indexed by BSR(base)*32+BSR(value), a shift, an add and a LOAD hanging off the BSR - fixed by making it arithmetic (`>>2` for hex, `(n*0AAABh)>>17` for octal). Final A/B vs 097: **1.54x geomean, every row at or above parity** (worst 1.01x, octal 2.00x, binary 2.61x). **AND THE VERY FIRST BENCH PARKED THE CHANGE** on one row of sixteen - "zero-padded to 100" at 182.11 ns vs 115.25, 0.63x - because the padding loop was a byte at a time; overlapping 32-byte SSE2 blocks took it to 41.65 ns, 2.76x |
| [280](changes/280-rtllargeintegertochar/) | `RtlLargeIntegerToChar` (eight-digit peel on one PROVED 64-bit reciprocal; multi-digit power-of-two stores; zero-padded field widths) | `ntdll.dll!RtlLargeIntegerToChar` | **LANDED - SUPERSEDES [100](changes/100-rtllargeintegertochar/)** - geomean **3.25x** over 18 rows, worst row 1.68x, up to **14.2x**, EVERY ROW BETTER (core ntdll). **CHANGE 279 DEFERRED THIS DELIBERATELY AND THE REASON IS NOW SETTLED BY PROOF.** Changes 067, 278 and 279 all rest on `(v*51EB851Fh)>>37 == v/100`, proved by RUNNING it over all 2^32 values - a proof that says nothing about a 64-bit domain, and 2^64 cases cannot be run. So this never divides a 64-bit value by 100: it peels EIGHT decimal digits at a time with ONE 64-bit division by 10^8 until what is left fits in 32 bits, where 067's constant applies (q1=v/10^8, q2=q1/10^8, q2<1845 -> 4+8+8 = 20, the longest answer). **probes/div64.c PROVES THAT ONE CONSTANT OVER THE WHOLE 2^64 DOMAIN WITHOUT RUNNING 2^64 CASES**: both sides of `umulh(n,12379400392853802749)>>26 == n/10^8` are monotone and the right side steps only at multiples of 10^8, so agreement at every step is agreement everywhere - and it checks EVERY ONE of the **184467440737** steps, on both sides, **368934881474 checks in 20.6 s** on 16 threads; it also computes the Granlund-Montgomery round-up criterion in exact arithmetic (e = M*d - 2^90 = 875776 <= 2^26) as an independent second opinion. Two arguments, one exhaustive and one arithmetic, that agree. **THE LANDED CHANGE 100 IS WRONG ON EVERY NEGATIVE LENGTH** - 315000 of 315000 cases, including returning SUCCESS and writing to the buffer where the export REFUSES, and differing by one byte even at -19 where no padding is needed. The contract was measured, not assumed: probes/contract.c established it is change 279's room rule (length >= digits, terminator only if it fits), that a negative length is a zero-padded field width honoured LITERALLY (-96 fills a 96-byte buffer exactly), that INT_MIN is the one negative length that refuses, that the value is UNSIGNED despite PLARGE_INTEGER being signed (0x8000000000000000 -> 9223372036854775808), and - by putting the LARGE_INTEGER on a NOACCESS page - **that the base is validated BEFORE the value pointer is dereferenced**, which this implementation reproduces. Longest answers are 64 binary, 22 octal, 20 decimal, 16 hex, so the corpus sweeps lengths to +/-80 where 279 swept +/-40: a sweep to 40 would never reach base 2's room rule at all. 1512057 correctness cases three-way vs the scalar model and live ntdll on the status AND every byte of a 320-byte poison-filled buffer - on FAILING calls too - **0 mismatches on the first run**, with 622174 zero-padded and 200459 through the peel. 40000 live cases with all five paths driven. **19 MUTANTS, 18 CAUGHT BY BOTH GATES AND THE 19TH PROVED EQUIVALENT**: the wide fill's tail store one byte SHORT survives, and that is not a hole - this change writes the padding BEFORE the digits, the fill invariant is `rax+rcx == r9+pad` which is exactly the first digit position, so the extra byte is always overwritten by a digit; the same off-by-one in the OTHER direction is caught, which is what proves the fill is under test. **THE A/B AGAINST 100 CAUGHT A LOSS THE ntdll GATE COULD NOT SEE**: "7" and "0" came out at 0.80x of 100 while every row already read BETTER against the shipped export - and making the digit count cheaper did NOT fix it (the cost was seven taken branches between deciding "one digit" and storing one byte, not the count); writing that case where it is decided took it to 1.49x and lifted the ntdll geomean 2.90 -> 3.25x. Also documented and NOT reproduced: on a field width past the end of the buffer the shipped export RETURNS C0000005 for a small overrun and RAISES past ~32 bytes, while this always raises - matching it would mean an SEH frame on a leaf, paid on every call, to change how a caller bug is delivered; probes/overrun.c measures exactly that boundary and no corpus enters the regime |
| [281](changes/281-strchriw/) | `StrChrIW` (case-insensitive character search; per-needle match sets, AVX2) | `shlwapi.dll!StrChrIW` | **LANDED** - geomean **145.36x** over 12 rows, worst row 20.9x, best **377.9x**, EVERY ROW BETTER. The shipped export takes **21939.92 ns to scan 511 code units -- 43 ns PER CHARACTER** -- because its equality test is CompareStringW with NORM_IGNORECASE evaluated once per code unit; CompareStringOrdinal compares the same 511 in 85.91 ns. **FIVE HYPOTHESES ABOUT THE RELATION WERE WRONG BEFORE THE RIGHT ONE**, and every failure was the same failure this repo keeps auditing others for: a test that COULD NOT EXPRESS the case that was wrong. (1) probes/contract.c: "it is the ordinal upcase table, exactly", 0 disagreements over 3892 candidate pairs -- WRONG, because it built those pairs from CharUpperW/CharLowerW/RtlUpcase/RtlDowncase and so could never ask about (U+1D2C MODIFIER LETTER CAPITAL A, 'a'); gate 1 caught it, 8 mismatches in 140561. (2) NOT linguistic (probes/widerfold.c): e-acute does not find 'e', fullwidth 'a' does not find 'a', sharp s does not expand. (3) NOT FoldStringW(MAP_FOLDCZONE) composed with an upcase -- that fails on U+01BB..U+01BD, which fold onto DIGITS. (4) **NOT AN EQUIVALENCE RELATION AT ALL**: probes/locale.c proved it INVARIANT (en-US, de-DE, TURKISH, invariant, Turkish preferred-UI), probes/context.c proved every match decided by ONE character (200000 strings, 0 context-dependent), probes/isequiv.c proved it SYMMETRIC (0 asymmetric in 400000) -- and then **U+D7B0 matches U+D7A2, U+D7B1 matches U+D7A2, U+D7B0 does NOT match U+D7B1**. It is a TOLERANCE relation with **168 intransitive triples**, so it has NO CLASSES; the class-based build was wrong in 66 of 206096 gate cases, every one of them a case where OURS AGREED WITH LIVE and only the model disagreed. (5) "searching for the terminator returns NULL" -- WRONG TOO: that was measured on "abcXYZabc", which has no ignorable character. NUL has zero collation weight and matches every other zero-weight code unit, so a 275-char random string returns offset 28 for needle 0. **That one was caught by the LIVE HARNESS ONLY AFTER IT WAS STRENGTHENED BECAUSE TWO MUTANTS SURVIVED IT** -- its strings sat in the middle of a static buffer so an unbounded scan still agreed, and its corpus never used needle 0; one case in three now ends at a PAGE_NOACCESS page and one in 37 searches for the terminator, and that second change found the real defect in 940 of 40000 cases. THE IMPLEMENTATION never asks what a character folds to -- the needle is fixed for the call, so it asks whether each character is one of the needle's partners, fetched once: 56825 needles match only themselves (one broadcast, one VPCMPEQW per 16), 5390 have 2-8 partners (four broadcasts at <=4, an inline list at 5-8), and 3321 have more than eight while sharing only ELEVEN DISTINCT SETS (an 8 KB membership bitmap, one BT per code unit). FOUR is the register budget, not a guess: Win64 makes xmm6-xmm15 non-volatile, and the first draft used ymm6/ymm7 as scratch. 206101 correctness cases three-way on the returned OFFSET over EVERY needle 0..65535, every 32-byte start alignment and a guard page, 0 mismatches; 40000 live cases with all four dispatch shapes driven explicitly (a uniform draw reaches the bitmap needles 5%% of the time); 13 mutants, 13 caught |
| [282](changes/282-strrchriw/) | `StrRChrIW` (case-insensitive character search, BACKWARDS over an explicit range) | `shlwapi.dll!StrRChrIW` | **LANDED** - geomean **261.12x** over 11 rows, worst row 27.8x, best **5304.6x**, EVERY ROW BETTER. The shipped export costs 47 ns PER CHARACTER (24263.40 ns for 511 code units) and -- the 5304x row -- **SCANS FORWARD EVEN THOUGH IT RETURNS THE LAST MATCH**, so a hit at index 509 costs it the same 16.8 us as a miss while a backward scan finds it in the first block and leaves. Reuses change 281's match relation UNCHANGED (symmetric, intransitive, 10553170 pairs, stored per needle) and its four-way dispatch. **THE SHAPE IS NOT StrChrIW's AND WAS MEASURED, NOT ASSUMED**: (start, END-exclusive, wMatch), returns the LAST match, and **HAS NO TERMINATOR** -- "abcd\0fghijk" with end=start+11 finds 'J' at 9, and an end past a guard page FAULTS rather than stopping. probes/contract.c also settled a genuinely ambiguous signature: **StrChrNIW takes a COUNT, not an end pointer** -- the discovery sweep had called it with a pointer and got a plausible answer, but BOTH readings return NULL on that call, so it never distinguished them (change 273's all-ones-hex-digits trap). **GATE 1 FAILED ON ITS FIRST RUN WITH 21 MISMATCHES, ALL NEEDLE 0**, and found a hole in change 281 itself: 281 extracted the relation from a haystack of every code unit 1..65535, which COULD NOT CONTAIN A NUL because StrChrIW stops at the terminator -- so what matches code unit ZERO was unreachable BY CONSTRUCTION. StrRChrIW has no terminator and can be asked: probes/nulchar.c found **3238 needles match a NUL**, exactly the 3237 ignorables plus NUL itself, symmetrically. Same defect class as 097/100, but sitting in the EXTRACTION METHOD rather than in a test; folded into 281's tables with a consistency check, and 281's gates re-run unaffected. 228956 correctness cases three-way over every needle 0..65535, every start alignment x every length 1..80 with a match at every position AND matches planted OUTSIDE the range that must stay invisible, NULs at every position, and guard pages at BOTH ends (reading past `end` and reading before `start` are two different mistakes); 0 mismatches. 40000 live cases. **10 MUTANTS, 9 CAUGHT, 1 PROVED EQUIVALENT -- AND THE ONE THAT SURVIVED BOTH GATES WAS THE IMPORTANT ONE**: dropping `and ecx,-2` returns a pointer ONE BYTE off, into the middle of a wchar_t, and both gates compared `p - base` on a wchar_t* which DIVIDES THE ODD BYTE AWAY. Both now compare BYTE offsets -- change 268's lesson in another costume. Two more were gate-4 corpus gaps (no empty ranges; nothing matching before a range) and are fixed. The survivor is equivalent and provably so: r8 steps down 32 at a time from an aligned r11 to an aligned r10 and can never be less than it, so `jbe` and `je` test the same condition |
| [283](changes/283-strrstriw/) | `StrRStrIW` (case-insensitive SUBSTRING search, backwards; vector first-character filter in front of a scalar verifier) | `shlwapi.dll!StrRStrIW` | **LANDED** - geomean **74.30x** over 8 rows, worst row 17.8x, best 145x, EVERY ROW BETTER. The shipped export costs 21816.97 ns over 511 code units. **THIS CHANGE WAS WRONG TWICE AND A GATE PASSED IT BOTH TIMES** - the entry worth reading in this table, because both errors were about what the TERMINATOR means and in both cases the corpus could not express the case. Draft 1 believed a match must fit before the terminator (highest start `hlen-nlen`); corpus 8, written to catch a MUTANT that removed exactly that clamp, instead caught the LIVE export returning a match the clamp forbids -- `{Q, SOFT HYPHEN}` is found at the last character of "zzzq", because the soft hyphen is one of the 3320 code units that match a NUL (change 282) and it was matched against the terminator. Draft 2 therefore read real memory past the terminator; the live-substitution gate, which reuses ONE buffer for 30000 cases so the bytes after a terminator are the previous case's letters, found 3 disagreements at once. probes/pastnul2.c settled it: the string behaves as though followed by ENDLESS NULs and those NULs are NEVER LOADED -- with 'W' after the terminator `{Q,SHY,SHY}` is still found while `{Q,W}` is not, a needle LONGER than the whole string can match, the same holds at an EMBEDDED NUL (`{B,SHY,SHY}` found, `{B,SHY,C}` not), and nothing faults with the terminator as the last readable code unit. **SO A NEEDLE CAN ONLY RUN PAST THE END IF ITS TRAILING CHARACTERS ALL MATCH A NUL**: with maxtail that suffix length, the top candidate is min(hlen-nlen+maxtail, hlen-1, endq) and the range splits into region A (the match lies wholly inside the string -- vector filter and last-character probe unrestricted) and region B (at most maxtail candidates, searched FIRST, comparing only the characters really present). maxtail is zero for every ordinary needle, which is why draft 1 passed everything not built to ask, and it is computed only when it can change the answer. **A SECOND REAL DEFECT**: the last-character-first probe read hay[p+nlen-1] unconditionally, which past the terminator is an ACCESS VIOLATION WHERE THE EXPORT DOES NOT EVEN FAULT (the export compares left to right and short-circuits) -- caught by corpus 11, and fixed for free by the region split. 13263 correctness cases three-way on BYTE offsets, 0 mismatches; corpora 7-13 EACH EXIST BECAUSE A MUTANT SURVIVED and are named after the case the earlier ones could not express (interior near-misses, a match below `start`, every member of every filter dispatch class, rejected candidates at a guard page, a NON-ZERO buffer, a needle whose FIRST character matches a NUL). 16 mutants: 15 caught, 1 PROVED equivalent (dropping the filter re-arm selects the exhaustive wide scan -- same answers over 43263 cases, 74.3x -> 71.8x); mutant 5 does not terminate and is recorded as a HANG, not a mismatch. probes/partners.c: only NINE partner counts exist (0,2,3,4,5,6,7,8,255), so the WIDE threshold is testable only through U+004B whose set has five members. First change with a real frame and seven saved registers (r15/r14/r13/r12/rbx/rsi/rdi via .pushreg) plus internal calls; match_pair clobbers exactly rax/r12/r13, so the maxtail loop needs no spills. 30000 live cases, 0 differ |
| [284](changes/284-strstriw/) | `StrStrIW` (case-insensitive SUBSTRING search, FORWARDS, first match; vector first-character filter in front of a scalar verifier) | `shlwapi.dll!StrStrIW` | **LANDED** - geomean **89.41x** over 8 rows, worst row 5.3x, best 329x, EVERY ROW BETTER. The shipped export costs 1281 ns over 511 code units (far less than StrRStrIW's 21816.97 ns, because a forward search stops at the first hit). **NOTHING WAS INHERITED FROM CHANGE 283** - that change shipped two wrong drafts which each passed a gate, so probes/contract.c asked every question again. Most answers matched: per character not a span collation, the intransitive triple, the VIRTUAL NUL (with 'W' after the terminator `{Q,SHY,SHY}` is found and `{Q,W}` is not), a needle longer than the string can match, a match may start only at a REAL character, an embedded NUL ends the search but a NUL-matching needle character matches it. **ONE ANSWER DID NOT, AND IT IS A REAL DIFFERENCE BETWEEN THE TWO EXPORTS**: probes/contract.c asked about the EMPTY NEEDLE over "abcXYZabc", got NULL, and the first draft refused it the way 283 correctly does for StrRStrIW. The live-substitution gate then differed on 96 of 30000 cases, every one an empty needle over a haystack that happened to contain a soft hyphen. "abcXYZabc" holds no code unit matching a NUL and an empty needle's first code unit IS the terminator, so it found nothing for a reason unrelated to the needle - the same blind corpus this family keeps producing. probes/emptyneedle.c settled it: **StrStrIW returns the FIRST code unit matching a NUL (soft hyphen at 4 -> 8, two of them -> the first, at index 0 -> 0, none -> NULL, and a zero width space which is ignorable but NOT NUL-matching -> NULL), while StrRStrIW returns NULL whatever the haystack holds.** Reproduced exactly by treating nlen 0 as nlen 1 - the needle's first code unit IS the terminator, its set is the 3238 NUL-matching units, and the range stays [0,hlen-1] so the terminator is never a match. **THE TERMINATOR SCAN IS VECTORISED AND THAT IS NOT A DETAIL**: measured scalar-first, the row "hit near the START (1)" came out at 1.00x -- A DEAD TIE -- because a forward search finding its match at index 1 had already walked all 511 code units to measure the string, which the export never does; wterm (16 code units at a time, aligned down and masked, page-safe) took that row to 5.27x, the 511 MISS row from 120x to 314x and the geomean from 74.4x to 89.4x. maxtail is computed IN REGION B, not at setup: vscan uses r8 as its cursor and the first draft held maxtail there across the scan, reporting 115 spurious matches at the last character. 7891 correctness cases three-way on BYTE offsets, 0 mismatches - corpora 7-13 were carried over from 283 BEFORE this gate was first run, because each of them exists there because a mutant survived. 18 mutants: 17 caught (two of them by NON-TERMINATION, recorded as hangs not mismatches), 1 PROVED equivalent (region B starting one position low re-tests at most two candidates, one with an identical test region A already failed and one with a strictly stronger test). Mutant 4 also exposed a gate-4 blind spot - removing region B's cap only matters when the WHOLE needle matches a NUL, and the live harness now forces that. 30000 live cases, 0 differ. Three internal routines under one frame with seven saved registers |
| [285](changes/285-strcspniw/) | `StrCSpnIW` (case-insensitive SPAN over a SET: how many leading characters are not in the set) | `shlwapi.dll!StrCSpnIW` | **LANDED** - geomean **93.38x** over 8 rows, worst row 19.9x, best 356x, EVERY ROW BETTER. The shipped export costs 2971 ns over 511 code units, the most expensive of the remaining StrXxxIW family. **THE RELATION WAS EXTRACTED, NOT ASSUMED**: probes/contract.c threw up an apparent contradiction (a set of {SOFT HYPHEN} does not match a ZERO WIDTH SPACE, although change 283 called such a pair a match), so probes/relation.c used the export itself as an oracle -- `StrCSpnIW({c},{m})==0` is a direct membership test, so one row costs 65535 calls -- and diffed twelve rows against change 281's tables: **786420 pairs, ZERO disagreements**, symmetric, and a four-member set is EXACTLY the union of its members' rows over all 65535 code units. The contradiction was mine: n[0x200B] is 0, so the ZERO WIDTH SPACE matches only itself and is not one of the 3237 ignorables, while match(0x00AD,0x034F) is 1 -- two landed changes had a corpus filler built on that error and were fixed (af5ba7a). **THE VIRTUAL NUL THAT 283 AND 284 HAD TO MODEL IS UNOBSERVABLE HERE**: whether the terminator counts as a set member or merely stops the scan, the answer is the length either way -- so the terminator is folded into the accept set and this change NEVER MEASURES THE STRING, which is what 284 learned the hard way. **FOUR MEASUREMENTS SHAPED THE ALGORITHM**: a 12-character set cost 12462 ns (16.8x) because the accept-list cap of 16 is exhausted by a four-character set -> 256 entries and nine bounded vector passes, 553 ns; that set matching at index 3 cost 231 ns (5.99x) because one full pass per chunk scans everything -> DISJOINT DOUBLING WINDOWS find it in the first window at no extra cost to the no-match case (the windows do not overlap, so the block count is unchanged), 65 ns; a one-character set slipped 49.2 -> 55.1 ns -> four-or-fewer accept entries skip the windows entirely; and a set holding an ignorable cost 916 ns (21.1x) because the scalar path called match_pair per (character, member) pair -> member-major with the kind hoisted gives three tight loops and NO CALLS, 310 ns. 5803 correctness cases three-way, 0 mismatches. 19 mutants: 13 caught, 6 PROVED equivalent -- including the one change 282 found FATAL (an odd byte offset), harmless here because TZCNT on a VPCMPEQW mask is always even AND the return value is a COUNT that divides the odd byte away. **AND THREE CORPORA TURNED OUT TO BE VACUOUS -- PASSING WHILE PROVING NOTHING** because the filler was itself in the set ('z' matches 'Z'; U+FFFD has n=255 and IS an ignorable). The fix is a CHECK, not a better filler: U+0002 matches only itself, proved at startup against all 21 sets, and plant() re-proves it per case by asking the live export what the untouched string gives. probes/pooltail.c also measured two facts the sweep turned into questions: a pool slot is eight words, so a member with EIGHT partners has the next slot's first word just past its members (U+02B9 -> U+02BA, 15 slots like that), and of ELEVEN distinct sentinel sets only one contains a NUL -- so the scalar loop's own terminator test really is needed. First change with a frame, seven saved registers, a 576-byte .allocstack and three internal routines |
| [286](changes/286-strchrniw/) | `StrChrNIW` (case-insensitive character search bounded by a COUNT) | `shlwapi.dll!StrChrNIW` | **LANDED** - geomean **125.64x** over 8 rows, worst row 32.8x, best 351x, EVERY ROW BETTER - the highest geomean AND the highest worst row of the whole 281-286 family, because a count-bounded single-character search has no verifier to defeat and therefore no adversarial case of the kind that holds 283 to 17x and 284 to 5x. **THE ONLY PRIOR NUMBER FOR THIS EXPORT WAS MEASURING SOMETHING ELSE**: discovery/charclass_strcmp_2026.c reported 1655 ns over 511 code units -- the sole reason it was on the list -- by timing `nn(A, A+511, L'#')` and labelling it "range form" after reusing StrRChrIW's typedef. The real shape is (start, match, count), so that call passed the low half of an ADDRESS as the character and 35 as the count; a wrong argument order does not fault, it just answers a different question. probes/contract.c settled it by calling the same address through BOTH prototypes (count reading -> the right pointer, range reading -> NULL), and this change's bench found the real cost to be **16614-17330 ns**, an order of magnitude more. **AND THE TERMINATOR IS NEVER A MATCH HERE**, which is where it parts company with 283 and 284: there a needle character matching a NUL matched the terminator itself and getting it wrong cost 283 two drafts; here searching "abcd" for a NUL gives NULL, and so does searching it for a SOFT HYPHEN which the relation says matches a NUL. So THREE exports over the SAME relation treat the terminator three different ways. The terminator is folded into the vector scan, which also makes a count of 0xFFFFFFFF safe: the bound address is never approached because the NUL stops the scan first. The WIDE path was measured too -- calling match_pair per character cost 838 ns (22.6x, the worst row) because every call re-derived the character's kind, so the kind is dispatched ONCE into two tight call-free loops: 316 ns, 60.0x. That leaves this change with NO match_pair routine at all, because nothing would call it. 4669 correctness cases three-way, 0 mismatches FIRST RUN, with every corpus shape and the plant() vacuity guard carried in from 283-285 before the gate was run once. **18 mutants, 18 CAUGHT, NO SURVIVORS** -- the first change in this run with none, which is what carrying the lessons in from the start buys. Mutant 7 is a deliberate CROSS-CHECK: dropping the word rounding so an odd byte offset returns is the IDENTICAL edit change 285 PROVED EQUIVALENT (its return is a count, and sar rax,1 divides the odd byte away) and it is FATAL here (the return is a pointer) -- the same edit, three verdicts across 282/285/286, each earned by the return type. 30000 live cases, 0 differ, with the count forced one short of the match, exactly reaching it, and far past the terminator |
| [287](changes/287-getstringtypew/) | `GetStringTypeW` (per-code-unit character classification, all three CT_CTYPE tables) | `kernelbase.dll!GetStringTypeW` | **LANDED** - geomean **3.79x** over 10 rows, worst row 2.60x, EVERY ROW BETTER. The shipped export costs 421.05 ns over 511 code units (discovery/uncovered_2026b.c), the most expensive uncovered export in that sweep that is not a known collation wall. **THE LOWEST GEOMEAN OF THE RUN, AND THAT IS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN A SHORTFALL**: unlike 281-286, where the export paid for a per-character collation call, here the export is ALSO a table lookup -- both sides do the same fundamental work, and 3.8x is what removing the call overhead and the surrounding generality is worth. At 0.2 ns per code unit (~0.9 cycles) the loop is within 1.4x of the two-loads-per-unit port limit. **ONE QUESTION DECIDED WHETHER IT COULD BE WRITTEN, AND IT IS CHANGE 281's**: a classification depending on a character's NEIGHBOURS or on the thread LOCALE cannot be a table, which is how 274 and 276 died. probes/contract.c: 20000 random strings up to 2048 units, **0 context-dependent words** for all three info types; the whole CT_CTYPE1 table rebuilt under SEVEN thread locales, **0 entries different**; all 65535 units classified; and strictly one info type at a time (CT1|CT2, 0 and 8 all refused). **THREE THINGS THE BENCH OVERTURNED.** (1) probes/tableshape.c measured the tables as highly redundant -- 20/12/57 distinct values, only 62/62/67 of 256 pages distinct, so a two-level directory-and-page layout is 4.1x smaller -- and measured end to end it came out at 2.25x, because every unit cost a compare, a branch and two dependent loads: THE MEMORY SAVING WAS REAL AND THE SPEED WAS NOT, so it is flat (384 KB BSS, one load, no branch) at 3.79x, with the two-level tables kept for the MODEL so the three-way gate compares different routes.
