(2) Reading the source with ONE vector load per eight units and VPEXTRW for the indices -- one load per unit instead of two, a 0.38-cycle floor on paper -- measured SLOWER (130.77 vs 102.15 ns; 3.08x vs 3.81x), because the vector-to-GPR transfer costs more here than the scalar load it removes; the negative result is recorded in impl.asm rather than dropped. (3) The "alternating Latin-1/CJK" row was written to punish a branch that no longer exists and now times the same as every other row -- which is itself the evidence that nothing in the loop depends on the input's composition. **AND THE LIVE GATE FOUND A CONTRACT FACT THE PROBE HAD MISSED**: the SHIPPED export died with an access violation, pre-patch, on a guard-page string of length 5 asked for with cchSrc = 8. An explicit COUNT IS AUTHORITATIVE AND EXACT -- with five characters and the terminator as the last readable words, cchSrc 6 is fine and 7 FAULTS, while -1 is safe because it stops at the terminator. Our code already read exactly cch, so nothing changed in the implementation; what changed is that the corpus stopped feeding the export an input it cannot survive. The correctness corpus DID contain counts past the terminator and they passed, because those strings have readable memory after them -- which is why the guard page belongs in a gate. 198870 correctness cases, 0 mismatches, comparing the return value, every output word AND THE WORD JUST PAST THE END (this is the first change that writes a caller-supplied buffer, so "one word too many" is its own failure mode) -- including every one of the 65536 code units individually for all three types. 16 mutants: 14 caught, 2 PROVED equivalent -- one of them the odd-byte-offset edit for the THIRD time in the project, FATAL in 282 (a pointer return) and equivalent in 285 and here (the value is divided by two either way). 30000 live cases, 0 differ, with the tables derived from the SHIPPED export BEFORE the patch went on, because deriving them through our own code would make the gate compare us against ourselves |
| [288](changes/288-foldstringw-digits/) | `FoldStringW`, the `MAP_FOLDDIGITS` path (every Unicode digit folded to its ASCII equivalent) | `kernelbase.dll!FoldStringW` | **LANDED** - geomean **4.88x** over 7 rows, worst row 1.78x, EVERY ROW BETTER. The shipped export costs 636.57 ns over 511 code units (discovery/uncovered_2026b.c), the second most expensive uncovered export in that sweep behind only lstrcmpiW, which is an annotated collation wall. **THE SCOPE IS ONE OF FIVE FLAG PATHS AND IT WAS MEASURED, NOT CHOSEN**: probes/contract.c folded every code unit under every flag and MAP_FOLDDIGITS is the ONLY strictly 1:1 one (0 grew, 462 changed), while MAP_FOLDCZONE turns one unit into as many as EIGHTEEN and MAP_COMPOSITE grows 12197 of them -- a mapping that changes the length is not a per-character table at any width, and composition depends on neighbours, which is the wall 274 and 276 died on. Those four flags are separate problems and this function DECLINES them, the same way crypt32!CryptBinaryToStringA is covered by four separate changes. MAP_FOLDDIGITS itself is CONTEXT-FREE (20000 random strings up to 2048 units, 0 disagreements, no length ever changing) and LOCALE-INVARIANT (seven thread locales, 0 entries different). The table is FLAT because change 287 already measured the alternative: a two-level layout for the same shape came out at 2.25x against 3.79x flat. **TWO REAL DEFECTS, BOTH FOUND BY A MUTANT THAT SURVIVED RATHER THAN ONE THAT WAS CAUGHT.** Gates 1, 2 and 3 were green at 66410 cases / 0 mismatches and the code was still wrong twice. Mutant #10 deleted the NULL-destination refusal and the gate passed everything, because the gate helper derives the destination from cchDest (`cchDest ? a : 0`) and so could only ever pair NULL with 0 -- A CORPUS THAT COULD NOT EXPRESS THE CASE, again. Worse and new: THE REFUSAL HAD NEVER BEEN MEASURED AT ALL. It was written into impl.asm from the natural assumption that NULL must be refused, and reference.c inherited the ordering FROM impl.asm rather than from the export, so the three-way comparison was structurally blind to it -- **an assumption held by both sides of a comparison is invisible to comparing them**. probes/nulldest.c asked the export and found the refusal real but the ORDER wrong twice: (1) a NULL destination with a too-small cchDest gives 87, NOT 122, so the NULL test beats the buffer test and the draft had it the other way round -- a real wrong answer on a reachable input; (2) an unsupported flag reports 1004 alone but 87 when a pointer is also bad, so the flag test is FIFTH and the draft had it first. Fixing (2) NARROWED THE DECLARED DIVERGENCE: with the flag test fifth this function now matches the export on every refusal, declined flags included, and what is left of the scope limit is exactly one input class -- a supported-but-unimplemented flag with wholly valid parameters. The measured order is src NULL, dest == src, cchSrc 0, (cchDest != 0 && dest NULL), the flag, cchDest == 0 -> the count, cchDest < count -> 122; step 4 is CONDITIONAL on cchDest (a NULL destination with cchDest 0 SUCCEEDS) and steps 1-5 all precede the length scan, proven with a five-unit unterminated string ending exactly at a guard page that RETURNS 87 rather than faulting. **THE OVERLAP BEHAVIOUR IS UNSPECIFIED AND COMPLETELY DETERMINISTIC**: dest == src is refused by POINTER EQUALITY ONLY (src+1, src+4, src+8, src-4 all succeed), and probes/overlap.c measured what the export then produces -- exactly what a naive forward one-unit-at-a-time loop produces, at every offset, reading units it has already overwritten; with twelve Arabic-Indic digits and dest = src+3 it returns 0030 0031 0032 repeated, the signature of reading its own output. The unrolled loop does not reproduce that, so impl.asm DETECTS OVERLAP AND DROPS ITS UNROLL, and the cost falls only on inputs the documentation already forbids. 66656 correctness cases, 0 mismatches, three-way against the live export and an independent scalar model on the return value, every output word, THE WORD JUST PAST THE END and GetLastError on every refusal (the implementation writes the error straight to the TEB at gs:[68h], so the gate verifies that offset rather than trusting it). 39 mutants: 36 caught, 3 PROVED EQUIVALENT BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN BY ARGUMENT in probes/unrollhazard.c -- the unrolled and naive loops differ at offsets 1, 2 and 3 AND NOWHERE ELSE at all twelve lengths from 8 to 96 (structurally: four units are read, then written, so a write can only reach an unread unit inside the same group of four), a destination BELOW the source never differs, and every multiple-of-eight split of the unroll agrees; the third survivor is the odd-byte-offset edit for the FOURTH time in the project, FATAL in 282 (a pointer return) and equivalent in 285, 287 and here because the value is divided by two either way. **NO GATE 4, STATED RATHER THAN OMITTED**: a single-flag implementation is not a drop-in for a five-flag export, so hot-patching it would break every MAP_FOLDCZONE caller, and a weakened gate driving only MAP_FOLDDIGITS could not fail -- the change-081 precedent. Gate 3 stands in and is a weak substitute, catching only the 3 of 39 mutants that break the frame; that is why gate 1 grew to seven sections and the mutant set to 39. **THE FIRST FIVE-ARGUMENT CHANGE IN THE REPOSITORY**, so the first to exercise a stack-passed argument: cchDest arrives at [rsp+40] and is read at [rsp+96] after seven pushes, with a mutant at [rsp+88] confirming the gate reaches it, and the ABI harness gained CALL5. Three bench runs gave 10.20x, 4.74x and 4.88x and **THE 10.2x RUN IS DISCARDED AND NOT CLAIMED** -- in it the SYSTEM export timed 1101-1215 ns instead of 628-737 and the 16-unit row 276 ns instead of 24, a cold path in the export, not in us; our own timings were 103-108 ns in all three, within 3%. The four 511-unit rows time within 0.5 ns of each other whether every unit changes, none does, or they alternate, which is the evidence that nothing in the loop depends on the input |
| [289](changes/289-widechartomultibyte/) | `WideCharToMultiByte` for the UTF-8 code page -- the encoder, its MEASURING MODE (`cbMultiByte == 0`), and the NUL-terminated form (`cchWideChar` negative) | `kernelbase.dll!WideCharToMultiByte` | **LANDED** - geomean **2.71x** over 84 rows, worst row 1.22x, LANDS in 5 of 5 runs. **THE HIGHEST FAN-IN GENUINELY CONVERTIBLE FUNCTION ON THIS MACHINE**: 310 desktop modules and 126 startup modules bind it, measured by tools/desktop-surface.py against the LIVE process set rather than a static dependency walk. discovery/desktop-startup-timings.md clocked the shipped CP_UTF8 path at 0.235 ns/byte on ASCII and **1.071 ns/byte on Cyrillic** -- about 0.93 GB/s on the input UTF-8 exists for. **THE DISPATCH BOUNDARY IS THE DESIGN.** Our code runs only for CP_UTF8 with dwFlags == 0, no lpDefaultChar, no lpUsedDefaultChar, valid arguments and a non-overlapping destination; everything else tail-calls the real export through one `jmp qword ptr [__imp_WideCharToMultiByte]`. That is not a hedge -- the ACP and OEM code pages are OS data tables, the best-fit mapping behind WC_NO_BEST_FIT_CHARS is more OS data, and UTF-7 is a different encoder with its own state machine; guessing at those is how a faster function corrupts text. The gate still drives 8 code pages x 9 flag sets x every pointer shape through the function to prove the boundary is where the header says it is. **THREE THINGS MSDN GETS WRONG, each proved both ways against the running export**: lpDefaultChar is IGNORED for CP_UTF8 (the shipped code checks it only for CP_UTF7); lpUsedDefaultChar is ACCEPTED and WRITTEN; and dwFlags accepts exactly 0x6F0, rejecting every other bit with ERROR_INVALID_FLAGS, with only WC_ERR_INVALID_CHARS doing anything -- and it fails the call AFTER the U+FFFD bytes are already in the caller's buffer. Also proved: a negative cchWideChar means NUL-terminated for ANY negative value (checked -2, -1000 and INT_MIN) and the terminator is converted and counted; on success the last error is left exactly as the caller had it, which correctness.c verifies with a sentinel before every call. **BENCHED ON FOUR INPUT CLASSES, NOT ONE** -- ASCII, 2-byte, mixed-width and lone-surrogate, each in all three modes at four sizes -- which is the direct lesson of discovery/utf8_nonascii_rows.c, where changes 016 and 034 were benched on ASCII alone while running 0.21x-0.94x on every other class. The measuring mode is implemented from the first commit; 016 and 034 shipped without theirs and FAULTED on a documented call. The encoder is change 016's, already proved bit-exact against ntdll!RtlUnicodeToUTF8N, and reusing it rather than re-deriving the malformed-input rules is what made this tractable. Proven live in live-substitution/live_subst_cvt.c. |
| [290](changes/290-multibytetowidechar/) | `MultiByteToWideChar` for the UTF-8 code page -- decoder, measuring mode and the NUL-terminated form | `kernelbase.dll!MultiByteToWideChar` | **PARKED** - geomean **1.82x**, and it loses EVERY MIXED-WIDTH class. 211 desktop modules and 89 startup modules bind it. It is 5x-9x on homogeneous input -- ascii 4096 at 7.17x, NUL-terminated ascii 8191 at 9.71x, 2-byte 64 at 5.52x, 3-byte 8191 at 5.14x, and even malformed input at 1.49x -- and 0.66x-0.94x on ASCII interleaved with 3-byte, ASCII with 4-byte, 2-byte with 3-byte, and all four widths together. **EVERY regressing class is mixed-width and every winning class is homogeneous.** That is not a new discovery: discovery/utf8_width_mixtures.c established exactly this about change 034 `RtlUTF8ToUnicodeN`, which IS this change's engine, and narrowed the cause to the scalar decoder rather than the probe ladder. The vector path handles a block only when every sequence in it is the same width; once widths interleave the block falls to a per-character scalar decode, and ntdll's scalar decoder does not pay for a failed vector probe first. So 290 inherits 034's shape including its weakness, and reusing 034's encoder is exactly what made 290 tractable -- it cannot beat its engine on the input its engine is worst at. **THE BENCH IS WHY THIS IS KNOWN**: it carries six mixed-width classes alongside the homogeneous ones, and an ASCII-only bench -- which is what 016 and 034 originally had -- would have reported 5x-7x and landed this change with a hole in it. Kept because it is bit-exact including the malformed maximal-subpart rule, because its dispatch boundary is proved so it is a correct drop-in for CP_UTF8, and because it makes the cost of the mixed-width problem concrete on a second function. **WHAT WOULD FIX IT is stated and deliberately not attempted**: a width-agnostic vectorised decoder of the `vpermb`/`vpcompressb` shuffle-table kind, computing output positions for a 64-byte block of arbitrary mixed widths without falling back to scalar. This machine has AVX512VBMI/VBMI2 and can host one; bench #1 cannot, which is part of why 034 was never written that way. That is a change to 034, not to 290, and it would lift both. **THAT DECODER NOW EXISTS, AND SO DOES THE 290 VARIANT BUILT OVER IT.** [`impl_tgl.asm`](changes/290-multibytetowidechar/impl_tgl.asm) puts a 64-byte width-agnostic `gen64` (lifted from [`changes/034`'s TGL variant](changes/034-rtlutf8tounicoden/impl_tgl.asm)) in front of `gen16`, which is kept verbatim as `gen16_small`, and **LANDS at 2.42x-2.45x geomean in 5 of 5 runs with no size class regressed** (parent on the same machine: 1.780x, PARKED). EVERY row this sentence calls regressing is now a win -- `a+3 4096` 0.79x->1.59x, `1234 4096` 0.79x->1.86x, `2+3 8191` 0.91x->1.57x, `m:a+4 8191` 0.80x->1.14x -- and malformed input, which was not the target, moved furthest: `bad 8191` 0.97x->5.55x, `bad 32000` 1.27x->10.72x. Nothing the gate proves moved: the dispatch boundary, the measuring mode, the NUL-terminated form, the per-unit overflow rule and the maximal-subpart rule are all the parent's. **THE FIRST RUN PARKED AT 2.284x** with every mixed-width class already fixed and five 8-BYTE rows at 0.65x-0.82x -- gen64's masked load is CORRECT at any length, so the draft let it take every input and correctness said nothing; it is just the heavier instrument for eight bytes. One guard (below 18 bytes, hand back to gen16_small) cleared it. Unlike change 294's dispatch floor this one was removable, because a better block was already sitting behind it. **AND IT WAS VERIFIED AGAINST THE DEFECT THAT BROKE 034, NOT ONLY AGAINST THE FIX**: probes/longbad.c checks 11580 long subjects against the LIVE kernelbase export on the return value, the last error and every output unit -- 0 differ -- and a mutant carrying 034's exact bug is caught BOTH by that probe and by this change's own correctness.c (630 failures), so 290's corpus is not blind where 034's was: it plants malformed bytes at many offsets rather than only the midpoint, which is what makes a full 64-byte block reachable. The parent stays the implementation of record on benches #1 and #2, which have no AVX-512. |
| [291](changes/291-expandenvironmentstringsw/) | `ExpandEnvironmentStringsW` -- the percent-sign scan, the substitution, and the measuring call | `kernel32.dll!ExpandEnvironmentStringsW` | **LANDED** - geomean **3.63x**, up to **16.65x** on the case that was measured as slow, LANDS in 12 of 13 runs. 131 desktop modules and 53 startup modules bind it. discovery/desktop_startup_top.c measured the shipped export at **294 ns to decide that a 254-character string contains nothing to expand** -- 0.580 ns/byte for a scan -- and that number is what this change goes after. Why it is that slow, from the disassembly: kernelbase!ExpandEnvironmentStringsW calls ntdll!wcslen as a SEPARATE PASS over the string and then hands the whole thing to ntdll!RtlExpandEnvironmentStrings, which walks it one character at a time looking for a percent sign. Finding a byte in a string is the one thing this repository does best: noexp 254 at 16.09x, noexp 4096 at 16.65x, the measuring call at 14.69x, mixed 500 at 3.50x, all-percent 512 at 3.14x. The rows where the cost is the environment LOOKUP rather than the scan sit at parity (0.91x-1.12x), which is correct -- those are a real GetEnvironmentVariableW call and there is nothing in them for assembly to remove. **THE BENCH TIMES TWO SUBJECTS ON PURPOSE**, because a string with nothing to expand measures the cost of DECIDING there is nothing to do, which is the common case and a different function from the one that substitutes; discovery/shlwapi_url_str.c once made exactly that mistake with UrlEscape and ended up timing a scan that copied its input out unchanged. 245,608 correctness cases, 0 mismatches, against the live export; ABI static PASS and dynamic clean on six call shapes. Proven live in live-substitution/live_subst_cvt.c, comparing the return value, the WHOLE destination buffer and the last-error value on all 77 cases. |
| [292](changes/292-filetimetosystemtime/) | `FileTimeToSystemTime` -- the Win32 wrapper over era-based civil-from-days date math | `kernel32.dll!FileTimeToSystemTime` | **LANDED** - geomean **3.24x** measured serially, 5 clean runs of 5; 4.02x on the 1601 epoch row and 4.04x on 1970. 95 startup modules bind it. **THIS CHANGE IS WHY discovery's 'flat' RULE NEEDED CORRECTING.** A flat row is one whose cost does not move with input size, and for a function taking a VARIABLE-length input that rules it out -- nine of the first twenty-four candidates were ruled out exactly so, including GetSystemTimeAsFileTime at 1.80 ns despite 561 modules binding it, the highest fan-in on the machine. But a FILETIME is always eight bytes, so this is flat BY CONSTRUCTION and the verdict has to come from the magnitude instead: **36-47 ns, flat AND not finished**. What made it tractable is that its engine was already here and already proved: change 126 `RtlTimeToTimeFields` (1.73x) replaced ntdll's division-heavy scalar date math with the era-based civil-from-days algorithm -- no month table, no leap-year branch, no loop, every constant divide a multiply-high whose magic number was verified exhaustively at every quotient boundary -- and it is bit-exact on all 10.67M day boundaries in the domain. The work here was the Win32 wrapper and its validation, not the arithmetic. The leap-day and February-in-a-non-leap-year cases are named bench rows rather than folded into an average, because those are the two the era-based algorithm has to get right and a mean would hide them. The rejection path for a negative FILETIME is also a named row at 1.69x. |
| [293](changes/293-systemtimetofiletime/) | `SystemTimeToFileTime` -- the inverse wrapper, field validation and days-from-civil | `kernel32.dll!SystemTimeToFileTime` | **LANDED** - geomean **2.94x** measured serially, 5 clean runs of 5, range 2.888x-3.001x. 109 startup modules bind it. Shipped cost 13-18 ns, flat by construction (a SYSTEMTIME is always sixteen bytes) and, like its inverse, flat AND not finished. typical 3.73x, leap day 3.07x, February in a non-leap year 3.32x, max year 30827 3.71x, and the two VALIDATION-FAILURE paths are named rows at 1.39x and 1.36x rather than being left out -- a wrapper that is fast only when its input is valid has optimised the wrong half. Its engine is change 127 `RtlTimeFieldsToTime` (1.54x), the inverse of 126, validated with an 800-year sweep plus 109k round-trips, whose RESULTS.md records that it rejects year 30828 by a HARD BOUND rather than by overflow -- exactly the kind of thing a Win32 wrapper may or may not reproduce, so it was probed rather than assumed. |
| [294](changes/294-strchr/) | `strchr` -- the byte sibling of `wcschr`, dual-searching for the needle and the terminator | `ucrtbase.dll!strchr` | **PARKED** - geomean **1.26x**, 1.90x at 8 KB, and a DISPATCH FLOOR between 1 and 63 bytes that no threshold removes. 75 desktop modules bind it, it runs ~26 GB/s on the absent case at 4 KB and 32 KB, and unlike its neighbours strrchr, wcsstr, wcsnlen and strncmp it is NOT in image/KEEP-AS-IS.md. The needle-ABSENT table lands cleanly at 1.285x (255 at 1.34x, 1023 at 2.20x, 8191 at 1.90x and 51.8 GB/s against ucrtbase's ~27); the failure is narrower -- with the needle FOUND AT THE LAST CHARACTER the 31- and 63-byte classes sit at 0.96x and 0.87x, because the shipped SSE2 loop returns the moment either the needle or zero hits and at that size there is nothing to amortise on either side. **THE EXPERIMENT THAT SETTLED IT**, because 'the widening threshold is in the wrong place' was the obvious reading and it is wrong. The implementation follows change 003-wcschr's Tiger Lake variant -- a VEX-128 first probe, since a 256-bit first probe obliges a `vzeroupper` on every return path. Holding that 128-bit phase for two extra blocks took the 31-byte class from a consistent 0.83x to ~1.03x and MOVED the regression to 63. That fix also had a real bug worth recording: `widen` jumped straight to the 256-bit path when the aligned base happened to already BE 32-aligned, so half of all alignments skipped the new blocks entirely and still measured 0.83x. Then the decisive test -- narrow phase at 2, 4 and 6 blocks -- gave regressed classes of {63,31,15,1}, {7,63,1,3,31} and {63,7,31,3} respectively, with the geomean never leaving 1.18x-1.28x and SOME class between 1 and 63 bytes always below parity; eight final runs at two blocks were **0 of 8 clean**. Extending the narrow phase relocates which class loses; it does not remove the loss. Parked consistently with three changes parked for the identical reason -- memcmp ('tuned small path; dispatch-floor'), strncmp (1.46x-2.06x large, 0.91x at the 32-byte class) and _wcslwr (2-6x at >= 32 B, 'ucrtbase's tight 8-wchar small path wins at size 8'). 536,035 correctness checks against the reference and BOTH hosts, including strchr(s, 0) returning the terminator and needle values above 127. |
| [295](changes/295-rtlunicodestringtointeger/) | `RtlUnicodeStringToInteger` -- counted UTF-16 string to ULONG, bases 2/8/10/16 and base-0 prefix inference | `ntdll.dll!RtlUnicodeStringToInteger` | **LANDED** - geomean **1.63x**, 5 clean runs of 5, and EVERY ROW BETTER in every run. 25 distinct live desktop/startup modules bind it. discovery/ntdll_tier3.c measured the shipped export at **0.66 ns per BYTE** on digit strings -- about thirty times worse per byte than anything else in that table -- with 11.95 ns to parse nine digits, 13.15 for ten, and 25.70 ns for a `0x` prefix in base 0. Results: 9 digits 1.80x, 10 digits 1.63x, 256 digits 1.88x, base-0 0xDEADBEEF 2.12x, base-2 32 bits 2.51x, base-16 64 digits 2.36x, and the REFUSAL paths are named rows too -- Length=0, odd Length, an invalid base, a digit outside the base, and a high character that stops the parse all measure 1.34x-1.51x. Its ANSI sibling is change 129 `RtlCharToInteger`, whose RESULTS.md records that the shipped export was itself WRONG about a leading NUL, which is why every contract point here -- the accepted prefixes and their case, the accepted explicit bases, which characters count as leading whitespace, the sign handling, what happens at the exact overflow boundary and whether *Value is written on failure -- was probed against the live export rather than taken from documentation. The 256-entry digit-table approach and the branchless mul-carry overflow guard that needs no division come from changes 111-strtoul and 112-strtoi64. |
| [296](changes/296-rtlcopyunicodestring/) | `RtlCopyUnicodeString` -- bounded UTF-16 copy with truncation, AVX2 below 1 KB and ERMS above it | `ntdll.dll!RtlCopyUnicodeString` | **PARKED** - geomean **1.33x**, and the 1024-wchar class sits at ~0.98x, clean in 3 runs of 10. 31 distinct live modules bind it; discovery/ntdll_tier3.c measured 12.50 ns for 254 characters, about 40 GB/s against 130+ for this repository's copy code. It returns VOID, so every observable effect is in the destination struct and buffer and the corpus compares both in full. **THE ERMS FIX IS MOST OF THIS CHANGE'S VALUE.** As first written it measured 0.75x at 1024w and 0.82x at 4095w -- the only two classes that regressed -- because the 64-byte AVX2 loop plateaus at ~48 GB/s while the shipped ntdll reaches ~64. That is the same finding change 130 `RtlSetBits` made here: on Tiger Lake the ERMS string instructions beat a vector loop well before the sizes Zen 3 needed, and 130's own comment records that rep is a LOSS below a few kilobytes there. Adding `rep movsb` above 1 KB for the forward non-overlapping case took 1024w to ~0.98x and 4095w to 1.06x-1.88x, 78-131 GB/s. The BYTE form is deliberate: 130 also established that `rep stosd` never gets the fast-string path at all. **AN ALIGNMENT ATTEMPT THAT FAILED 503 OF 162920 CASES, and is not a fixable oversight.** 130 aligns its `rep stosb` destination and measured a real win; the same edit here broke correctness, because A FILL HAS NO SOURCE AND A COPY DOES -- aligning means writing a 64-byte head before the rep starts, and when the destination sits just BELOW an overlapping source that store lands on source bytes the rep has not read yet (with dst = src-4 it writes [src-4, src+60) while the rep is about to read from src+rdx). Copying exactly the alignment delta WOULD be safe, but that needs a second rep, whose startup is the whole thing being avoided. Left unaligned with the reason in the source. Independently worth keeping: probes/shape.c sweeping dst & 63 with length and source fixed gives 96.1 GB/s at offsets 0 and 32 and 55.6-57.6 GB/s at every other offset -- malloc returns 16-byte alignment, so half of all destinations landed on the slow column, and that alone had the same code reading 1.38x and 0.84x on neighbouring size classes before the loop aligned its stores. 162,920 correctness checks, with src == NULL, odd MaximumLength truncation rounding DOWN to a whole WCHAR, and dst->MaximumLength never modified, all probed rather than assumed. |
| [297](changes/297-windowscomparestringordinal/) | `WindowsCompareStringOrdinal` -- ordinal (NOT linguistic) UTF-16 comparison of two HSTRINGs | `combase.dll!WindowsCompareStringOrdinal` | **LANDED** - geomean **3.13x** over 5 clean runs of 5, EVERY ROW BETTER, up to 5.98x at 4000 characters and 5.84x at 254. Identified in tier 1 at 0.177 ns/byte against 0.038 for change 041's bounded UTF-16 compare. **WHY THIS ONE IS HERE WHEN ITS NEIGHBOURS ARE NOT, established rather than assumed**: the name says ORDINAL, and discovery/strchri_is_linguistic.c and strcmpn_is_linguistic.c ruled the StrCmp/StrChrI family OUT precisely because those fold through the locale machinery and would need the OS collation tables. A NAME IS NOT EVIDENCE, so probes/wcso.c measured it -- 400,000 random pairs over an alphabet loaded with case pairs, ignorables, combining marks, sharp-s, U+0130/U+0131, lone and paired surrogates, PUA and non-characters gave ZERO differences from a plain code-unit compare, on a corpus where a LINGUISTIC CompareStringW disagrees 19.7% of the time, and nothing moved under en-US, tr-TR, lt-LT, az-Latn-AZ, el-GR or ja-JP. Ordering is by UTF-16 CODE UNIT and not code point: U+FFFF compares GREATER than U+10000. **THE SHIPPED EXPORT IS A SHIM** -- it reads the handles directly (`mov r9d,[rdx+4]` length, `mov r8,[rdx+10h]` buffer) and forwards to kernelbase!CompareStringOrdinal through the api-ms-win-core-string-l1-1-0 IAT slot, and that cross-DLL hop is ~8 ns: nothing against 83 ns at 254 characters, but most of the answer at the short sizes where this change has its largest ratio. Reading the handle fields is therefore what the shipped code itself does, not a guess at an undocumented layout, and it was cross-checked against WindowsGetStringLen and WindowsGetStringRawBuffer over 164 LIVE HANDLES of every kind combase can build -- heap, fast-pass reference, preallocated-and-promoted, and substring. Contract all measured: a NULL result pointer calls RoOriginateErrorW(E_INVALIDARG, 6, L"result"), and that call is NOT decoration -- it leaves a live IRestrictedErrorInfo on the thread which correctness.c compares FIELD BY FIELD; the same handle returns 0 as the first test in the shipped body; a NULL handle is the empty string; embedded NULs are ordinary characters and the scan runs to the declared length; and a non-NULL handle whose BUFFER is NULL gives *result = 0 against anything with GetLastError() == 87, unreachable through any documented creator and replicated anyway. 618,035 correctness checks over nine sections including an embedded NUL at every position of every length 1..40, both buffers ending exactly at a PAGE_NOACCESS boundary with no terminator at all, and 300,000 fuzz pairs over 16x16 start alignments. **NOTHING IS PUSHED**: the two lengths live in the CALLER'S SHADOW SPACE, so a four-character comparison does not pay two pushes and two pops it cannot amortise. AVX2 + BMI1 only, so this file is portable to benches #1 and #2 unchanged. |
| [298](changes/298-findresourceexw/) | `FindResourceExW` -- the resource name/type normaliser and the directory lookup | `kernelbase.dll!FindResourceExW` | **PARKED** - **1.2x-1.4x** on real string-named lookups and **1.5x-5.4x** on the normaliser in isolation, stable over 20 runs with not one regression in 380 measurements, and it STILL does not land. 112 desktop modules bind it. Two reasons, and the second is the important one. (1) The common call shape is two MAKEINTRESOURCE integers -- what LoadIcon, LoadString, LoadBitmap, LoadMenu and LoadAccelerators all pass -- which contains no string at all, so there is nothing to remove, and 85-95% of that call is ntdll!LdrFindResource_U, which cannot be replaced. (2) **THE CONTROL PROVES THE GATE IS UNSATISFIABLE ON THIS SUBJECT BY ANY IMPLEMENTATION, INCLUDING THE SHIPPED ONE.** probes/control.c runs the same harness on the same cases with THE LIVE EXPORT ON BOTH SIDES -- two invocations of one function -- and reports 180 measurements with a median of 1.00 spanning **0.71x to 1.69x**, of which **23 score WORSE** and 44 score BETTER, per-run geomean 0.984-1.062, and only **8 of 20 control runs would pass 'no size class regressed'**. Our ID table scores 12 of 160 WORSE and passes in 11 of 20 -- statistically the same as a function compared with itself. So the honest reading is not 'our ID path is 1.03x faster', it is that this benchmark cannot resolve a 3% effect on this subject at all, because most of each call is a black box whose run-to-run spread is an order of magnitude wider than the wrapper being replaced. The gdi32 row is the cleanest demonstration: the control's own MEDIAN there is 1.06x, purely because bench.h measures the first-listed implementation first. **IT ALSO FOUND A DEFECT IN THE SELECTOR THAT CHOSE IT**: discovery/momentary_tier2.c asked for GetModuleHandleW(L"user32.dll") in a console process that never loads user32 -- linking user32.lib does not load it, the linker emits an import only for a symbol something references -- so the handle was NULL, the guard was false, and the FindResourceExW row was SKIPPED WITHOUT SAYING SO, after which the timings document recorded the function as 'not yet timed cleanly'. A guard that drops a measurement silently is worse than one that crashes; the probe now calls LoadLibraryW and warns on every skip. 231,600 correctness checks -- 198,517 on the normaliser and 33,083 three-way live calls comparing return value AND last error -- and a dynamic ABI probe passing on all 19 exit paths. |
